r(x,t)=r_{0}(x)+\int_{0}^{t}u(x,\tau)\,{\mathrm{d}}\tau,\qquad r^{n}(x,t)=a^{n}+n\int_{0}^{x}v(y,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}y,

(3.20)

where
\displaystyle r_{0}(x)\vcentcolon=\big(a^{n}+n\int_{0}^{x}\rho_{0}^{-1}(r(y))\,{\mathrm{d}}y\big)^{\frac{1}{n}}
. Equations
\eqref{eqs:LFNS-k}_{1}
–
\eqref{eqs:LFNS-k}_{3}
are then obtained by translating (
1.6
) using the coordinate transformation relations (
3.19
).

By virtue of the well-known global existence theorem for problem (
3.15
)–(
3.16
), the initial data constructed in (
3.14
) implies the following theorem (see
[
23
,
43
,
42
]
):

Theorem 3.2
.

Given initial data
(\widetilde{v}_{a,k}^{0},\widetilde{u}_{a,k}^{0},\widetilde{e}_{a,k}^{0})(x)
for
x\in[0,k]
,
then there exists a unique classical solution

(v_{a,k},u_{a,k},e_{a,k})(x,t)
of problem (
3.15
)–(
3.16
) in domain
(x,t)\in[0,k]\times[0,\infty)

satisfying

v_{a,k}(x,t)\in C^{1+\alpha,1+\frac{\alpha}{2}}\big([0,k]\times[0,\infty)\big),\quad(u_{a,k},e_{a,k})(x,t)\in C^{2+\alpha,1+\frac{\alpha}{2}}\big([0,k]\times[0,\infty)\big)

for some
\alpha\in(0,1)
.

By the above theorem, there exists a unique global classical solution of (
3.15
)–(
3.16
) for each fixed
(a,k)\in(0,1)\times\mathbb{N}
. This allows us to derive the
a-priori
estimates in §4 below.

4.
The A-Priori Estimates

This section is devoted to the derivation of the
a-priori
estimates for solutions
of the approximation problem (
3.15
)–(
3.16
).
For simplicity, we suppress the approximation parameters
(a,k)\in(0,1)\times\mathbb{N}

and denote

(v,u,e,r)\equiv(\widetilde{v}_{a,k},\widetilde{u}_{a,k},\widetilde{e}_{a,k},\widetilde{r}_{a,k})
as the solution obtained from Theorem
3.2
in
[0,k]\times[0,T]
for each
T>0
.
First, we state the main theorem of this section.

Theorem 4.1
.

The following estimates are satisfied
:

(i)

The entropy estimate holds with
\psi(\zeta):=\zeta-1-\log\zeta
:

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{0}^{k}\Big(\dfrac{1}{2}\lvert u\rvert^{2}+\psi(e)+(\gamma-1)\psi(v)\Big)(x,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}x+\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{k}\dfrac{r^{2m}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\rvert^{2}}{ve^{2}}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{k}\Big\{\Big(\lambda+\dfrac{2\mu}{n}\Big)\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\rvert^{2}}{ve}+2m\mu\dfrac{v}{e}\Big(\dfrac{{\mathrm{D}}_{x}\big(r^{m}u\big)}{v\sqrt{n}}-\sqrt{n}\dfrac{u}{r}\Big)^{2}\Big\}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\leq C_{0}.

(4.1)

(ii)

For each given constant
\varepsilon\geq 0
,

\underline{v}(\varepsilon,t,a)\leq v(x,t)\leq\overline{v}(\varepsilon,t,a)\qquad\text{for all $(x,t)\in[\varepsilon,\infty)\times[0,T)$},

(4.2)

with
\overline{v}=\overline{v}(z,t,a)
and
\underline{v}=\underline{v}(z,t,a)
explicitly given by

\begin{cases}\overline{v}=C_{0}(1+t)^{2}f(z,a)\exp\big\{C_{0}tf(z,a)+C_{0}(1+t)h(z,a)f(z,a)\exp\{C_{0}tf(z,a)\}\big\},\\
\underline{v}=C_{0}(1+t)^{-1}f^{-1}(z,a)\exp\{-tC_{0}f^{2}(z,a)\}\Gamma(z,t,a),\end{cases}

(4.3)

where
h(z,a)
,
f(z,a)
, and
\Gamma(z,t,a)
are defined by

\displaystyle h(z,a)\vcentcolon=\big(a^{n}+nz\psi_{-}^{-1}(\frac{C_{0}}{z})\big)^{-\frac{2m}{n}},\quad f(z,a)\vcentcolon=\exp\big\{C_{0}h(z,a)\big\},

(4.4)

\displaystyle\Gamma(z,t,a)\vcentcolon=\exp\big\{-\dfrac{mC_{0}t}{\beta}h^{\frac{n}{2m}}(z,a)\big\},

with
\psi_{-}^{-1}(y)\vcentcolon[0,\infty)\to(0,1]
as
the left
inverse of

\psi
.
In particular, for each
\varepsilon>0
,

\begin{cases}C^{-1}(T)\leq v(x,t)\leq C(T)\quad&for all $(x,t)\in[1,k]\times[0,T)$,\\
C^{-1}(\varepsilon)\leq v(x,t)\leq C(\varepsilon)&for all $(x,t)\in[\varepsilon,k]\times[0,T]$,\\
C^{-1}(a)\leq v(x,t)\leq C(a)&for all $(x,t)\in[0,k]\times[0,T]$.\end{cases}

(4.5)

(iii)

For each
a\in(0,1),\,\mathcal{L}_{0}[v,u,e](T)\leq C(a)
and, for
(x,t)\in[0,k]\times[0,T]
,

\qquad\ \ C^{-1}(a)\leq v(x,t)\leq C(a),\,\,\,\lvert u(x,t)\rvert\leq C(a)\sigma^{-\frac{1}{4}}(t),\,\,\,C^{-1}(a)\leq e(x,t)\leq C(a)\sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t);

and, for each
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
,
\mathcal{L}_{y}[v,u,e](T)\leq C(\varepsilon)
and

\qquad C^{-1}(\varepsilon)\leq v(x,t)\leq C(\varepsilon),\,\,\,\sigma(t)^{\frac{1}{4}}\lvert u(x,t)\rvert+\sigma^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)e(x,t)\leq C(\varepsilon)\quad\mbox{for $(x,t)\in[\varepsilon,k]\times[0,T]$},

where
\sigma(t)\vcentcolon=\min\{1,t\}
and, for
y\in[0,1)
,

\mathcal{L}_{y}[v,u,e](T)
is defined by

\begin{split}\mathcal{L}_{y}[v,u,e](T)\vcentcolon=&\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{y}^{k}\left\lvert\big(v-1,u^{2},e-1,\sqrt{\sigma}r^{m}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}u,\sigma r^{m}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\big)\right\rvert^{2}(x,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}x\\
&+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{y}^{k}\left\lvert\big(r^{m}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}u,r^{m}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e,r^{m}u{\mathrm{D}}_{x}u,\sqrt{\sigma}{\mathrm{D}}_{t}u,\sigma{\mathrm{D}}_{t}e\big)\right\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t.\end{split}

4.1.
Entropy estimate

We now start the proof of Theorem
4.1
with the derivation of entropy estimate stated in
(iii)(i)
, which is motivated from the second law of thermodynamics. It encapsulates the dissipative effect of viscosity and thermal diffusion.

Proof of
(iii)(i)
in
Theorem
4.1
.

It follows from
\eqref{eqs:LFNS-k}_{1}
–
\eqref{eqs:LFNS-k}_{3}
and direct calculations that

\displaystyle\dfrac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\int_{0}^{k}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\lvert u\rvert^{2}+\psi(e)+(\gamma-1)\psi(v)\Big)\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle=-\beta\int_{0}^{k}\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\rvert^{2}}{ev}{\mathrm{d}}x+2m\mu\int_{0}^{k}\dfrac{1}{e}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m-1}u^{2})\,{\mathrm{d}}x-\kappa\int_{0}^{k}\dfrac{r^{2m}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\rvert^{2}}{ve^{2}}{\mathrm{d}}x.

(4.6)

According to
\eqref{eqs:LFNS-k}_{1}
and
\eqref{eqs:LFNS-k}_{4}
,
we can obtain

2m\mu\dfrac{1}{e}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m-1}u^{2})-\beta\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\rvert^{2}}{ev}=-\Big(\dfrac{2\mu}{n}+\lambda\Big)\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\rvert^{2}}{ve}-2m\mu\dfrac{v}{e}\Big(\dfrac{{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)}{v\sqrt{n}}-\dfrac{u\sqrt{n}}{r}\Big)^{2},

which, along with (
4.1
), yields (
(iii)(i)
).
∎

4.2.
Upper and lower bounds of the density

Now we aim to prove (
4.2
)–(
4.5
).
By (
(iii)(i)
), we first obtain some upper and lower bounds of the particle path function
r(x,t)
.

Lemma 4.1
.

Let
\psi_{-}^{-1}(\cdot)

be the left branch inverse of
\psi
. Then

a^{n}+nx\psi^{-1}_{-}(\dfrac{C_{0}}{x})\leq r^{n}(x,t)\leq C_{0}(1+x)\qquad\text{ for $(x,t)\in[0,k]\times[0,\infty)$}.

(4.7)

Proof.

Since
\psi(\zeta)\vcentcolon=\zeta-\log\zeta-1
is strictly convex in
(0,\infty)
and
\psi(1)=0
,
then there exists the left inverse function
\psi_{-}^{-1}\vcentcolon[0,\infty)\to(0,1]

and the right inverse function
\psi_{+}^{-1}\vcentcolon[0,\infty)\to[1,\infty)
.
In particular, it is direct to see that
y\mapsto\psi_{-}^{-1}(y)
is strictly decreasing
(
\psi_{-}^{-1}(0)=1
and
\psi_{-}^{-1}(y)\to 0
as
y\to\infty
),
while
y\mapsto\psi_{+}^{-1}(y)
is strictly increasing (
\psi_{+}^{-1}(0)=1
and
\psi_{+}^{-1}(y)\to\infty
as
y\to\infty
).

Now, from relation (
3.20
), Theorem
4.1
(iii)(i)
, and the Jensen inequality,
it follows that, for all
(x,t)\in[0,k]\times[0,\infty)
,

\psi(\dfrac{r^{n}(x,t)-a^{n}}{nx})=\psi(\dfrac{1}{x}\int_{0}^{x}v(y,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}y)\leq\dfrac{1}{x}\int_{0}^{x}\psi(v)(y,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}y\leq\dfrac{C_{0}}{x}.

(4.8)

Next, the proof is divided into two cases:
0<\frac{r^{n}-a^{n}}{nx}\leq 1
and
\frac{r^{n}-a^{n}}{nx}\geq 1
.

Case 1:
0<\frac{r^{n}-a^{n}}{nx}\leq 1
. Then
\frac{r^{n}-a^{n}}{nx}=\psi_{-}^{-1}(\psi(\frac{r^{n}-a^{n}}{nx}))
.
Since
\psi_{-}^{-1}(\cdot)
is monotone decreasing, it follows from (
4.8
) that

\dfrac{r^{n}-a^{n}}{nx}=\psi_{-}^{-1}(\psi(\dfrac{r^{n}-a^{n}}{nx}))\geq\psi_{-}^{-1}(\dfrac{C_{0}}{x}).

On the other hand, since
\frac{r^{n}-a^{n}}{nx}\leq 1
, it follows from
a<1
that

r^{n}(x,t)\leq a^{n}+nx\leq n(1+x).

Case 2:
\frac{r^{n}-a^{n}}{nx}\geq 1
. Then it follows from
\psi_{-}^{-1}(y)\leq 1
for all
y\in[0,\infty)
that

r^{n}(x,t)\geq a^{n}+nx\geq a^{n}+nx\psi_{-}^{-1}(\dfrac{C_{0}}{x}).

Moreover,
\frac{r^{n}-a^{n}}{nx}=\psi_{+}^{-1}(\psi(\frac{r^{n}-a^{n}}{nx}))
. Since
\psi_{+}^{-1}(\cdot)
is
monotone increasing, it follows from (
4.8
) that

\dfrac{r^{n}-a^{n}}{nx}=\psi_{+}^{-1}(\psi(\dfrac{r^{n}-a^{n}}{nx}))\leq\psi_{+}^{-1}(\dfrac{C_{0}}{x}).

It is verified in Proposition
B.1

that
x\mapsto x\psi_{+}^{-1}(\frac{C_{0}}{x})
is monotone increasing.
If
x\leq 1
, then
r^{n}(x,t)\leq a^{n}+nx\psi_{+}^{-1}(\frac{C_{0}}{x})\leq 1+n\psi_{+}^{-1}(C_{0})\leq C_{0}+C_{0}x
.
If
x>1
, since
y\mapsto\psi_{+}^{-1}(y)
is monotone increasing,
then
r^{n}(x,t)\leq a^{n}+nx\psi_{+}^{-1}(\frac{C_{0}}{x})\leq 1+nx\psi_{+}^{-1}(C_{0})\leq C_{0}+C_{0}x
.
This concludes the proof.
∎

Lemma 4.2
.

Fix
t\in[0,T]
. For each
i=1,\dotsc,k
,
there exist

A_{i}(t)
,
B_{i}(t)\in(i-1,i)
so that

\displaystyle C_{0}^{-1}\leq v(A_{i}(t),t),\,e(B_{i}(t),t)\leq C_{0}.

Proof.

We give our proof only for
A_{i}(t)
, since the proof for
B_{i}(t)
is the same.
Fix an integer
i=1,\dotsc,k
. Since
\psi(\cdot)

is convex, it follows from Jensen’s inequality and Theorem
4.1
(iii)(i)
that

\displaystyle\psi(\int_{i-1}^{i}v(x,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}x)\leq\int_{i-1}^{i}\psi(v(x,t))\,{\mathrm{d}}x\leq\dfrac{C_{0}}{\gamma-1}.

As in Lemma
4.1
, denote
z\mapsto\psi_{\pm}^{-1}(z)
to be the left and
right branch of the convex function
\psi(\zeta)
. Since
\psi(1)=0
, we have

\displaystyle 0<\psi_{-}^{-1}(\dfrac{C_{0}}{\gamma-1})\leq\int_{i-1}^{i}v(x,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}x\leq\psi_{+}^{-1}(\dfrac{C_{0}}{\gamma-1})<\infty.

By the mean value theorem, there exists a point
A_{i}(t)\in(i-1,i)
such that

\displaystyle\psi_{-}^{-1}(\dfrac{C_{0}}{\gamma-1})\leq v(A_{i}(t),t)\leq\psi_{+}^{-1}(\dfrac{C_{0}}{\gamma-1}).

Since
e(x,t)
satisfies the same estimate that differs only by constant
\gamma-1
in Theorem
4.1
(iii)(i)
,
by the same argument, there also exists
B_{i}(t)\in(i-1,i)
such that
C_{0}^{-1}\leq e(B_{i}(t),t)\leq C_{0}
.
∎

Using Lemmas
4.1
–
4.2
and Theorem
4.1
(iii)(i)
,
we are ready to prove Theorem
4.1
(iii)(ii)
.

Proof of Theorem
4.1
(iii)(ii)
.

Fix

i=1,\dotsc,k-2
. Since we focus on obtaining an explicit bound for
v(x,t)
near the origin
x=0
,
the case for
i\geq k-1
is not considered in the proof (but can be handled by repeating the same argument).
We divide the proof into six steps.

1. Applying Lemma
4.2
, it follows that, for each
(x,t)\in[i-1,i+1]\times[0,T)
,
there exist
A_{i}(t)\in(x,x+3)
and
B_{i}(t)\in(i,i+1)
such that

C_{0}^{-1}\leq v(A_{i}(t),t),\,e(B_{i}(t),t)\leq C_{0}.

(4.9)

Now, from
\eqref{eqs:LFNS-k}_{1}
–
\eqref{eqs:LFNS-k}_{2}
,

{\mathrm{D}}_{t}u+r^{m}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}p=\beta r^{m}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}{\mathrm{D}}_{t}\log v.

(4.10)

Dividing both sides of (
4.10
) by
\beta r^{m}
and integrating over
[x,A_{i}(t)]\times[0,t]
for some
(x,t)\in(i-1,i+1]\times[0,T)
, we have

\log\dfrac{v(A_{i}(t),t)v_{0}(x)}{v_{0}(A_{i}(t))v(x,t)}=\dfrac{1}{\beta}\int_{x}^{A_{i}(t)}\dfrac{u}{r^{m}}\bigg|_{0}^{t}{\mathrm{d}}y+\dfrac{m}{\beta}\int_{x}^{A_{i}(t)}\int_{0}^{t}\dfrac{u^{2}}{r^{n}}\,{\mathrm{d}}s{\mathrm{d}}y+\dfrac{1}{\beta}\int_{0}^{t}\big(p(A_{i}(t),s)-p(x,s)\big)\,{\mathrm{d}}s,

where
p(x,s)\vcentcolon=p(v(x,s),e(x,s))
. Taking the exponential on both sides yields

\dfrac{E(x,t)}{D(x,t)}Y(t)\dfrac{v_{0}(A_{i}(t))v(x,t)}{v(A_{i}(t),t)v_{0}(x)}=\exp\Big\{\dfrac{1}{\beta}\int_{0}^{t}p(x,s)\,{\mathrm{d}}s\Big\},

(4.11)

where

\begin{split}&E(x,t)\vcentcolon=\exp\Big\{\dfrac{m}{\beta}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{x}^{A_{i}(t)}\dfrac{u^{2}}{r^{n}}\,{\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}s\Big\},\hskip 9.24994ptY(t)\vcentcolon=\exp\Big\{\dfrac{1}{\beta}\int_{0}^{t}p(A_{i}(t),s)\,{\mathrm{d}}s\Big\},\\
&D(x,t)\vcentcolon=\exp\Big\{\dfrac{1}{\beta}\int_{x}^{A_{i}(t)}\Big(\dfrac{u_{0}(y)}{r_{0}^{m}(y)}-\dfrac{u(y,t)}{r^{m}(y,t)}\Big)\,{\mathrm{d}}y\Big\}.\end{split}

Multiplying
(
4.11
)
with
\beta^{-1}p(x,t)
and integrating in time, we have

1+\dfrac{1}{\beta}\int_{0}^{t}\dfrac{E(x,s)}{D(x,s)}Y(s)p(x,s)\dfrac{v_{0}(A(x,s))v(x,s)}{v(A(x,s),s)v_{0}(x)}\,{\mathrm{d}}s=\exp\Big\{\dfrac{1}{\beta}\int_{0}^{t}p(x,s)\,{\mathrm{d}}s\Big\}.

Substituting this back into (
4.11
), it follows that

\dfrac{E(x,t)}{D(x,t)}Y(t)\dfrac{v_{0}(A_{i}(t))v(x,t)}{v(A_{i}(t),t)v_{0}(x)}=1+\dfrac{1}{\beta}\int_{0}^{t}\dfrac{E(x,s)}{D(x,s)}Y(s)p(x,s)\dfrac{v_{0}(A(x,s))v(x,s)}{v(A(x,s),s)v_{0}(x)}\,{\mathrm{d}}s.

(4.12)

2. First, for
D(x,t)
, it follows from Lemma
4.1
–
4.2

and Theorem
4.1
(iii)(i)
that

\displaystyle\Big\lvert\int_{x}^{A_{i}(t)}\dfrac{u}{r^{m}}(y,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}y\Big\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\int_{x}^{A_{i}(t)}r^{-2m}\,{\mathrm{d}}y\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\int_{x}^{A_{i}(t)}\lvert u\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}y\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\int_{x}^{A_{i}(t)}\big(a^{n}+ny\psi_{-}^{-1}(\frac{C_{0}}{y})\big)^{-\frac{2m}{n}}{\mathrm{d}}y\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}C_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}+C_{0}\big(a^{n}+nx\psi_{-}^{-1}(\frac{C_{0}}{x})\big)^{-\frac{2m}{n}}.

Then, using the definition of
f(x,a)
in (
4.4
) and Lemma
4.2
, we have

(e^{C_{0}}f(x,a))^{-1}\leq D(x,t)\leq e^{C_{0}}f(x,a).

(4.13)

3. Now we estimate the terms:
Y(t)
and
E(x,t)
.
In view of the definition of
\Gamma(z,t,a)
in (
4.4
), Theorem
4.1
(iii)(i)
,
and Lemma
4.1
, we see that, for
t\geq s\geq 0
,

1\leq E(x,t)\leq\Gamma^{-1}(x,t,a),\,\,\,\,\,\Gamma(x,t-s,a)\leq\dfrac{E(x,s)}{E(x,t)}=\exp\Big\{\dfrac{m}{\beta}\int_{s}^{t}\int_{i-1}^{i}\dfrac{u^{2}}{r^{n}}\,{\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}\tau\Big\}\leq 1.

(4.14)

Combining (
4.9
) and (
4.13
)–(
4.14
) with (
4.11
) yields that, for all
(x,t)\in(i-1,i+1]\times[0,T)
,

v(x,t)Y(t)\leq C_{0}f^{2}(x,a)\Big\{1+\int_{0}^{t}e(x,s)Y(s)\,{\mathrm{d}}s\Big\}.

(4.15)

Since the above argument is true for any
(x,t)\in(i-1,i+1]\times[0,T)
, for a fixed
x\in(i-1,i]
,
we can integrate both sides of the above equation in
y\in[x,x+1]\subset(i-1,i+1]
. It then follows from the entropy inequality (
(iii)(i)
) and Jensen’s inequality that

Y(t)\leq C_{0}f^{2}(x,a)\Big\{1+\int_{0}^{t}Y(s)\,{\mathrm{d}}s\Big\}\qquad\text{for all $t\in[0,T)$,}

where we have used that
x\mapsto f(x,a)
is monotone decreasing. Thus, by Grönwall’s inequality,

1\leq Y(t)=C_{0}(1+t)f^{3}(x,a)\exp\big\{C_{0}tf^{2}(x,a)\big\}\qquad\text{for each $t\in(0,T]$.}

(4.16)

4. Since
B_{i}(t)\in(i,i+1)
from (
4.9
), then
x<i<B_{i}(t)
for all
t\in[0,T)
.
Using the Cauchy-Schwartz inequality, Lemma
4.1
, estimate (
(iii)(i)
), and Jensen’s inequality, it follows that, for each
y\in[x,i+1]\subseteq(i-1,i+1]
and
s\in[0,T)
,

\displaystyle\Big\lvert\sqrt{e(B_{i}(s),s)}-\sqrt{e(y,s)}\Big\rvert=\dfrac{1}{2}\Big\lvert\int_{y}^{B_{i}(s)}\dfrac{{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e}{\sqrt{e}}(z,s)\,{\mathrm{d}}z\Big\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{1}{2}\Big(\int_{0}^{k}\dfrac{r^{2m}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\rvert^{2}}{ve^{2}}(z,s)\,{\mathrm{d}}z\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\sup\limits_{z\in[x,i+1]}\frac{v}{r^{2m}}(z,s)\int_{i-1}^{i+1}e(z,s)\,{\mathrm{d}}z\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\Big(a^{n}+nx\psi_{-}^{-1}(\dfrac{C_{0}}{x})\Big)^{-\frac{m}{n}}\big(Q(s)V(x,s)\big)^{\frac{1}{2}},

(4.17)

where

V(x,s)\vcentcolon=\sup\limits_{z\in[x,i+1]}v(z,s),\qquad Q(s)\vcentcolon=\int_{0}^{k}\dfrac{r^{2m}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\rvert^{2}}{ve^{2}}(z,s)\,{\mathrm{d}}z,

(4.18)

and
Q(t)\in L^{1}(0,T)
due to the entropy estimate (
(iii)(i)
). According to the definition of
h(z,a)
in (
4.4
), the construction in (
4.9
) implies that, for all
y\in[x,i+1]\subseteq(i-1,i+1]
,

e(y,t)\leq C_{0}+C_{0}h(x,a)Q(s)V(x,s)\qquad\text{for all $(y,s)\in[x,i+1]\times[0,T)$}.

(4.19)

Substituting (
4.19
) and (
4.16
) into (
4.15
), it implies that, for all
y\in[x,i+1]\subseteq(i-1,i+1]
,

\displaystyle v(y,t)

\displaystyle\leq v(y,t)Y(t)

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}(1+t)f^{5}(x,a)\exp\big\{C_{0}tf^{2}(x,a)\big\}\Big\{1+t+\int_{0}^{t}h(x,a)Q(s)V(x,s){\mathrm{d}}s\Big\},

where we used that
x\mapsto f(x,a)
is decreasing.
Taking the supremum over
y\in[x,i+1]
, we have

\displaystyle V(x,t)\leq C_{0}(1+t)f^{5}(x,a)\exp\big\{C_{0}tf^{2}(x,a)\big\}\Big\{1+t+\int_{0}^{t}h(x,a)Q(s)V(x,s)\,{\mathrm{d}}s\Big\}.

(4.20)

Applying Grönwall’s inequality to (
4.20
), we obtain that, for all
(x,t)\in[i-1,i]\times[0,T)
,

\displaystyle v(x,t)\leq V(x,t)\leq C_{0}(1+t)^{2}f^{5}(x,a)\exp\Big\{C_{0}tf^{2}(x,a)+C_{0}(1+t)(f^{5}h)(x,a)\exp\{C_{0}tf^{2}(x,a)\}\Big\}.

In addition, for the lower bound of
v(x,t)
, it follows from (
4.11
) that

v(x,t)=\dfrac{D(x,t)}{Y(t)E(x,t)}\dfrac{v_{0}(x)v(A_{i}(t),t)}{v_{0}(A_{i}(t))}\exp\Big\{\int_{0}^{t}p(x,s)\,{\mathrm{d}}s\Big\}\geq C_{0}^{-1}\dfrac{D(x,t)}{Y(t)E(x,t)}.

By (
4.9
), (
4.13
)–(
4.14
), and estimates (
4.16
), we find that, for all
(x,t)\in[i-1,i]\times[0,T]
,

\displaystyle v(x,t)\geq C_{0}f^{-1}Y^{-1}E^{-1}\geq C_{0}(1+t)^{-1}f^{-4}(x,a)\exp\{-C_{0}tf^{2}(x,a)\}\Gamma(x,t,a).

Since this is true for each
i=1,\dotsc,k-2
, it follows that, for all
(x,t)\in[0,k-2]\times[0,T]
,

\begin{cases}v(x,t)\leq C_{0}(1+t)^{2}f^{5}(x,a)\exp\Big\{C_{0}tf^{2}(x,a)+C_{0}(1+t)(f^{5}h)(x,a)\exp\{C_{0}tf^{2}(x,a)\}\Big\},\\
v(x,t)\geq C_{0}f^{-4}\exp\{-C_{0}tf^{2}\}\Gamma(x,t,a).\end{cases}

5. From definition (
4.4
), it follows that, for all
x\in[1,k-2]
,

\displaystyle f(x,a)\leq\exp\big\{C_{0}(n\psi_{-}^{-1}(C_{0}))^{-\frac{2m}{n}}\big\},\,\,\,h(x,a)\leq(n\psi_{-}^{-1}(C_{0}))^{-\frac{2m}{n}},\,\,\,\Gamma(x,t,a)\geq\exp\big\{-\dfrac{mC_{0}t}{n\beta\psi_{-}^{-1}(C_{0})}\big\},

so that
C^{-1}(T)\leq v(x,t)\leq C(T)
for all
(x,t)\in[1,k-2]\times[0,T]
. Since
x\mapsto x\psi_{-}^{-1}(\frac{C_{0}}{x})
is positive and monotone increasing, then, for all
x\in[\varepsilon,k-2]
,

\displaystyle f(x,a)\leq\exp\big\{C_{0}(n\varepsilon\psi_{-}^{-1}(\frac{C_{0}}{\varepsilon}))^{-\frac{m}{n}}\big\},\,\,\,h(x,a)\leq(n\varepsilon\psi_{-}^{-1}(\frac{C_{0}}{\varepsilon}))^{-\frac{m}{n}},\,\,\,\Gamma(x,t,a)\geq\exp\big\{-\dfrac{mC_{0}t}{n\beta\varepsilon\psi_{-}^{-1}(\frac{C_{0}}{\varepsilon})}\big\},

which yield that, for each
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
,
C^{-1}(\varepsilon)\leq v(x,t)\leq C(\varepsilon)
for all
(x,t)\in[\varepsilon,k-2]\times[0,T]
.

6. Finally, we remark that, for the case when
x\in(k-2,k]
, we can choose
k\geq 3
and
A_{k}(t),B_{k}(t)\in(k-1,k)
by Lemma
4.2
, and
then repeat the same argument presented above. From this, we conclude that
C^{-1}(T)\leq v(x,t)\leq C(T)
for all
(x,t)\in(k-2,k]\times[0,T)
.
∎

4.3.
Exterior
L^{\infty}\big(0,T;L^{2}\big)
–estimates

To obtain the uniform estimates in
(a,k)\in(0,1)\times\mathbb{N}
, the derivations are restricted to domain
[\varepsilon,k]\times[0,T]
.
Thus, given fixed
\varepsilon>0
, we introduce the spatial cut-off function
g_{\varepsilon}(x)\in C^{1}
:

g_{\varepsilon}(x)\vcentcolon=\begin{cases}0&if $x\leq\dfrac{\varepsilon}{2}$,\\
\dfrac{8}{\varepsilon^{2}}\big(x-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\big)^{2}&if $\dfrac{\varepsilon}{2}\leq x\leq\dfrac{3\varepsilon}{4}$,\\
1-\dfrac{8}{\varepsilon^{2}}(x-\varepsilon)^{2}&if $\dfrac{3\varepsilon}{4}\leq x\leq\varepsilon$,\\
1&if $x\geq\varepsilon$.\end{cases}

(4.21)

It can directly be verified that
g_{\varepsilon}(x)
satisfies the following properties:

{\mathrm{supp}}(g_{\varepsilon}^{\prime})=[\frac{\varepsilon}{2},\varepsilon],\quad\,0\leq g_{\varepsilon}(x)\leq 1,\quad g_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(x)\geq 0,\quad\lvert g_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(x)\rvert^{2}\leq\dfrac{32}{\varepsilon^{2}}g_{\varepsilon}(x)\qquad\,\mbox{for all $x\in[0,\infty)$}.

(4.22)

Moreover, it is crucial that the estimates are independent of the approximation parameters
(a,k)\in(0,1)\times\mathbb{N}
. In order to achieve this,
the upper and lower bounds of
r(x,t)
must be given careful consideration. Recall that, from Lemma
4.1
,
r(x,t)
satisfies

nx\psi_{-}^{-1}(\frac{C_{0}}{x})\leq r^{n}(x,t)\leq C_{0}(1+x)\qquad\text{for $(x,t)\in[0,k]\times[0,T]$}.

It follows that, for each
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
,

\sup_{x\in[\frac{\varepsilon}{2},\varepsilon]}r(x,t)\leq C(\varepsilon),\qquad\,\,\sup_{x\in[\varepsilon,k]}r^{-1}(x,t)\leq C(\varepsilon).

(4.23)

Using
g_{\varepsilon}(x)
and Theorem
4.1
(iii)(i)
–
(iii)(ii)
,
our aim is to derive the
L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2})
–estimates listed in Theorem
4.1
(iii)(iii)
.
In order to do this, we first prove the following lemma, which is necessary for resolving the problematic boundary integrals for
x\in[\frac{\varepsilon}{2},\varepsilon]
.

Lemma 4.3

(Exterior
L^{1}(0,T;L^{\infty})
–Estimates of
e
and
u^{2}
)
.

For any fixed
\varepsilon>0
,

\int_{0}^{T}\sup_{y\in[\varepsilon,k]}\big(u^{2}+e\big)(y,s)\,{\mathrm{d}}s\leq C(\varepsilon).

(4.24)

Proof.

By Lemma
4.2
, for each
(y,s)\in[\varepsilon,k]\times[0,T]
, there exists
B_{i}(s)\in[0,k]

such that
\left\lvert y-B_{i}(s)\right\rvert\leq 1
and
C_{0}^{-1}\leq e(B_{i}(s),s)\leq C_{0}
. By the same calculation in (
4.2
), we have

\displaystyle\lvert\sqrt{e(y,s)}-\sqrt{e(B_{i}(s),s)}\rvert^{2}\leq

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{4}\sup_{z\in[\varepsilon,k]}\dfrac{v}{r^{2m}}(z,s)\Big\lvert\int_{B_{i}(s)}^{y}e(x,s)\,{\mathrm{d}}x\Big\rvert\Big\lvert\int_{B_{i}(s)}^{y}\dfrac{r^{2m}\,\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\rvert^{2}}{ve^{2}}(x,s)\,{\mathrm{d}}x\Big\rvert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,C(\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{k}\dfrac{r^{2m}\,\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\rvert^{2}}{ve^{2}}(x,s)\,{\mathrm{d}}x,

(4.25)

where
we have used Theorem
4.1
(iii)(ii)
, (
4.21
)–(
4.23
),
and
\lVert\psi(e)\rVert_{L^{1}(0,k)}\leq C_{0}
from Theorem
4.1
(iii)(i)
and Jensen’s inequality.
Taking the supremum over
y\in[\varepsilon,k]
on the left-hand side of (
4.25
) and integrating in
t\in[0,T]
,
it follows from
Lemma
4.2
and Theorem
4.1
(iii)(i)
that

\int_{0}^{T}\sup_{y\in[\varepsilon,k]}e(y,s)\,{\mathrm{d}}s\leq C_{0}T+C(\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{k}\dfrac{r^{2m}\,\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\rvert^{2}}{ve^{2}}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s\leq C(\varepsilon).

(4.26)

Next, notice that

\displaystyle\lvert u{\mathrm{D}}_{x}u\rvert=\lvert r^{-m}u{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)-mr^{-n}vu^{2}\rvert\leq 2r^{-2m}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\rvert^{2}+2m^{2}r^{-2n}v^{2}\lvert u\rvert^{2}.

Then, by the Sobolev embedding theorem:
W^{1,1}(\varepsilon,k)\xhookrightarrow{}C^{0}(\varepsilon,k)
, and Theorem
4.1
(iii)(i)
–
(iii)(ii)
,

\displaystyle\sup_{y\in[\varepsilon,k]}u^{2}(y,t)

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\varepsilon}^{k}u^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x+C\int_{\varepsilon}^{k}\lvert u{\mathrm{D}}_{x}u\rvert\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}+C\sup_{y\in[\varepsilon,k]}\Big(\dfrac{v}{r^{2m}}e+\dfrac{v}{r^{n}}\Big)(y,s)\int_{\varepsilon}^{k}u^{2}(x,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}x+C\int_{\varepsilon}^{k}\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\rvert^{2}}{ve}\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\leq C(\varepsilon)+C(\varepsilon)\sup_{y\in[\varepsilon,k]}e(y,t)+C\int_{0}^{k}\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\rvert^{2}}{ve}\,{\mathrm{d}}x.

(4.27)

\eqref{temp:euLinf}_{2}
follows from integrating the above in
[0,T]
and then using (
4.26
) and Theorem
4.1
(iii)(i)
.
∎

Lemma 4.4

(Exterior
L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2})
–Estimate of the Total Energy)
.

For any fixed
\varepsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{\varepsilon}^{k}\big(\lvert e-1\rvert^{2}+u^{4}\big)(x,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}x+\int_{0}^{T}\sup\limits_{x\in[\varepsilon,k]}e^{2}(x,s)\,{\mathrm{d}}s+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\varepsilon}^{k}\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}\big(\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\rvert^{2}+\lvert u{\mathrm{D}}_{x}u\rvert^{2}\big)\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s\leq C(\varepsilon).

(4.28)

Proof.

We divide the proof into four steps.

1. Denote the total energy density function
w\vcentcolon=\frac{1}{2}u^{2}+e-1
.
Then multiplying
\eqref{eqs:LFNS-k}_{2}
with
u
and adding it to
\eqref{eqs:LFNS-k}_{3}
yield

{\mathrm{D}}_{t}w={\mathrm{D}}_{x}\big(r^{m}u\widetilde{F}\big)-2m\mu{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m-1}u^{2})+\kappa{\mathrm{D}}_{x}\big(\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\big),

(4.29)

where
\widetilde{F}\vcentcolon=\beta v^{-1}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)-p(v,e)
.
Multiplying (
4.29
) with
g_{\varepsilon}w
and then integrating in
x
, we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{2}\dfrac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}w^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}r^{m}u\widetilde{F}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}w\,{\mathrm{d}}x+2m\mu\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}r^{m-1}u^{2}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}w\,{\mathrm{d}}x-\kappa\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e{\mathrm{D}}_{x}w\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\quad\,+\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\Big\{-r^{m}uw\widetilde{F}+2m\mu r^{m-1}u^{2}w-\kappa\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}w{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\Big\}\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle=\vcentcolon\sum_{i=1}^{4}I_{i}.

2. The terms
I_{j},j=1,2,3
, can be estimated as follows:

\displaystyle I_{1}=

\displaystyle-\beta\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\big\{\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}u{\mathrm{D}}_{x}u{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e+mr^{m-1}u^{3}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}u+mr^{m-1}u^{2}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\big\}\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle+(\gamma-1)\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{e}{v}r^{m}\big\{u^{2}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}u+u{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\big\}\,{\mathrm{d}}x-\beta\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}\lvert u{\mathrm{D}}_{x}u\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,\big(\dfrac{5\beta^{2}}{2\kappa}-\dfrac{2\beta}{3}\big)\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{\lvert r^{m}u{\mathrm{D}}_{x}u\rvert^{2}}{v}\,{\mathrm{d}}x+\dfrac{3\kappa}{10}\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{\lvert r^{m}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\rvert^{2}}{v}\,{\mathrm{d}}x+C\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\Big(\dfrac{vu^{4}}{r^{2}}+\dfrac{e^{2}u^{2}}{v}\Big)\,{\mathrm{d}}x,

\displaystyle I_{2}=

\displaystyle\,2m\mu\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}r^{m-1}(u^{3}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}u+u^{2}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e)\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,\dfrac{\beta}{6}\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}\lvert u{\mathrm{D}}_{x}u\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x+\dfrac{\kappa}{10}\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x+C\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{v}{r^{2}}u^{4}\,{\mathrm{d}}x,

\displaystyle I_{3}=

\displaystyle-\kappa\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\{{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e+u{\mathrm{D}}_{x}u\}\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-\dfrac{9}{10}\kappa\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x+\dfrac{5\kappa}{2}\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}\lvert u{\mathrm{D}}_{x}u\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x.

Using (
4.21
)–(
4.23
) and Theorem
4.1
(iii)(ii)
,
we have

\displaystyle I_{4}=

\displaystyle\int_{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}^{\varepsilon}g_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\Big\{-r^{m}uw\Big(\beta\dfrac{r^{m}}{v}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}u+\dfrac{m\beta}{r}u-(\gamma-1)\dfrac{e}{v}\Big)+2m\mu r^{m-1}u^{2}w-\kappa\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}w{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\Big\}\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}^{\varepsilon}\dfrac{\varepsilon^{2}\lvert g_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\rvert^{2}}{32}\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}\Big\{\dfrac{\beta}{2}\lvert u{\mathrm{D}}_{x}u\rvert^{2}+\dfrac{\kappa}{4}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\rvert^{2}\Big\}\,{\mathrm{d}}x+C\int_{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}^{\varepsilon}\Big\{\dfrac{\varepsilon^{2}\lvert g_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\rvert^{2}}{32}\big(\dfrac{vu^{4}}{r^{2}}+\dfrac{e^{2}u^{2}}{v}\big)+\dfrac{32}{\varepsilon^{2}}\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}w^{2}\Big\}\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}\Big\{\dfrac{\beta}{2}\left\lvert u{\mathrm{D}}_{x}u\right\rvert^{2}+\dfrac{\kappa}{4}\left\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\right\rvert^{2}\Big\}\,{\mathrm{d}}x+C\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\Big\{\dfrac{v}{r^{2}}u^{4}+\dfrac{e^{2}u^{2}}{v}\Big\}\,{\mathrm{d}}x+C(\varepsilon)\int_{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}^{\varepsilon}w^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x.

Combining all the above estimates, it follows that

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{2}\dfrac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}w^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x\leq

\displaystyle-\dfrac{\kappa}{4}\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}\left\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\right\rvert^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x+\dfrac{5(\beta^{2}+\kappa^{2})}{2\kappa}\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}\left\lvert u{\mathrm{D}}_{x}u\right\rvert^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x

(4.30)

\displaystyle+C\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{v}{r^{2}}u^{4}\,{\mathrm{d}}x+C\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{e^{2}u^{2}}{v}\,{\mathrm{d}}x+C(\varepsilon)\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon}(t),

where
\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon}(t)
is the problematic boundary integral near the origin, given by

\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon}(t)\vcentcolon=\int_{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}^{\varepsilon}\big((e-1)^{2}+u^{4}\big)(x,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}x.

(4.31)

3. Multiplying
g_{\varepsilon}u^{3}
to the momentum equation
\eqref{eqs:LFNS-k}_{2}
and integrating in
x\in[0,k]
, we obtain the following
L^{4}
-estimate for
u
:

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{4}\dfrac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\left\lvert u\right\rvert^{4}{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle=-\beta\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{1}{v}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u){\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u^{3})\,{\mathrm{d}}x+(\gamma-1)\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{e}{v}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u^{3})\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\Big\{\dfrac{(\gamma-1)e}{v}r^{m}u^{3}-\dfrac{\beta}{v}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)r^{m}u^{3}\Big\}\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle=\vcentcolon\,\sum_{i=1}^{3}J_{i}.

The terms
J_{1}
and
J_{2}
can be estimated as follows:

\displaystyle J_{1}=

\displaystyle-\beta\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\Big(3\dfrac{\lvert r^{m}u{\mathrm{D}}_{x}u\rvert^{2}}{v}+4mr^{m-1}u^{3}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}u+m^{2}\dfrac{u^{4}v}{r^{2}}\Big)\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,-\beta\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}\lvert u{\mathrm{D}}_{x}u\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x+m^{2}\beta\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{v}{r^{2}}u^{4}\,{\mathrm{d}}x,

\displaystyle J_{2}\leq

\displaystyle\,\dfrac{\beta}{4}\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{\lvert r^{m}u{\mathrm{D}}_{x}u\rvert^{2}}{v}\,{\mathrm{d}}x+C\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\Big(\dfrac{e^{2}u^{2}}{v}+\dfrac{vu^{4}}{r^{2}}\Big)\,{\mathrm{d}}x.

Using (
4.21
)–(
4.23
)
and Theorem
4.1
(iii)(ii)
, we have

\displaystyle J_{3}\leq

\displaystyle\,\dfrac{\gamma-1}{2}\int_{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}^{\varepsilon}\dfrac{\varepsilon^{2}}{32}\lvert g_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\rvert^{2}\dfrac{e^{2}u^{2}}{v}\,{\mathrm{d}}x+\dfrac{\gamma-1}{2}\int_{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}^{\varepsilon}\dfrac{32}{\varepsilon^{2}}\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}u^{4}\,{\mathrm{d}}x+C\int_{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}^{\varepsilon}\dfrac{1}{\varepsilon}r^{m-1}u^{4}\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\,+\dfrac{\beta}{4}\int_{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}^{\varepsilon}\dfrac{\varepsilon^{2}}{32}\lvert g_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\rvert^{2}\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}\lvert u{\mathrm{D}}_{x}u\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x+\beta\int_{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}^{\varepsilon}\dfrac{32}{\varepsilon^{2}}\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}u^{4}\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,\dfrac{\beta}{4}\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}\lvert u{\mathrm{D}}_{x}u\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x+\dfrac{\gamma-1}{2}\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{e^{2}u^{2}}{v}\,{\mathrm{d}}x+C(\varepsilon)\int_{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}^{\varepsilon}u^{4}\,{\mathrm{d}}x.

Combining all the above estimates, it follows that

\displaystyle\dfrac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{\lvert u\rvert^{4}}{4}{\mathrm{d}}x\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\Big(\dfrac{e^{2}u^{2}}{v}+\dfrac{vu^{4}}{r^{2}}\Big)\,{\mathrm{d}}x-\dfrac{\beta}{2}\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{\lvert r^{m}u{\mathrm{D}}_{x}u\rvert^{2}}{v}\,{\mathrm{d}}x+C(\varepsilon)\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon}(t).

(4.32)

4. Multiplying (
4.32
) by
4\widetilde{C}\equiv\frac{10(\beta^{2}+\kappa^{2})}{\kappa\beta}
and adding it to (
4.30
),
it follows from (
(iii)(i)
) that

\displaystyle\widetilde{C}\dfrac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}u^{4}\,{\mathrm{d}}x+\dfrac{1}{2}\dfrac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}w^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x+\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}\Big\{\dfrac{\kappa}{4}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\rvert^{2}+\beta\widetilde{C}\lvert u{\mathrm{D}}_{x}u\rvert^{2}\Big\}\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\leq C(\varepsilon)\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon}(t)+C\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\Big(\dfrac{v}{r^{2}}u^{4}+\dfrac{\left\lvert ue\right\rvert^{2}}{v}\Big)\,{\mathrm{d}}x.

(4.33)

Using Theorem
4.1
(iii)(i)
–
(iii)(ii)
and (
4.21
)–(
4.23
),
we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\Big(\dfrac{v}{r^{2}}u^{4}+\dfrac{\left\lvert ue\right\rvert^{2}}{v}\Big)\,{\mathrm{d}}x\leq C(\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}u^{4}\,{\mathrm{d}}x+C(\varepsilon)\sup_{y\in[\frac{\varepsilon}{2},k]}(g_{\varepsilon}\lvert e\rvert^{2})(y,t).

(4.34)

By Lemma
4.2
, for each
(x,t)\in[0,k]\times[0,T]
,
there exist both an integer
i\in\{0,\dotsc,k-1\}
and a point
B_{i}(t)\in[0,k]

such that
x,B_{i}(t)\in[i,i+1]
and
C_{0}^{-1}\leq e(B_{i}(t),t)\leq C_{0}
.
Using Theorem
4.1
(iii)(i)
,
\lvert x-B_{i}(t)\rvert\leq 1
, and (
4.21
)–(
4.23
),
we have

\displaystyle\lvert(g_{\varepsilon}e)(x,t)-(g_{\varepsilon}e)(B_{i}(t),t)\rvert^{2}=\Big(\int_{B_{i}(t)}^{x}(g_{\varepsilon}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e+g_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}e)(y,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}y\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\Big(\int_{B_{i}(t)}^{x}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\rvert^{2}}{e^{2}}\,{\mathrm{d}}y\Big)\Big(\int_{B_{i}(t)}^{x}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{v}{r^{2m}}e^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}y\Big)+C(\varepsilon)\Big(\int_{B_{i}(t)}^{x}e(y,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}y\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 4\Big(\int_{0}^{k}\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}\dfrac{\left\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\right\rvert^{2}}{e^{2}}\,{\mathrm{d}}y\Big)\Big\{\Big\lvert\int_{B_{i}(t)}^{x}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{v}{r^{2m}}\lvert e-1\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}y\Big\rvert+\Big\lvert\int_{B_{i}(t)}^{x}\dfrac{g_{\varepsilon}v}{r^{2m}}\,{\mathrm{d}}y\Big\rvert\Big\}+C(\varepsilon)\Big(\int_{i}^{i+1}e\,{\mathrm{d}}y\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle=C(\varepsilon)\Big\{1+Q(t)\Big(1+\Big\lvert\int_{B_{i}(t)}^{x}g_{\varepsilon}\lvert e-1\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}y\Big\rvert\Big)\Big\},

where
Q(t)
is defined in (
4.18
).
Taking the supremum over
x\in[\frac{\varepsilon}{2},k]
, it follows from Theorem
4.1
(iii)(ii)
and (
4.21
)–(
4.23
)
that

\sup_{x\in[\frac{\varepsilon}{2},k]}(g_{\varepsilon}\lvert e\rvert^{2})(x,t)\leq C(\varepsilon)\Big\{1+Q(t)\Big(1+\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\lvert e-1\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x\Big)\Big\}.

(4.35)

Substituting (
4.35
) into (
4.34
) yields

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\Big(\dfrac{v}{r^{2}}u^{4}+\dfrac{\lvert ue\rvert^{2}}{v}\Big)\,{\mathrm{d}}x\leq C(\varepsilon)\big(1+Q(t)\big)\Big(1+\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}(u^{4}+w^{2})\,{\mathrm{d}}x\Big),

(4.36)

where we have used:

\lvert u\rvert^{4}+w^{2}\geq\dfrac{3}{4}\lvert u\rvert^{4}+\dfrac{1}{2}(e-1)^{2}
.
Substituting (
4.36
) into (
4.3
), it follows that

\displaystyle\dfrac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}(u^{4}+w^{2})\,{\mathrm{d}}x+\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}\big(\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\rvert^{2}\lvert u{\mathrm{D}}_{x}u\rvert^{2}\big)\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\leq C(\varepsilon)\big(1+Q(t)\big)\Big(1+\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}(u^{4}+w^{2})\,{\mathrm{d}}x\Big)+C(\varepsilon)\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon}(t).

Therefore, applying Grönwall’s inequality and using
Q(t)\in L^{1}(0,T)
,
we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{k}\big(\lvert u\rvert^{4}+\lvert e-1\rvert^{2}\big)(x,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}x\leq C(\varepsilon)\Big(1+\int_{0}^{k}(u_{0}^{4}+w_{0}^{2})\,{\mathrm{d}}x+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon}\,{\mathrm{d}}s\Big).

(4.37)

5. Using Lemma
4.3
, we claim that
\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon}(t)
defined in (
4.31
) satisfies:

\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon}(s)\,{\mathrm{d}}s=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}^{\varepsilon}\big(\lvert e-1\rvert^{2}+\lvert u\rvert^{4}\big)\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s\leq C(\varepsilon).

(4.38)

To show this claim, we first use Lemma
4.3
and Theorem
4.1
(iii)(i)
to obtain

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon}(s)\,{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{t}\sup_{y\in[\frac{\varepsilon}{2},\varepsilon]}\lvert e-1\rvert(y,s)\big(\int_{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}^{\varepsilon}(e+1)(x,s)\,{\mathrm{d}}x\big)\,{\mathrm{d}}s+C\int_{0}^{t}\sup_{y\in[\frac{\varepsilon}{2},\varepsilon]}u^{2}(y,s)\big(\int_{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}^{\varepsilon}u^{2}(x,s)\,{\mathrm{d}}x\big)\,{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\leq C(\varepsilon)\Big(1+\int_{0}^{t}\sup_{y\in[\frac{\varepsilon}{2},\varepsilon]}e(y,s)\big(\int_{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}^{\varepsilon}e(x,s)\,{\mathrm{d}}x\big)\,{\mathrm{d}}s\Big).

It follows by Jensen’s inequality and Theorem
4.1
(iii)(i)
that

\displaystyle\psi(\dfrac{2}{\varepsilon}\int_{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}^{\varepsilon}e(x,s)\,{\mathrm{d}}x)\leq\dfrac{2}{\varepsilon}\int_{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}^{\varepsilon}\psi(e)(x,s)\,{\mathrm{d}}x\leq\dfrac{2C_{0}}{\varepsilon},

so that

∫_

ε

2

ε
e(x,s)  dx ≤

2

ψ
^-1_+ (

2C

ε

) ≤C(
ε
).

Substituting this back into the estimate of
\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon}

and using Lemma
4.3
, it follows that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon}(s)\,{\mathrm{d}}s\leq C(\varepsilon)\Big(1+\int_{0}^{t}\sup_{y\in[\frac{\varepsilon}{2},\varepsilon]}e(y,s)\,{\mathrm{d}}s\Big)\leq C(\varepsilon).

This prove the claim (
4.38
).

6. Using Grönwall’s inequality and (
4.37
)–(
4.38
),
we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}(x)(u^{4}+w^{2})(x,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}x+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\rvert^{2}\lvert u{\mathrm{D}}_{x}u\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s\leq\int_{0}^{k}(u_{0}^{4}+w_{0}^{2})\,{\mathrm{d}}x+C(\varepsilon).

By the inequality:

\lvert u\rvert^{4}+w^{2}\geq\frac{3}{4}\lvert u\rvert^{4}+\frac{1}{2}(e-1)^{2}
,
we see that
\lVert(e-1,u^{2})\rVert_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\varepsilon,k))}\leq C(\varepsilon)
.

Finally, substituting the above estimate into (
4.35
), we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\sup\limits_{x\in[\varepsilon,k]}e^{2}(x,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\sup_{x\in[\frac{\varepsilon}{2},k]}(g_{\varepsilon}e^{2})(x,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}t\leq C(\varepsilon).

This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 4.5

(Exterior
L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2})
–Estimate of the Specific Volume)
.

For each
\varepsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{\varepsilon}^{k}\lvert v-1\rvert^{2}(x,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}x\leq C(\varepsilon).

Proof.

Multiplying
\eqref{eqs:LFNS-k}_{1}
with
g_{\varepsilon}(v-1)
and integrating in
[0,k]
, then, by Theorem
4.1
(iii)(ii)
,

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{2}{\mathrm{D}}_{t}\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}(v-1)^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x=\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}(v-1){\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\,{\mathrm{d}}x\leq C(\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}(v-1)^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x+\dfrac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\rvert^{2}}{v}\,{\mathrm{d}}x.

Applying Lemma
4.4
and Grönwall’s inequality, the lemma is proved.
∎

The following lemma is necessary for the high-order estimates of
(u,e)

in §
4.4

below.

Lemma 4.6

(Exterior
L^{2}
–estimate of
{\mathrm{D}}_{x}u
)
.

For each
\varepsilon>0
,

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\varepsilon}^{k}\Big(\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\rvert^{2}}{v}+\dfrac{r^{2m}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}u\rvert^{2}}{v}\Big)\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s\leq C(\varepsilon).

(4.39)

Proof.

Multiplying
\eqref{eqs:LFNS-k}_{1}
by
g_{\varepsilon}
and integrating over
[0,k]
,
we have

\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}(x){\mathrm{D}}_{t}(v(x,t)-1)\,{\mathrm{d}}x=-\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(x)(r^{m}u)(x,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}x.

(4.40)

Multiplying
\eqref{eqs:LFNS-k}_{2}
by
g_{\varepsilon}u
and integrating over
x\in[0,k]
,
using Theorem
4.1
(iii)(i)
–
(iii)(ii)
, Lemma
4.4
,
and (
4.40
), it follows that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\dfrac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\lvert u\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x+\beta\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\rvert^{2}}{v}{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle=(\gamma-1)\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)}{v}(e-1)\,{\mathrm{d}}x+(\gamma-1)\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{{\mathrm{D}}_{t}v}{v}{\mathrm{d}}x+\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(x)\dfrac{r^{m}u}{v}\big((\gamma-1)e-\beta{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\big)\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle=\vcentcolon\sum_{i=1}^{3}I_{i}.

(4.41)

The term
I_{1}
is estimated by using Theorem
4.1
(iii)(ii)

and Lemma
4.4
:

\begin{split}&I_{1}\vcentcolon=(\gamma-1)\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)}{v}(e-1)\,{\mathrm{d}}x\leq\dfrac{\beta}{4}\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\rvert^{2}}{v}{\mathrm{d}}x+C(\varepsilon).\end{split}

(4.42)

I_{2}
is estimated by using
\eqref{eqs:LFNS-k}_{1}
, Theorem
4.1
(iii)(i)
,
and (
4.21
)–(
4.23
):

\displaystyle\dfrac{I_{2}}{\gamma-1}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{{\mathrm{D}}_{t}v}{v}{\mathrm{d}}x=\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}{\mathrm{D}}_{t}(\log v-v+1)\,{\mathrm{d}}x-\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}r^{m}u\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}{\mathrm{D}}_{t}(\log v-v+1)\,{\mathrm{d}}x+\Big(\int_{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}^{\varepsilon}r^{2m}{\mathrm{d}}x\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\int_{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}^{\varepsilon}u^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}{\mathrm{D}}_{t}(\log v-v+1)\,{\mathrm{d}}x+C(\varepsilon).

(4.43)

I_{3}
is estimated by using Lemma
4.4
and (
4.21
)–(
4.23
):

\displaystyle I_{3}\leq

\displaystyle\,\dfrac{(\gamma-1)^{2}}{2}\sup\limits_{y\in[\frac{\varepsilon}{2},\varepsilon]}(v^{-2}r^{2m})(y,t)\int_{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}^{\varepsilon}u^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x+(\gamma-1)\Big(\int_{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}^{\varepsilon}v^{-2}r^{2m}{\mathrm{d}}x\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\int_{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}^{\varepsilon}u^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle+\dfrac{1}{2}\int_{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}^{\varepsilon}e^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x+\dfrac{\beta}{4}\int_{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}^{\varepsilon}\dfrac{\varepsilon^{2}}{32}\lvert g_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(x)\rvert^{2}\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\rvert^{2}}{v}{\mathrm{d}}x+\dfrac{32\beta}{\varepsilon^{2}}\sup\limits_{y\in[\frac{\varepsilon}{2},\varepsilon]}\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}(y,t)\int_{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}^{\varepsilon}u^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,\dfrac{\beta}{4}\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\rvert^{2}}{v}{\mathrm{d}}x+C(\varepsilon).

(4.44)

According to (
4.3
)–(
4.3
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}(x)u^{2}(x,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}x+\dfrac{\beta}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\rvert^{2}}{v}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\leq C(\varepsilon)+(\gamma-1)\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}(\log v-v+1)\,{\mathrm{d}}x+(\gamma-1)\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}(v_{0}-\log v_{0}-1)\,{\mathrm{d}}x.

Since
\log v-v+1\leq 0
and
C_{0}\leq v_{0}(x)\leq C_{0}^{-1}
,
it implies from Taylor’s theorem that

\dfrac{\beta}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\rvert^{2}}{v}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s\leq C(\varepsilon)+(\gamma-1)C_{0}\int_{0}^{k}(v_{0}-1)^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x\leq C(\varepsilon).

(4.45)

The other term in (
4.39
) is estimated by rewriting (
4.45
)
and using Theorem
4.1
(iii)(i)
–
(iii)(ii)
.
∎

4.4.
Exterior
L^{\infty}\big(0,T;H^{1}\big)
–estimates of the velocity and internal energy

We now derive the high-regularity estimates for
(u,e)
in Theorem
4.1
(iii)(iii)
. For simplicity, we define

\Lambda(v,e)\equiv(v-1)^{2}+(e-1)^{2},\quad\,\sigma(t)\vcentcolon=\min\{1,t\}.

It follows from Lemma
4.4
-
4.5
that
\left\lVert\Lambda(v,e)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\varepsilon,k))}\leq C(\varepsilon)
. Furthermore, we introduce the effective viscosity flux
F
, which is defined by

\displaystyle F\vcentcolon=\beta\dfrac{{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)}{v}-p(v,e)+(\gamma-1)=\beta\dfrac{{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)}{v}-(\gamma-1)\dfrac{e-v}{v}.

Using
Lemmas
4.4
–
4.6
,
we have the following observation on
F
:

Lemma 4.7

(Estimates on Effective Viscosity Flux)
.

\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}(x)F^{2}(x,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}x\leq C(\varepsilon)\Big(1+\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\rvert^{2}}{v}{\mathrm{d}}x\Big),\\
&\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}(x)F^{2}(x,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\leq C(\varepsilon),\\
&\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}(x)(r^{2m}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}F\rvert^{2})(x,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\leq\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}(x)\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{t}u\rvert^{2}(x,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t.\end{split}

(4.46)

Proof.

It follows from the definition of
F
and the momentum equation
\eqref{eqs:LFNS-k}_{2}
that

\displaystyle F=\beta\dfrac{{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)}{v}-(\gamma-1)\dfrac{e-1}{v}+(\gamma-1)\dfrac{v-1}{v},

\displaystyle{\mathrm{D}}_{t}u=\beta r^{m}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}\Big(\dfrac{{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)}{v}\Big)-r^{m}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(p-(\gamma-1))=r^{m}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}F,

which, along with Theorem
4.1
(iii)(ii)
and Lemmas
4.4
–
4.6
, yields that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}(x)F^{2}(x,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}x\leq

\displaystyle\,2\beta^{2}\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\rvert^{2}}{v^{2}}{\mathrm{d}}x+4(\gamma-1)^{2}\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{\Lambda(v,e)}{v^{2}}{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,C(\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\rvert^{2}}{v}{\mathrm{d}}x+C(\varepsilon),

and
\eqref{eqs:SL2F}_{2}
–
\eqref{eqs:SL2F}_{3}
obviously.
∎

Lemma 4.8

(Intermediate Step)
.

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{k}\sigma g_{\varepsilon}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{t}u\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s+\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\int_{0}^{k}\Big(\sigma g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\rvert^{2}}{v}\Big)(x,s)\,{\mathrm{d}}x\leq C(\varepsilon)\Big\{1+\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{k}\sigma^{2}g_{\varepsilon}^{2}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{t}e\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big\}.

Proof.

\displaystyle=\dfrac{\beta}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\sigma^{\prime}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\rvert^{2}}{v}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s+m\beta\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\sigma g_{\varepsilon}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m-1}u^{2})\dfrac{{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)}{v}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\sigma g_{\varepsilon}r^{m}{\mathrm{D}}_{t}u{\mathrm{D}}_{x}p\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s+\dfrac{\beta}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\sigma g_{\varepsilon}{\mathrm{D}}_{t}(v^{-1})\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\quad-\beta\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\sigma g_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}r^{m}{\mathrm{D}}_{t}u\dfrac{{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)}{v}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle=\vcentcolon\sum_{i=1}^{5}I_{i}.

I_{1}

\displaystyle I_{1}\vcentcolon=\dfrac{\beta}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\sigma^{\prime}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\rvert^{2}}{v}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s\leq\dfrac{\beta}{2}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\rvert^{2}}{v}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s\leq C(\varepsilon).

\displaystyle\leq m\beta\int_{0}^{t}\sup_{y\in[\frac{\varepsilon}{2},k]}r^{-2}(y,s)\big(\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}\lvert u{\mathrm{D}}_{x}u\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x\big)\,{\mathrm{d}}s+m\beta\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\rvert^{2}}{v}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s

2. Using

Combining the estimates of

\displaystyle I_{3}\leq

\displaystyle\,\dfrac{\beta}{4}\int_{0}^{k}\Big(\sigma g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\rvert^{2}}{v}\Big)(x,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}x+\dfrac{1}{8}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\sigma g_{\varepsilon}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{t}u\rvert^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s+C(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle+C(\varepsilon)\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\sigma g_{\varepsilon}^{2}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{t}e\rvert^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}+C(\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{t}\sup_{y\in[\frac{\varepsilon}{2},k]}e(y,s)\big(\int_{0}^{k}\Big(\sigma g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\rvert^{2}}{v}\Big)(x,s)\,{\mathrm{d}}x\big){\mathrm{d}}s.

4.
I_{4}
is rewritten into three parts by using the continuity equation

\displaystyle I_{4}=

\displaystyle-\dfrac{1}{2\beta}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\sigma g_{\varepsilon}F^{2}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s-\dfrac{\gamma-1}{\beta}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\sigma g_{\varepsilon}F\dfrac{e-v}{v}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle-\dfrac{(\gamma-1)^{2}}{2\beta}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\sigma g_{\varepsilon}\Big(\dfrac{e-v}{v}\Big)^{2}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=

\displaystyle\,\sum_{i=1}^{3}I_{4}^{(i)}.

I_{4}^{(1)}
is estimated by using integration by parts, Lemma
4.7
,
and (
4.21
)–(
4.23
).

\displaystyle\beta I_{4}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\sigma g_{\varepsilon}uFr^{m}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}F\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s+\dfrac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\sigma g_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}F^{2}r^{m}u\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\sigma g_{\varepsilon}u^{2}F^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s+\dfrac{1}{8}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\sigma g_{\varepsilon}r^{2m}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}F\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\quad+\dfrac{1}{4}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}^{\varepsilon}\sigma\dfrac{\varepsilon^{2}}{32}\lvert g_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\rvert^{2}\lvert u\rvert^{2}F^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s+\dfrac{1}{4}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}^{\varepsilon}\dfrac{32}{\varepsilon^{2}}r^{2m}F^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{1}{8}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\sigma g_{\varepsilon}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{t}u\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s+C(\varepsilon)+C(\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{t}\sup_{y\in[\frac{\varepsilon}{2},k]}\lvert u\rvert^{2}(y,s)\big(\int_{0}^{k}\sigma g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\rvert^{2}}{v}{\mathrm{d}}x\big){\mathrm{d}}s.

I_{4}^{(2)}
is estimated by using Theorem
4.1
(iii)(ii)

and
Lemmas
4.4
–
4.7
:

\displaystyle\dfrac{\beta}{\gamma-1}I_{4}^{(2)}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\sigma g_{\varepsilon}F^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s+\int_{0}^{t}\sup_{y\in[\frac{\varepsilon}{2},k]}\dfrac{(e-v)^{2}}{v}\big(\int_{0}^{k}\sigma g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\rvert^{2}}{v}{\mathrm{d}}x\big)\,{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\leq C(\varepsilon)+C(\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{t}\sup_{y\in[\frac{\varepsilon}{2},k]}\lvert e(y,s)\rvert^{2}\big(\int_{0}^{k}\sigma g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\rvert^{2}}{v}{\mathrm{d}}x\big)\,{\mathrm{d}}s.

I_{4}^{(3)}
is estimated by using Theorem
4.1
(iii)(ii)

and Lemma
4.4
–
4.6
:

\displaystyle\dfrac{2\beta}{(\gamma-1)^{2}}I_{4}^{(3)}

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\sigma g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{\Lambda(v,e)}{v^{2}}\left\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\right\rvert\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{(\gamma-1)^{2}}{2\beta}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\sigma g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{\Lambda(v,e)^{2}}{v^{3}}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s+\dfrac{(\gamma-1)^{2}}{2\beta}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\sigma g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\rvert^{2}}{v}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\leq C(\varepsilon)+C(\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{t}\big(\sup_{y\in[\frac{\varepsilon}{2},k]}\lvert e-1\rvert^{2}(y,s)+1\big)\big(\int_{0}^{k}\sigma g_{\varepsilon}\lvert e-1\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x\big)\,{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\leq C(\varepsilon)+C(\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{t}\sup_{y\in[\frac{\varepsilon}{2},k]}e^{2}(y,s)\,{\mathrm{d}}s\leq C(\varepsilon).

Combining the estimates for
I_{4}^{(1)}
–
I_{4}^{(3)}
, it follows that

\displaystyle I_{4}\leq\dfrac{1}{8}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\sigma g_{\varepsilon}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{t}u\rvert^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s+C(\varepsilon)+C(\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{t}\sup_{y\in[\varepsilon/2,k]}(e^{2}+u^{2})(y,s)\big(\int_{0}^{k}\sigma g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\rvert^{2}}{v}{\mathrm{d}}x\big)\,{\mathrm{d}}s.

5.
I_{5}

is estimated by using Theorem
4.1
(iii)(ii)
, Lemmas
4.4
–
4.6
,
and (
4.21
)–(
4.23
):

\displaystyle I_{5}

\displaystyle\leq\beta^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\sup_{y\in[\frac{\varepsilon}{2},\varepsilon]}\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}(y,s)\big(\int_{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}^{\varepsilon}\dfrac{32}{\varepsilon^{2}}\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\rvert^{2}}{v}{\mathrm{d}}x\big)\,{\mathrm{d}}s+\dfrac{1}{4}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}^{\varepsilon}\sigma\dfrac{\varepsilon^{2}}{32}\lvert g_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\rvert^{2}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{t}u\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{1}{4}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}^{\varepsilon}\sigma g_{\varepsilon}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{t}u\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t+C(\varepsilon).

6. Combining the estimates of
I_{1}
–
I_{5}

together, it follows that

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\sigma g_{\varepsilon}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{t}u\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s+\dfrac{\beta}{4}\int_{0}^{k}\Big(\sigma g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\rvert^{2}}{v}\Big)(x,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\leq C(\varepsilon)+C(\varepsilon)\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\sigma^{2}g_{\varepsilon}^{2}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{t}e\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+C(\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{t}\sup_{y\in[\frac{\varepsilon}{2},k]}(e+e^{2}+u^{2})(y,s)\Big(\int_{0}^{k}\big(\sigma g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\rvert^{2}}{v}\big)(x,s)\,{\mathrm{d}}x\Big)\,{\mathrm{d}}s.

Applying Grönwall’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\sigma g_{\varepsilon}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{t}u\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s+\dfrac{\beta}{4}\int_{0}^{k}\Big(\sigma g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\rvert^{2}}{v}\Big)(x,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\leq C(\varepsilon)\Big(1+\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\sigma^{2}g_{\varepsilon}^{2}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{t}e\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big)\Big(1+\big(\int_{0}^{t}h_{\varepsilon}(s)\,{\mathrm{d}}s\big)\exp\big(\int_{0}^{t}h_{\varepsilon}(s)\,{\mathrm{d}}s\big)\Big),

where
h_{\varepsilon}(s)\vcentcolon=C(\varepsilon)\sup_{y\in[\frac{\varepsilon}{2},k]}(e+e^{2}+u^{2})(y,s)
.
By Lemmas
4.3
–
4.4
, we obtain (
4.47
).
∎

Lemma 4.9

(
L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\varepsilon,k))
–Estimates of
{\mathrm{D}}_{x}u
and
{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e
)
.

For each
\varepsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\varepsilon}^{k}\big(\sigma^{2}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{t}e\rvert^{2}+\sigma g_{\varepsilon}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{t}u\rvert^{2}\big)\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s+\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\int_{\varepsilon}^{k}\Big(\sigma^{2}\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\rvert^{2}+\sigma\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\rvert^{2}}{v}\Big)(x,s)\,{\mathrm{d}}x\leq C(\varepsilon).

(4.48)

Proof.

We divide the proof into six steps.

1. Rewriting the energy equation
\eqref{eqs:LFNS-k}_{3}
in terms of the effective
viscosity flux
F
, then multiplying both sides by
\sigma^{2}(t)g_{\varepsilon}^{2}{\mathrm{D}}_{t}e
, and integrating by parts, it follows that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\sigma^{2}g_{\varepsilon}^{2}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{t}e\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s+\dfrac{\kappa}{2}\int_{0}^{k}\Big(\sigma^{2}g_{\varepsilon}^{2}\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\rvert^{2}\Big)(x,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle=\kappa\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\sigma\sigma^{\prime}(s)g_{\varepsilon}^{2}\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s+m\kappa\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\sigma^{2}g_{\varepsilon}^{2}\dfrac{r^{2m-1}u}{v}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\sigma^{2}g_{\varepsilon}^{2}\mathcal{A}{\mathrm{D}}_{t}e\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s-\dfrac{\kappa}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\sigma^{2}g_{\varepsilon}^{2}\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v^{2}}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\sigma^{2}g_{\varepsilon}^{2}F{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u){\mathrm{D}}_{t}e\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s-\kappa\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}2\sigma^{2}g_{\varepsilon}g_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}{\mathrm{D}}_{t}e{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\sum_{i=1}^{6}I_{i},

where
\mathcal{A}\vcentcolon=-(\gamma-1){\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)-4m\mu\dfrac{u}{r}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)+2mn\mu\dfrac{v}{r^{2}}u^{2}
.
By Lemma
4.4
, we have

I_{1}\vcentcolon=\kappa\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\sigma\sigma^{\prime}(s)g_{\varepsilon}^{2}\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s\leq\kappa\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}^{k}\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s\leq C(\varepsilon).

I_{2}
is estimated by using (
4.21
)–(
4.23
)
and Lemma
4.4
:

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2m\kappa\int_{0}^{t}\sup_{y\in[\frac{\varepsilon}{2},k]}\dfrac{u}{r}(y,s)\big(\int_{0}^{k}\sigma^{2}g_{\varepsilon}^{2}\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}\,\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x\big){\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\leq C(\varepsilon)+C(\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{t}\sup_{y\in[\frac{\varepsilon}{2},k]}u^{2}(y,s)\big(\int_{0}^{k}\sigma^{2}g_{\varepsilon}^{2}\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x\big)\,{\mathrm{d}}s.

2.
I_{3}
is estimated by using Theorem
4.1
(iii)(ii)
and
Lemmas
4.4
–
4.6
:

\displaystyle I_{3}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\sigma^{2}g_{\varepsilon}^{2}\Big\{-(\gamma-1){\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)-4m\mu\dfrac{u}{r}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)+2mn\mu\dfrac{v}{r^{2}}u^{2}\Big\}{\mathrm{D}}_{t}e\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s

(4.49)

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{1}{16}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\sigma^{2}g_{\varepsilon}^{2}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{t}e\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s+C(\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{t}\sup_{y\in[\frac{\varepsilon}{2},k]}(1+u^{2})(y,s)\int_{0}^{k}\sigma^{2}g_{\varepsilon}^{2}\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\rvert^{2}}{v}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle+C(\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{k}\sigma^{2}g_{\varepsilon}^{2}u^{4}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{1}{16}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\sigma^{2}g_{\varepsilon}^{2}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{t}e\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t+C(\varepsilon)\Big(1+\int_{0}^{t}\sup_{y\in[\frac{\varepsilon}{2},k]}u^{2}(y,s)\big(\int_{0}^{k}\sigma^{2}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\rvert^{2}}{v}{\mathrm{d}}x\big)\,{\mathrm{d}}s\Big).

In fact, by Lemmas
4.3
and
4.8
, it follows that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\sup_{y\in[\frac{\varepsilon}{2},k]}u^{2}(y,s)\big(\int_{0}^{k}\sigma^{2}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\rvert^{2}}{v}{\mathrm{d}}x\big)\,{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\int_{0}^{k}\Big(\sigma g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\rvert^{2}}{v}\Big)(x,s)\,{\mathrm{d}}x\int_{0}^{t}\sup_{y\in[\frac{\varepsilon}{2},k]}u^{2}(y,\widetilde{t}\,)\,{\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{t}

\displaystyle\leq C(\varepsilon)+C(\varepsilon)\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\sigma^{2}g_{\varepsilon}^{2}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{t}e\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{1}{16}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\sigma^{2}g_{\varepsilon}^{2}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{t}e\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s+C(\varepsilon).

Substituting the above estimate into (
4.49
) leads to

\displaystyle I_{3}\leq\dfrac{1}{8}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\sigma^{2}g_{\varepsilon}^{2}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{t}e\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s+C(\varepsilon).

3.

I_{4}
is first rewritten in terms of
F
and then estimated by using Theorem
4.1
(iii)(ii)
:

\displaystyle I_{4}\vcentcolon

\displaystyle=-\dfrac{\kappa}{2\beta}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\sigma^{2}g_{\varepsilon}^{2}\Big(F+(\gamma-1)\dfrac{e-v}{v}\Big)\dfrac{\lvert r^{m}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\rvert^{2}}{v}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{\kappa}{2\beta}\int_{0}^{t}\sup_{y\in[\frac{\varepsilon}{2},k]}\lvert\sqrt{g_{\varepsilon}}F\rvert(y,s)\big(\int_{0}^{k}\sigma^{2}g_{\varepsilon}^{\frac{3}{2}}\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x)\,{\mathrm{d}}s+C(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle+C(\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{t}\sup_{y\in[\frac{\varepsilon}{2},k]}e(y,s)\big(\int_{0}^{k}\sigma^{2}g_{\varepsilon}^{2}\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x\big)\,{\mathrm{d}}s.

By Lemma
4.7
and the Sobolev embedding theorem:
W^{1,1}(\varepsilon,k)\xhookrightarrow{}C^{0}(\varepsilon,k)
, it follows that

\displaystyle\sup_{y\in[\frac{\varepsilon}{2},k]}\lvert\sqrt{g_{\varepsilon}}F\rvert^{2}(y,s)

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}^{k}\big(g_{\varepsilon}F^{2}+2g_{\varepsilon}\lvert F{\mathrm{D}}_{x}F\rvert+2g_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}F^{2}\big)(x,s)\,{\mathrm{d}}x

(4.50)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}^{k}\big(C(\varepsilon)F^{2}+g_{\varepsilon}r^{2m}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}F\rvert^{2}\big)\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}^{k}\Big(C(\varepsilon)\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\rvert^{2}}{v}+g_{\varepsilon}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{t}u\rvert^{2}\Big)\,{\mathrm{d}}x+C(\varepsilon),

so that, using Lemma
4.6

and the Cauchy-Schwartz inequality, we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{\kappa}{\beta}\int_{0}^{t}\sup_{y\in[\frac{\varepsilon}{2},k]}\lvert g_{\varepsilon}^{\frac{1}{2}}F\rvert(y,s)\big(\int_{0}^{k}\sigma^{2}g_{\varepsilon}^{\frac{3}{2}}\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\rvert^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x\big){\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\leq C(\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{t}\Big\{\sigma^{2}+\int_{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}^{k}\sigma^{2}\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\rvert^{2}}{v}\,{\mathrm{d}}x+\int_{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}^{k}\sigma^{2}g_{\varepsilon}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{t}u\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x\Big\}{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\quad+C(\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{t}\sigma^{2}\Big(\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}^{\frac{3}{2}}\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x\Big)^{2}{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\sigma g_{\varepsilon}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{t}u\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s+C(\varepsilon)+C(\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x\Big)\Big(\int_{0}^{k}\sigma^{2}g_{\varepsilon}^{2}\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x\Big){\mathrm{d}}s.

Using the above estimate, Lemma
4.8
, and the Cauchy-Schwartz inequality,
one has

\displaystyle I_{4}

\displaystyle\leq C(\varepsilon)+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\sigma g_{\varepsilon}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{t}u\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s+C(\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{t}\sup_{y\in[\frac{\varepsilon}{2},k]}e(y,s)\big(\int_{0}^{k}\sigma^{2}g_{\varepsilon}^{2}\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x\big){\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\quad+C(\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x\Big)\Big(\int_{0}^{k}\sigma^{2}g_{\varepsilon}^{2}\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x\Big){\mathrm{d}}s,

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{1}{8}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\sigma^{2}g_{\varepsilon}^{2}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{t}e\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s+C(\varepsilon)+C(\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{t}\sup_{y\in[\frac{\varepsilon}{2},k]}e(y,s)\big(\int_{0}^{k}\sigma^{2}g_{\varepsilon}^{2}\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x\big){\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\quad+C(\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x\Big)\Big(\int_{0}^{k}\sigma^{2}g_{\varepsilon}^{2}\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x\Big){\mathrm{d}}s.

4.
I_{5}
is first estimated as follows:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} I_{5}&\leq 4\int_{0}^{t}\sup_{y\in[\frac{\varepsilon}{2},k]}(g_{\varepsilon}v\lvert F\rvert^{2})(y,s)\big(\int_{0}^{k}\sigma^{2}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\rvert^{2}}{v}{\mathrm{d}}x\big){\mathrm{d}}s+\dfrac{1}{16}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\sigma^{2}g_{\varepsilon}^{2}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{t}e\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s.\end{aligned}

(4.51)

By the same argument as (
4.50
), we have

\displaystyle\sup_{y\in[\frac{\varepsilon}{2},k]}(g_{\varepsilon}\lvert F\rvert^{2})(y,s)\leq C(\varepsilon)+C(\varepsilon)\int_{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}^{k}\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\rvert^{2}}{v}{\mathrm{d}}x+C(\varepsilon)\Big(\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}F^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\int_{0}^{k}g_{\varepsilon}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{t}u\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}},

which, along with Theorem
4.1
(iii)(ii)
and Lemma
4.6
, implies that

\displaystyle 4\int_{0}^{t}\sup_{y\in[\frac{\varepsilon}{2},k]}(g_{\varepsilon}v\lvert F\rvert^{2})(y,s)\big(\int_{0}^{k}\sigma^{2}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\rvert^{2}}{v}{\mathrm{d}}x\big){\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\leq C(\varepsilon)+C(\varepsilon)\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\int_{0}^{k}\Big(\sigma g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\rvert^{2}}{v}\Big)(x,s)\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\quad+C(\varepsilon)\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\sigma g_{\varepsilon}F^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\sigma g_{\varepsilon}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{t}u\rvert^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\int_{0}^{k}\Big(\sigma g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\rvert^{2}}{v}\Big)(x,s)\,{\mathrm{d}}x.

Then applying Lemmas
4.7
–
4.8
on the above
and using the Cauchy-Schwartz inequality yield

\displaystyle 4\int_{0}^{t}\sup_{y\in[\frac{\varepsilon}{2},k]}(g_{\varepsilon}v\lvert F\rvert^{2})(y,s)\big(\int_{0}^{k}\sigma^{2}g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\rvert^{2}}{v}{\mathrm{d}}x\big){\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\leq C(\varepsilon)+C(\varepsilon)\Big\{1+\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\sigma^{2}g_{\varepsilon}^{2}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{t}e\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big\}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big\{1+\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\sigma^{2}g_{\varepsilon}^{2}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{t}e\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{1}{16}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\sigma^{2}g_{\varepsilon}^{2}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{t}e\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s+C(\varepsilon).

Substituting the above into (
4.51
), we obtain

\displaystyle I_{5}\leq\dfrac{1}{8}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\sigma^{2}g_{\varepsilon}^{2}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{t}e\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s+C(\varepsilon).

5.
I_{6}

is estimated by using Theorem
4.1
(iii)(ii)
, Lemma
4.4
, and (
4.21
)–(
4.23
):

\displaystyle I_{6}

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{1}{8}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}^{\varepsilon}\sigma^{2}g_{\varepsilon}^{2}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{t}e\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s+8\kappa^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\sup_{y\in[\frac{\varepsilon}{2},\varepsilon]}\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}(y,s)\big(\int_{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}^{\varepsilon}\sigma^{2}\lvert g_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\rvert^{2}\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x\big)\,{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{1}{8}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\sigma^{2}g_{\varepsilon}^{2}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{t}e\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s+C(\varepsilon).

6. Combining
the estimates for
I_{1}
–
I_{6}
together, we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\sigma^{2}g_{\varepsilon}^{2}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{t}e\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s+\dfrac{\kappa}{2}\int_{0}^{k}\Big(\sigma^{2}g_{\varepsilon}^{2}\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\rvert^{2}\Big)(x,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\leq C(\varepsilon)+C(\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{h}_{\varepsilon}(s)\big(\int_{0}^{k}\sigma^{2}g_{\varepsilon}^{2}\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x\big)\,{\mathrm{d}}s,

(4.52)

where
\widetilde{h}_{\varepsilon}(s)\vcentcolon=\int_{0}^{k}\big(g_{\varepsilon}\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}\left\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\right\rvert^{2}\big)(x,s)\,{\mathrm{d}}x+\sup_{y\in[\frac{\varepsilon}{2},k]}(u^{2}+e)(y,s)
.
Then it follows from Lemmas
4.3
–
4.4

and the Grönwall inequality that

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\sigma^{2}g_{\varepsilon}^{2}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{t}e\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s+\dfrac{\kappa}{2}\int_{0}^{k}\Big(\sigma^{2}g_{\varepsilon}^{2}\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\rvert^{2}\Big)(x,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}x\leq C(\varepsilon),

which, along with Lemma
4.8
, yields (
4.48
).
This completes the proof.
∎

Corollary 4.1
.

For all
(x,t)\in[\varepsilon,k]\times[0,T]
,

\sigma^{\frac{1}{4}}(t)\lvert u(x,t)\rvert+\sigma^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)e(x,t)\leq C(\varepsilon).

(4.53)

Proof.

First, repeating the same calculation (
4.3
) in the proof of Lemma
4.3
, one has

\displaystyle\sigma^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)\sup_{y\in[\varepsilon,k]}u^{2}(y,t)\leq C(\varepsilon)\int_{\varepsilon}^{k}u^{2}(x,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}x+C\int_{\varepsilon}^{k}\sigma(t)\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\rvert^{2}}{v}(x,t){\mathrm{d}}x\leq C(\varepsilon),

where we have used Lemma
4.9
.
Then, by the Sobolev embedding theorem, Theorem
4.1
(iii)(ii)
,
Lemmas
4.4
and
4.9
, and (
4.21
)–(
4.23
),
we have

\displaystyle\sigma(t)\sup_{y\in[\varepsilon,k]}(e-1)^{2}(y,t)

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\varepsilon}^{k}\sigma(t)(e-1)^{2}(x,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}x+C\int_{\varepsilon}^{k}\sigma(t)(\lvert e-1\rvert\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\rvert)(x,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\leq C\sup_{y\in[\varepsilon,k]}\Big(\sigma(t)+\dfrac{v}{r^{2m}}(y,t)\Big)\int_{\varepsilon}^{k}(e-1)^{2}(x,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\quad\,+\int_{\varepsilon}^{k}\sigma^{2}(t)\Big(\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\rvert^{2}\Big)(x,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}x\leq C(\varepsilon),

which implies
\eqref{eqs:ubd}_{2}
.
∎

4.5.
Lower bound of the internal energy

We now estimate the lower bound of

e
.

Lemma 4.10

(Lower Bound of the Internal Energy
e
)
.

There exists
C(a)>0
such that

e(x,t)\geq C(a)^{-1}\qquad\text{for all $(x,t)\in[0,\infty)\times[0,T]$}.

(4.54)

Proof.

Multiplying equation
\eqref{eqs:LFNS-k}_{3}
with
-e^{-2}
to obtain

{\mathrm{D}}_{t}e^{-1}=(\gamma-1)\dfrac{{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)}{ev}-\kappa e^{-2}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}\Big(\dfrac{r^{2m}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e}{v}\Big)+\dfrac{2\mu m}{e^{2}}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m-1}u^{2})-\beta\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\rvert^{2}}{e^{2}v},

(4.55)

where
\beta:=2\mu+\lambda
.
To further reduce the equation, we write

-\kappa e^{-2}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}\Big(\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\Big)=\kappa{\mathrm{D}}_{x}\Big(\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e^{-1}\Big)-2\kappa\dfrac{r^{2m}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\rvert^{2}}{ve^{3}}.

Moreover, using the relation
{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m-1}u^{2})=\frac{2u}{r}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)-n\frac{vu^{2}}{r^{2}}
,
we have

\displaystyle-\beta\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\rvert^{2}}{e^{2}v}+\dfrac{2\mu m}{e^{2}}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m-1}u^{2})

\displaystyle=-\Big(\dfrac{2\mu}{n}+\lambda\Big)\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\rvert^{2}}{e^{2}v}-2m\mu\dfrac{v}{e^{2}}\Big(\dfrac{{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)}{\sqrt{n}v}-\sqrt{n}\dfrac{u}{r}\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq-\Big(\dfrac{2\mu}{n}+\lambda\Big)\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\rvert^{2}}{e^{2}v}.

Therefore, using the above two inequalities, (
4.55
) can be reduced to the inequality:

{\mathrm{D}}_{t}e^{-1}+2\kappa\dfrac{r^{2m}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\rvert^{2}}{ve^{3}}+\Big(\dfrac{2\mu}{n}+\lambda\Big)\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\rvert^{2}}{e^{2}v}\leq\dfrac{(\gamma-1){\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)}{ev}+\kappa{\mathrm{D}}_{x}\Big(\dfrac{r^{2m}}{v}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e^{-1}\Big).

(4.56)

Multiplying (
4.56
) by
je^{-j+1}
for
j\geq 2
integers and integrating in
x\in[0,k]
yield

\displaystyle\dfrac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\int_{0}^{k}e^{-j}{\mathrm{d}}x+j\Big(\dfrac{2\mu}{n}+\lambda\Big)\int_{0}^{k}\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\rvert^{2}}{e^{j+1}v}{\mathrm{d}}x+2j\kappa\int_{0}^{k}\dfrac{r^{2m}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e\rvert^{2}}{ve^{j+2}}{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\leq j(\gamma-1)\int_{0}^{k}e^{-j}\dfrac{{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)}{v}{\mathrm{d}}x-j(j-1)\kappa\int_{0}^{k}\dfrac{r^{2m}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e^{-1}\rvert^{2}}{ve^{j-2}}{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{k}v^{-1}e^{1-j}{\mathrm{d}}x+\dfrac{j}{2}\Big(\dfrac{2\mu}{n}+\lambda\Big)\int_{0}^{k}\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(r^{m}u)\rvert^{2}}{e^{j+1}v}{\mathrm{d}}x-j(j-1)\kappa\int_{0}^{k}\dfrac{r^{2m}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}e^{-1}\rvert^{2}}{ve^{j-2}}{\mathrm{d}}x.

Rearranging the above and using Lemma
4.1
and Theorem
4.1
(iii)(ii)
, we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\int_{0}^{k}e^{-j}{\mathrm{d}}x\leq C\int_{0}^{k}e^{-j}\dfrac{e}{v}{\mathrm{d}}x\leq C(a)\sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)\int_{0}^{k}e^{-j}{\mathrm{d}}x.

Integrating the above inequality in time, it follows that

\int_{0}^{k}e^{-j}(x,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}x\leq\lVert e_{0}^{-1}\rVert_{L^{j}(0,k)}^{j}+C(a)\int_{0}^{t}\sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\tau)\big(\int_{0}^{k}e^{-j}{\mathrm{d}}x\big)\,{\mathrm{d}}\tau.

Thus, by the Grönwall inequality, we obtain that, for each integer
j\geq 2
,

\displaystyle\lVert e^{-1}(\cdot,t)\rVert_{L^{j}(0,k)}^{j}

\displaystyle\leq C(a)\lVert e_{0}^{-1}\rVert_{L^{j}(0,k)}^{j}+C(a)\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\tau){\mathrm{d}}\tau\Big)\exp\Big\{C(a)\int_{0}^{t}\sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\tau){\mathrm{d}}\tau\Big\}\leq C(a).

Letting
j\to\infty
in the above inequality, we conclude that

\lVert e^{-1}(\cdot,t)\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq C(a)\lVert e_{0}^{-1}\rVert_{L^{\infty}}
.
∎

5.
Global Weak Solutions of the Exterior Problem in the Eulerian Coordinates

In this section, the approximate Lagrangian solutions are converted into
the approximate solutions in the Eulerian coordinates
by constructing a set of coordinate transformations.
Then these functions are extended into the entire domain

(r,t)\in[a,\infty)\times[0,T]
by using a set of smooth cut-off functions.
Finally, a global weak solution of problem (
1.6
) and (
3.9
) is obtained
via the limit:
k\to\infty
.

5.1.
Coordinate transformation and the Jacobian

Let
\{(\widetilde{v}_{a,k},\widetilde{u}_{a,k},\widetilde{e}_{a,k},\widetilde{r}_{a,k})(x,s)\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}

be the solutions obtained in the bounded Lagrangian domain for
(x,s)\in[0,k]\times[0,T]
.
For each fixed
(a,k)\in(0,1)\times\mathbb{N}
, the coordinate transformation
(r,t)=\mathcal{T}_{a,k}(x,s)
is defined as

r=\mathcal{T}_{a,k}^{(1)}(x,s)=\widetilde{r}_{a,k}(x,s),\quad t=\mathcal{T}_{a,k}^{(2)}(x,s)=s\qquad\,\,\,\text{for $(x,s)\in[0,k]\times[0,T]$}.

(5.1)

Then we see that
\mathcal{T}_{a,k}
has the image:

T
_a,k([0,k]×[0,T] ) = R_a,k
:=
{
(r,t) : t∈[0,T], r∈[a,
~

r

_a,k(k,t)]
}
.

Moreover, it follows from a direct calculation that

J\vcentcolon={\rm det}({\mathrm{D}}_{x}\mathcal{T}_{a,k}\,\,\,{\mathrm{D}}_{s}\mathcal{T}_{a,k})=\widetilde{v}_{a,k}(\widetilde{r}_{a,k})^{-m},

which, along with Theorem
4.1
and Lemma
4.1
, yields that

C^{-1}(a)(a^{n}+C(a)x)^{-\frac{m}{n}}\leq J(x,t)\leq a^{-m}C(a)\qquad\text{ for all $(x,t)\in[0,k]\times[0,T]$}.

(5.2)

Thus, the map
\mathcal{T}_{a,k}\vcentcolon[0,k]\times[0,T]\to R_{a,k}
is a diffeomorphism so that the inverse map
\mathcal{T}_{a,k}^{-1}
exists.
Define
(x_{a,k},s_{a,k})(r,t)\vcentcolon=\mathcal{T}_{a,k}^{-1}(r,t)
. Then

\widetilde{r}_{a,k}(x_{a,k}(r,t),t)=r,\quad s_{a,k}(r,t)=t\qquad\,\,\text{for all $(r,t)\in R_{a,k}$}.

Let
\widetilde{\theta}(x,t)\vcentcolon[0,k]\times[0,T]\to{\mathbb{R}}
be such that
\widetilde{\theta}\in C^{1}
.
Define
\theta(r,t)\vcentcolon=\widetilde{\theta}(x_{a,k}(r,t),t)
for each
(r,t)\in R_{a,k}
.
Then, by the inverse function theorem,
its Eulerian derivative can be computed:

\begin{cases}\partial_{r}\theta(r,t)=\dfrac{\big(\widetilde{r}_{a,k}\big)^{m}(x_{a,k}(r,t),t)}{\widetilde{v}_{a,k}(x_{a,k}(r,t),t)}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}\widetilde{\theta}(x_{a,k}(r,t),t)=r^{m}\dfrac{{\mathrm{D}}_{x}\widetilde{\theta}}{\widetilde{v}_{a,k}}(x_{a,k}(r,t),t),\\
\partial_{t}\theta(r,t)={\mathrm{D}}_{t}\widetilde{\theta}(x_{a,k}(r,t),t)-r^{m}\big(\dfrac{\widetilde{u}_{a,k}}{\widetilde{v}_{a,k}}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}\widetilde{\theta}\big)(x_{a,k}(r,t),t),\end{cases}

(5.3)

for each
(r,t)\in R_{a,k}
.
Now, for each
(a,k)
, solution
(\widetilde{v}_{a,k},\widetilde{u}_{a,k},\widetilde{e}_{a,k})(x,s)
in
(x,t)\in[0,k]\times[0,T]
is pulled back to
R_{a,k}
as:

(\overline{v}_{a,k},\overline{u}_{a,k},\overline{e}_{a,k})(r,t)\vcentcolon=(\widetilde{v}_{a,k},\widetilde{u}_{a,k},\widetilde{e}_{a,k})(x_{a,k}(r,t),t)\qquad\ \text{for $(r,t)\in R_{a,k}$}.

(5.4)

5.2.
Extension in the Eulerian domain

We now extend
(\overline{v}_{a,k},\overline{u}_{a,k},\overline{e}_{a,k})
into the whole Eulerian domain
[a,\infty)\times[0,T]
.
First, let
\chi\in C^{\infty}\vcentcolon{\mathbb{R}}\to[0,1]
be such that

\chi(\zeta)=1
if
\zeta\leq 0
,
\chi(\zeta)=0
if
\zeta\geq 1
, and
\lvert\chi^{\prime}\rvert\leq 2
in
{\mathbb{R}}
.
With this, the following cut-off functions are defined:

\varphi_{a,k}(r,t)\vcentcolon=\chi(\dfrac{2r-\widetilde{r}_{a,k}(k,t)}{\widetilde{r}_{a,k}(k,t)}).

(5.5)

Since
\widetilde{u}_{a,k}(k,t)=0
for all
t\in[0,T]
, it follows that, for all
(r,t)\in[a,\infty)\times[0,T]
,

\partial_{r}\varphi_{a,k}(r,t)=\dfrac{2}{\widetilde{r}_{a,k}(k,t)}\chi^{\prime}(\dfrac{2r-\widetilde{r}_{a,k}(k,t)}{\widetilde{r}_{a,k}(k,t)}),\qquad\partial_{t}\varphi_{a,k}(r,t)=0.

(5.6)

Then

\varphi_{a,k}\in C^{\infty}([0,\infty)\times[0,T])
.
Moreover, by construction,

\varphi_{a,k}(r,t)=1\ \text{ if $r\in[0,\frac{\widetilde{r}_{a,k}(k,t)}{2}]$},\qquad\varphi_{a,k}(r,t)=0\ \text{ if $r\in[\widetilde{r}_{a,k}(k,t),\infty)$}.

Now, by Theorem
4.1
(iii)(ii)
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{r}_{a,k}(k,t)

\displaystyle=\Big(a^{n}+n\int_{0}^{k}v_{a,k}(y,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}y\Big)^{\frac{1}{n}}\geq\Big(n\int_{\frac{1}{2}}^{k}v_{a,k}(y,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}y\Big)^{\frac{1}{n}}\geq\Big(\frac{n}{C(T)}\int_{\frac{1}{2}}^{k}{\mathrm{d}}y\Big)^{\frac{1}{n}}\geq\frac{1}{C(T)},

where the universal constant
C(T)>0
depends only on
T
and
n
.
It also follows from Theorem
4.1
that

\displaystyle\widetilde{r}_{a,k}(k,t)=\Big(a^{n}+n\int_{0}^{k}v_{a,k}(y,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}y\Big)^{\frac{1}{n}}\geq\frac{k^{\frac{1}{n}}}{C(a)},

so that, by (
5.6
),

\lvert\partial_{r}\varphi_{a,k}(r,t)\rvert\leq\min\{C(T),C(a)k^{-\frac{1}{n}}\},\ \ \partial_{t}\varphi_{a,k}(r,t)=0\qquad\mbox{for all $(r,t)\in[a,\infty)\times[0,T]$}.

(5.7)

Using (
5.1
)–(
5.5
), the extended approximate functions
(\rho_{a,k},u_{a,k},e_{a,k})(r,t)
in the entire Eulerian domain
(r,t)\in[a,\infty)\times[0,T]

are defined by (
3.1
) in §
3.1
.

The following lemmas show that the extended functions inherit the
a-priori
estimates derived
in §
4
. As before, we still denote
\sigma\vcentcolon=\min\{1,t\}
.

Lemma 5.1
.

There exists an integer
N(a)=N(a,T,C_{0})\in\mathbb{N}
such that, for all
k\geq N(a)
,

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{a}^{\infty}\Big\{\rho_{a,k}\big(\frac{1}{2}\lvert u_{a,k}\rvert^{2}+\psi(e_{a,k})\big)+G(\rho_{a,k})\Big\}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r+\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\int_{a}^{\infty}\dfrac{\lvert\partial_{r}e_{a,k}\rvert^{2}}{e_{a,k}^{2}}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{a}^{\infty}\Big\{\big(\dfrac{2\mu}{n}+\lambda\big)\dfrac{\lvert\partial_{r}u_{a,k}+m\frac{u_{a,k}}{r}\rvert^{2}}{e_{a,k}}+\dfrac{2m\mu}{n}\dfrac{\lvert\partial_{r}u_{a,k}-\frac{u_{a,k}}{r}\rvert^{2}}{e_{a,k}}\Big\}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t\leq C(T).

(5.8)

Proof.

Throughout the proof, we suppress
(a,k)
for simplicity and denote:

\displaystyle(\rho,u,e,\varphi)(r,t)\equiv(\rho_{a,k},u_{a,k},e_{a,k},\varphi_{a,k})(r,t)

\displaystyle\text{for $(r,t)\in[a,\infty)\times[0,T]$},

(5.9)

\displaystyle(\overline{v},\overline{u},\overline{e})(r,t)\equiv(\overline{v}_{a,k},\overline{u}_{a,k},\overline{e}_{a,k})(r,t)

\displaystyle\text{for $(r,t)\in R_{a,k}$},

\displaystyle(\widetilde{v},\widetilde{u},\widetilde{e},\widetilde{r})(x,t)\equiv(\widetilde{v}_{a,k},\widetilde{u}_{a,k},\widetilde{e}_{a,k},\widetilde{r}_{a,k})(x,t)
