\displaystyle\text{for $(x,t)\in[0,k]\times[0,T]$}.

Since
G(\cdot)
and
\psi(\cdot)
are convex, and
0\leq\varphi\leq 1
, it follows
that
G(\rho)\leq\varphi G(\overline{v}^{-1})
and
\psi(e)\leq\varphi\,\psi(\overline{e})
.
Using these and the fact that the Jacobian is bounded from (
5.2
),
one can convert the integral in the Eulerian coordinates into the Lagrangian coordinates:

\displaystyle\int_{a}^{\infty}\Big(\rho\big(\frac{1}{2}\lvert u\rvert^{2}+\psi(e)\big)+G(\rho)\Big)(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r

\displaystyle\leq\int_{a}^{\widetilde{r}(k,t)}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\lvert\overline{u}\rvert^{2}+\psi(\overline{e})+\overline{v}G(\overline{v}^{-1})\Big)\overline{v}^{-1}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r+\int_{a}^{\widetilde{r}(k,t)}(1-\varphi)\Big(\frac{1}{2}\lvert\overline{u}\rvert^{2}+\psi(\overline{e})\Big)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{k}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\lvert\widetilde{u}\rvert^{2}+\psi(\widetilde{e})+\psi(\widetilde{v})\Big)(x,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}x+\int_{0}^{k}\big(1-\varphi(\widetilde{r}(x,t),t)\big)\Big(\frac{1}{2}\widetilde{v}\lvert\widetilde{u}\rvert^{2}+\widetilde{v}\psi(\widetilde{e})\Big)(x,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}x,

where

zG(z^{-1})=\psi(z)
has been used.
By Theorem
4.1
(iii)(i)
, the first term of the right-hand side is bounded by
C_{0}>0
.
By the definition of
\widetilde{r}(x,t)
,

\Big(a^{n}+\frac{n}{C(a)}x\Big)^{\frac{1}{n}}\leq\widetilde{r}(x,t)=\Big(a^{n}+n\int_{0}^{x}\widetilde{v}(y,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}y\Big)^{\frac{1}{n}}\leq\big(a^{n}+nC(a)x\big)^{\frac{1}{n}}.

Thus, for fixed
a>0
, if
k\in\mathbb{N}
is such that

k\geq M(a)\vcentcolon=\dfrac{2^{n}-1}{n}a^{n}C(a)+2^{n}C(a)^{2}
, then

\widetilde{r}(k,t)\geq 2\widetilde{r}(1,t).

(5.10)

Using this and the support of the cut-off function
\varphi
, it follows that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{k}\big(1-\varphi(\widetilde{r}(x,t),t)\big)\Big(\frac{1}{2}\widetilde{v}\lvert\widetilde{u}\rvert^{2}+\widetilde{v}\psi(\widetilde{e})\Big)(x,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle=\int_{a}^{\widetilde{r}(k,t)}\big(1-\varphi(r,t)\big)\Big(\frac{1}{2}\lvert\overline{u}\rvert^{2}+\psi(\overline{e})\Big)(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\leq\int_{\widetilde{r}(1,t)}^{\widetilde{r}(k,t)}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\lvert\overline{u}\rvert^{2}+\psi(\overline{e})\Big)(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r

\displaystyle=\int_{1}^{k}\widetilde{v}(x,t)\Big(\frac{1}{2}\lvert\widetilde{u}\rvert^{2}+\psi(\widetilde{e})\Big)(x,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}x\leq\sup_{y\in[1,k]}\widetilde{v}(y,t)\int_{1}^{k}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\lvert\widetilde{u}\rvert^{2}+\psi(\widetilde{e})\Big)(x,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}x\leq C(T)C_{0},

where Theorem
4.1
(iii)(i)
–
(iii)(ii)
has been used.

Next, the dissipation terms in
(
5.1
)
are considered.
Using (
3.1
) and the fact that
z\mapsto z^{-2}
is convex in

z\in(0,\infty)
yield

\displaystyle\dfrac{\lvert\partial_{r}e\rvert^{2}}{2\lvert e\rvert^{2}}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{\lvert\varphi\partial_{r}\overline{e}+(\overline{e}-1)\partial_{r}\varphi\rvert^{2}}{2\lvert\varphi\overline{e}+(1-\varphi)\rvert^{2}}\leq 2^{-1}\big(\varphi\overline{e}^{-2}+(1-\varphi)\big)\lvert\varphi\partial_{r}\overline{e}+(\overline{e}-1)\partial_{r}\varphi\rvert^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\varphi^{2}\dfrac{\lvert\partial_{r}\overline{e}\rvert^{2}}{\overline{e}^{2}}+\dfrac{\lvert\overline{e}-1\rvert^{2}}{\overline{e}^{2}}\varphi\lvert\partial_{r}\varphi\rvert^{2}+(1-\varphi)\varphi^{2}\lvert\partial_{r}\overline{e}\rvert^{2}+(1-\varphi)\lvert\partial_{r}\varphi\rvert^{2}\lvert\overline{e}-1\rvert^{2}.

Multiplying both sides by
r^{m}
and integrating in
[a,\infty)\times[0,T]
, it follows from (
5.7
) and (
5.10
) that, if
k\geq M(a)
, then

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{a}^{\infty}\dfrac{\lvert\partial_{r}e\rvert^{2}}{e^{2}}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\int_{a}^{\infty}\Big\{\varphi^{2}\dfrac{\lvert\partial_{r}\overline{e}\rvert^{2}}{\overline{e}^{2}}+\dfrac{\lvert\overline{e}-1\rvert^{2}}{\overline{e}^{2}}\varphi\lvert\partial_{r}\varphi\rvert^{2}+(1-\varphi)\Big(\varphi^{2}\lvert\partial_{r}\overline{e}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\partial_{r}\varphi\rvert^{2}\lvert\overline{e}-1\rvert^{2}\Big)\Big\}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\int_{a}^{\widetilde{r}(k,t)}\dfrac{\lvert\partial_{r}\overline{e}\rvert^{2}}{\overline{e}^{2}}r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\widetilde{r}(1,t)}^{\widetilde{r}(k,t)}\Big\{\lvert\partial_{r}\overline{e}\rvert^{2}+C(T)\lvert\overline{e}-1\rvert^{2}+C(a)k^{-\frac{2}{n}}\dfrac{\lvert\overline{e}-1\rvert^{2}}{\overline{e}^{2}}\Big\}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t.

If
k\geq\max\{C^{\frac{n}{2}}(a)C^{\frac{n}{2}}(T),M(a)\}
, then translating the above integral into
the Lagrangian coordinates and using (
5.3
) and Theorem
4.1
yield

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{a}^{\infty}\dfrac{\lvert\partial_{r}e\rvert^{2}}{e^{2}}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{k}\dfrac{\widetilde{r}^{2m}}{\widetilde{v}}\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}\widetilde{e}\rvert^{2}}{\widetilde{e}^{2}}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{1}^{k}\Big\{\dfrac{\widetilde{r}^{2m}}{\widetilde{v}}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}\widetilde{e}\rvert^{2}+C(T)\widetilde{v}\lvert\widetilde{e}-1\rvert^{2}+\dfrac{C(a)}{k^{\frac{2}{n}}}\dfrac{\widetilde{v}\lvert\widetilde{e}-1\rvert^{2}}{\widetilde{e}^{2}}\Big\}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\Big(1+\sup\big\{(\widetilde{v}+k^{-\frac{2}{n}}\dfrac{\widetilde{v}}{\widetilde{e}^{2}})(y,s)\,\vcentcolon\,(y,s)\in[1,k]\times[0,T]\big\}\Big)\leq C(T),

Since
z\to z^{-1}
is convex in
z\in(0,\infty)
and
\partial_{r}u+m\frac{u}{r}=r^{-m}\partial_{r}(r^{m}u)
,
then

\displaystyle\dfrac{\lvert\partial_{r}u+m\frac{u}{r}\rvert^{2}}{2e}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\big(\varphi\overline{e}^{-1}+(1-\varphi)\big)\big|\varphi\partial_{r}\overline{u}+\overline{u}\partial_{r}\varphi+m\varphi\frac{\overline{u}}{r}\big|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\varphi^{3}\overline{e}^{-1}\lvert r^{-m}\partial_{r}(r^{m}\overline{u})\rvert^{2}+\varphi\lvert\partial_{r}\varphi\rvert^{2}\overline{e}^{-1}\lvert\overline{u}\rvert^{2}+(1-\varphi)\big(\varphi^{2}\lvert r^{-m}\partial_{r}(r^{m}\overline{u})\rvert^{2}+\lvert\overline{u}\partial_{r}\varphi\rvert^{2}\big).

By similar arguments as above, we see that, for all
k\geq\max\{C^{\frac{n}{2}}(a)C^{\frac{n}{2}}(T),M(a)\}
,

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{a}^{\infty}\dfrac{\lvert r^{-m}\partial_{r}(r^{m}\overline{u})\rvert^{2}}{e}r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\int_{a}^{\widetilde{r}(k,t)}\dfrac{\lvert r^{-m}\partial_{r}(r^{m}\overline{u})\rvert^{2}}{\overline{e}}r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t+\dfrac{C(a)}{k^{\frac{2}{n}}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{a}^{\widetilde{r}(k,t)}\dfrac{\lvert\overline{u}\rvert^{2}}{\overline{e}}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\widetilde{r}(1,t)}^{\widetilde{r}(k,t)}\big\{\lvert r^{-m}\partial_{r}(r^{m}\overline{u})\rvert^{2}+C(T)\overline{u}^{2}\big\}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{k}\Big\{\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(\widetilde{r}^{m}\widetilde{u})\rvert^{2}}{\widetilde{v}\widetilde{e}}+\dfrac{C(a)}{k^{\frac{2}{n}}}\dfrac{\widetilde{v}}{\widetilde{e}}\lvert\widetilde{u}\rvert^{2}\Big\}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{1}^{k}\Big\{\dfrac{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(\widetilde{r}^{m}\widetilde{u})\rvert^{2}}{\widetilde{v}}+C(T)\widetilde{v}\lvert\widetilde{u}\rvert^{2}\Big\}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\leq C(T),

By the same analysis, we also see that, for all
k\geq\max\{C^{\frac{n}{2}}(a)C^{\frac{n}{2}}(T),M(a)\}
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{a}^{\infty}\dfrac{1}{e}\Big\lvert\partial_{r}u-\dfrac{u}{r}\Big\rvert^{2}r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t\leq C(T).

Set
N(a)\vcentcolon=[(\max\{C^{\frac{n}{2}}(a)C^{\frac{n}{2}}(T),M(a)\}]\in\mathbb{N}
, where
[\cdot]
is the ceiling function.
Then the proof is completed.
∎

Lemma 5.2
.

For each
a\in(0,1)
,

\begin{split}&\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{a}^{\infty}\Big\{\big|(\rho_{a,k}-1,u_{a,k}^{2},e_{a,k}-1,\sqrt{\sigma}\partial_{r}u_{a,k},\sigma\partial_{r}e_{a,k})\big|^{2}\Big\}(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\\
&\,+\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{a}^{\infty}\Big\{\big|(\partial_{r}u_{a,k},\partial_{r}e_{a,k},u_{a,k}\partial_{r}u_{a,k},\sqrt{\sigma}\partial_{t}u_{a,k},\sigma\partial_{t}e_{a,k})\big|^{2}\Big\}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t\leq C(a),\end{split}

(5.11)

and, for all
(k,r,t)\in\mathbb{N}\times[a,\infty)\times[0,T]
,

C^{-1}(a)\leq\rho_{a,k}(r,t)\leq C(a),\,\,e_{a,k}(r,t)\geq C^{-1}(a),\,\,\sigma^{\frac{1}{4}}(t)\lvert u_{a,k}(r,t)\rvert+\sigma^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)e_{a,k}(r,t)\leq C(a).

(5.12)

Moreover, for fixed
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{N}\\
a\in(0,1)\end{subarray}}\bigg\{

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{\widetilde{r}_{a,k}(\varepsilon,t)}^{\infty}\Big\{\big|(\rho_{a,k}-1,u_{a,k}^{2},e_{a,k}-1,\sqrt{\sigma}\partial_{r}u_{a,k},\sigma\partial_{r}e_{a,k})\big|^{2}\Big\}(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\bigg.

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\widetilde{r}_{a,k}(\varepsilon,t)}^{\infty}\Big\{\big|(\partial_{r}u_{a,k},\partial_{r}e_{a,k},u_{a,k}\partial_{r}u_{a,k},\sqrt{\sigma}\partial_{t}u_{a,k},\sigma\partial_{t}e_{a,k})\big|^{2}\Big\}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t\bigg\}\leq C(\varepsilon),\quad

(5.13)

and, for any
(r,t)\in[\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t),\infty)\times[0,T]
and
(a,k)\in(0,1)\times\mathbb{N}
,

\begin{split}&C^{-1}(\varepsilon)\leq\rho_{a,k}(r,t)\leq C(\varepsilon),\quad\ \sigma^{\frac{1}{4}}(t)\lvert u_{a,k}(r,t)\rvert+\sigma^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)e_{a,k}(r,t)\leq C(\varepsilon).\end{split}

(5.14)

Proof.

As before, we suppress
(a,k)
for simplicity and use the notation in (
5.9
).
Only (
5.13
)–(
5.14
) are shown, since (
5.11
)–(
5.12
)
follow in the same manner.

First, using the fact that the Jacobian is bounded from (
5.2
)
and construction (
5.5
), the integration in the Eulerian coordinates
is converted to the following in the Lagrangian coordinates:

\displaystyle\int_{\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)}^{\infty}\lvert\rho-1\rvert^{2}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\leq\int_{\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)}^{\widetilde{r}(k,t)}\lvert\overline{v}^{-1}-1\rvert^{2}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r=\int_{\varepsilon}^{k}\frac{\lvert\widetilde{v}-1\rvert^{2}}{\widetilde{v}}{\mathrm{d}}x\leq C(\varepsilon),

where Theorem
4.1
has been used in the last inequality.
By the same method, one can also obtain the corresponding estimates for
\lvert e-1\rvert^{2}
and
\lvert u\rvert^{4}
in
\eqref{temp:veHREext1}_{1}
.

Now, using (
5.3
), (
5.7
), and Theorem
4.1
, it follows that

\displaystyle\int_{\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)}^{\infty}\sigma(t)\lvert\partial_{r}u\rvert^{2}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r

\displaystyle=\int_{\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)}^{\infty}\sigma(t)\lvert\varphi\partial_{r}\overline{u}+\overline{u}\partial_{r}\varphi\rvert^{2}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)}^{\widetilde{r}(k,t)}\sigma(t)\lvert\partial_{r}\overline{u}\rvert\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r+C\int_{\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)}^{\widetilde{r}(k,t)}\lvert\overline{u}\rvert^{2}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r

\displaystyle=\int_{\varepsilon}^{k}\sigma\dfrac{\widetilde{r}^{2m}}{\widetilde{v}}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}\widetilde{u}\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x+\int_{\varepsilon}^{k}\widetilde{v}\lvert\widetilde{u}\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x\leq C(\varepsilon).

In the same way, we can also obtain the estimates for
\sigma^{2}\lvert\partial_{r}e\rvert^{2}

and
\lvert(\partial_{r}u,\partial_{r}e,u\partial_{r}u)\rvert^{2}
in (
5.13
).

By (
5.3
) and Theorem
4.1
, it follows that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)}^{\infty}\sigma^{2}(t)\lvert\partial_{t}e\rvert^{2}(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)}^{\infty}\sigma^{2}(t)\varphi^{2}(r,t)\lvert\partial_{t}\overline{e}\rvert^{2}(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)}^{\widetilde{r}(k,t)}\sigma^{2}(t)\lvert\partial_{t}\overline{e}\rvert^{2}(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\varepsilon}^{k}\sigma^{2}(t)\widetilde{v}(x,t)\Big\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{t}\widetilde{e}-\widetilde{r}^{m}\dfrac{\widetilde{u}}{\widetilde{v}}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}\widetilde{e}\Big\rvert^{2}(x,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\varepsilon}^{k}\sigma^{2}\widetilde{v}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{t}\widetilde{e}\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\varepsilon}^{k}\sigma^{\frac{3}{2}}\lvert\sigma^{\frac{1}{4}}\widetilde{u}\rvert^{2}\dfrac{\widetilde{r}^{2m}}{\widetilde{v}}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{x}\widetilde{e}\rvert^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\leq C(\varepsilon).

By the same argument, one can also obtain the estimate for
\sigma\lvert\partial_{t}u\rvert^{2}
in (
5.13
).

Next, for
(r,t)
such that
t\in[0,T]
and
r\in[\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t),\infty)\cap R_{a,k}
, there exists
(x,s)\in[\varepsilon,k]\times[0,T]
such that
(r,t)=(\widetilde{r}(x,s),s)

and the following estimates hold for
(r,t)\in R_{a,k}
:

\displaystyle\rho(r,t)

\displaystyle=\varphi(\widetilde{r}(x,t),t)\widetilde{v}^{-1}(x,t)+1-\varphi(\widetilde{r}(x,t),t)\leq C(\varepsilon)\varphi(\widetilde{r}(x,t),t)+1-\varphi(\widetilde{r}(x,t),t)\leq C(\varepsilon),

\displaystyle\rho(r,t)

\displaystyle\geq C(\varepsilon)^{-1}\varphi(\widetilde{r}(x,t),t)+1-\varphi(\widetilde{r}(x,t),t)\geq C(\varepsilon)^{-1}.

For
(r,t)
such that
t\in[0,T]
and
r\in[\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t),\infty)\times[0,T]\backslash R_{a,k}
,
then
r>\widetilde{r}(k,t)
so that
\varphi(r,t)=0
and

\rho(r,t)=\overline{v}^{-1}(r,t)\varphi(r,t)-\varphi(r,t)+1=1
,
which leads to the upper and lower bounds of the extended density.
In a similar manner, we can also obtain the bounds for
(u,e)

listed in (
5.14
).
∎

Throughout §
5.3
–§
5.7
,
we will suppress parameter
a\in(0,1)
and denote

\displaystyle(\rho_{k},u_{k},e_{k},\varphi_{k})\equiv(\rho_{a,k},u_{a,k},e_{a,k},\varphi_{a,k}),\ \ (\overline{v}_{k},\overline{u}_{k},\overline{e}_{k})\equiv(\overline{v}_{a,k},\overline{u}_{a,k},\overline{e}_{a,k}),

(5.15)

\displaystyle(\widetilde{v}_{k},\widetilde{u}_{k},\widetilde{e}_{k},\widetilde{r}_{k})\equiv(\widetilde{v}_{a,k},\widetilde{u}_{a,k},\widetilde{e}_{a,k},\widetilde{r}_{a,k})

Note that the main aim of these subsections is to prove Theorem
3.1
via the limit:
k\to\infty
.

5.3.
Construction of the particle path function

In this section, we construct the particle path function

\widetilde{r}(x,t)
.

Lemma 5.3
.

There exist both a continuous function
\widetilde{r}(x,t)\vcentcolon[0,\infty)\times[0,T]\to[a,\infty)

and a subsequence
k\to\infty
such that

(i)

for any compact subset
K\Subset[0,\infty)\times[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{k\to\infty}\sup_{(x,t)\in K}\lvert\overline{r}_{k}(x,t)-\widetilde{r}(x,t)\rvert=0,

where
\overline{r}_{k}(x,t)
is defined by

\overline{r}_{k}(x,t)=\begin{cases}\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,t)&if $(x,t)\in[0,k]\times[0,T]$,\\
x-k+\widetilde{r}_{k}(k,t)&if $(x,t)\in[k,\infty)\times[0,T]$.\end{cases}

(5.16)

(ii)

for each
\varepsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\lvert\widetilde{r}(x_{1},t)-\widetilde{r}(x_{2},t)\rvert\leq C(\varepsilon)\lvert x_{1}-x_{2}\rvert

\displaystyle\text{for all}\ (x_{1},x_{2},t)\in[\varepsilon,\infty)^{2}\times[0,T],

\displaystyle\lvert\widetilde{r}(x,t_{1})-\widetilde{r}(x,t_{2})\rvert\leq C(\varepsilon)\lvert t_{1}^{\frac{3}{4}}-t_{2}^{\frac{3}{4}}\rvert

\displaystyle\text{for all}\ (x,t_{1},t_{2})\in[\varepsilon,\infty)\times[0,T]^{2}.

(iii)

for each
t\in[0,T]
, the function
x\mapsto\widetilde{r}(x,t)
is strictly increasing and

nx\psi^{-1}_{-}(\dfrac{C_{0}}{x})\leq\widetilde{r}^{\,n}(x,t)\leq C_{0}(1+x)\qquad\text{for all}\ \ (x,t)\in[0,\infty)\times[0,T].

Proof.

For each integer
l\in\mathbb{N}
,
let
S_{l}\vcentcolon=[0,l]\times[0,T]\subset[0,\infty)\times[0,T]
.
Set
x_{1},\,x_{2},\,x\in[0,l]
and
t_{1},\,t_{2},\,t\in[0,T]
. We consider two cases:
k\geq l
or
k<l
.

Case 1:
k\geq l
. Then
\overline{r}_{k}(x_{i},t)=\widetilde{r}_{k}(x_{i},t)
for
i=1,\,2
.
It follows from Theorem
4.1
and the mean value theorem that

\lvert\overline{r}_{k}(x_{1},t)-\overline{r}_{k}(x_{2},t)\rvert\leq\lvert x_{1}-x_{2}\rvert\sup_{x\in[0,k]}|{\mathrm{D}}_{x}\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,t)|\leq\lvert x_{1}-x_{2}\rvert\sup_{x\in[0,k]}\big|\dfrac{\widetilde{v}_{k}}{\widetilde{r}_{k}^{\,m}}\big|\leq C(a)\lvert x_{1}-x_{2}\rvert.

Case 2:
k<l
. For this case, we consider three sub-cases:

If
x_{1},\,x_{2}\in[0,k]
, the same inequality as Case 1 can be obtained by repeating
the same argument.

If
x_{1},x_{2}\in[k,l]
, then, by (
5.16
),

\lvert\overline{r}_{k}(x_{1},t)-\overline{r}_{k}(x_{2},t)\rvert=\lvert x_{1}-x_{2}\rvert
.

If
x_{1}\in[0,k]
and
x_{2}\in[k,l]
, then, by (
5.16
),

\lvert\overline{r}_{k}(x_{1},t)-\overline{r}_{k}(x_{2},t)\rvert\leq\lvert\widetilde{r}_{k}(x_{1},t)-\widetilde{r}_{k}(k,t)\rvert+\lvert x_{2}-k\rvert\leq C(a)(k-x_{1})+x_{2}-k\leq C(a)\lvert x_{1}-x_{2}\rvert.

In summary, for both cases,

\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\lvert\overline{r}_{k}(x_{1},t)-\overline{r}_{k}(x_{2},t)\rvert\leq C^{\ast}(a)\lvert x_{1}-x_{2}\rvert.

(5.17)

Moreover, for both cases
k\geq l
and
k<l
, it can be shown that, if
t_{*}=\min\{1,t_{1},t_{2}\}
, then

\lvert\overline{r}_{k}(x,t_{1})-\overline{r}_{k}(x,t_{2})\rvert=\Big\lvert\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}u_{k}(x,s)\,{\mathrm{d}}s\Big\rvert\leq C(a)\Big\lvert\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}s^{-\frac{1}{4}}\,{\mathrm{d}}s\Big\rvert\leq C(a)t_{*}^{-\frac{1}{4}}\lvert t_{1}-t_{2}\rvert.

(5.18)

Similarly, by (
5.16
), Theorem
4.1
, and Lemma
4.1
,
it can be shown that, for each
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
,

\displaystyle\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\lvert\overline{r}_{k}(x_{1},t)-\overline{r}_{k}(x_{2},t)\rvert\leq C(\varepsilon)\lvert x_{1}-x_{2}\rvert

\displaystyle\text{ for all $(x_{1},x_{2},t)\in[\varepsilon,l]^{2}\times[0,T]$,}

(5.19)

\displaystyle\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\lvert\overline{r}_{k}(x,t_{1})-\overline{r}_{k}(x,t_{2})\rvert\leq C(\varepsilon)\big|t_{1}^{\frac{3}{4}}-t_{2}^{\frac{3}{4}}\big|

\displaystyle\text{ for all $(x,t_{1},t_{2})\in[\varepsilon,l]\times[0,T]^{2}$.}

Furthermore, the following bound holds for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
:

\sup_{(x,t)\in S_{l}}\overline{r}_{k}(x,t)=\sup_{(x,t)\in S_{l}}\Big(a^{n}+n\int_{0}^{x}\widetilde{v}_{k}(y,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}y\Big)^{\frac{1}{n}}\leq\big(a^{n}+nC(a)l\big)^{\frac{1}{n}}.

(5.20)

By (
5.17
)–(
5.18
) and (
5.20
), one can apply Proposition
A.1

to obtain a subsequence (still denoted)
k\to\infty

and a continuous function
\widetilde{r}(x,t)\vcentcolon[0,\infty)\times[0,T]\to[a,\infty)
such that

\lim\limits_{k\to\infty}\sup_{(x,t)\in K}\lvert\widetilde{r}(x,t)-\overline{r}_{k}(x,t)\rvert=0\qquad\text{ for all}\ K\Subset[0,\infty)\times[0,T].

(5.21)

Applying the strong convergence (
5.21
) for (
5.19
), one obtains Lemma
5.3
(iii)(ii)
.

Next, let
0<x_{1}<x_{2}<\infty
. For all
k\geq 2^{n}x_{2}C^{2}(a)+\frac{2^{n}-1}{n}C(a)
, one can verify
2\widetilde{r}_{k}(x_{1},t)\leq 2\widetilde{r}_{k}(x_{2},t)\leq\widetilde{r}_{k}(k,t)
. It follows that

\int_{\widetilde{r}_{k}(x_{1},t)}^{\widetilde{r}_{k}(x_{2},t)}\rho_{k}(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r=\int_{\widetilde{r}_{k}(x_{1},t)}^{\widetilde{r}_{k}(x_{2},t)}\overline{v}_{k}^{-1}(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r=x_{2}-x_{1}.

Since
\rho_{k}(r,t)\leq C(x_{1})
for
r\in[\widetilde{r}_{k}(x_{1},t),\infty)
from Lemma
5.2
,

\displaystyle x_{2}-x_{1}=\int_{\widetilde{r}_{k}(x_{1},t)}^{\widetilde{r}_{k}(x_{2},t)}\rho_{k}(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\leq\dfrac{C(x_{1})}{n}\big(\widetilde{r}_{k}^{\,n}(x_{1},t)-\widetilde{r}_{k}^{\,n}(x_{2},t)\big).

Taking
k\to\infty
on the above and using (
5.21
) yields

0<x_{2}-x_{1}<\dfrac{C(x_{1})}{n}\big(\widetilde{r}^{\,n}(x_{1},t)-\widetilde{r}^{\,n}(x_{2},t)\big).

This shows that
x\mapsto\widetilde{r}(x,t)
is strictly increasing for each
t\in[0,T]
.

For a fixed point
(x,t)\in[0,\infty)\times[0,T]
, it follows from Lemma
4.1
that

\displaystyle nx\psi_{-}^{-1}(\frac{C_{0}}{x})\leq\widetilde{r}_{k}^{\,n}(x,t)\leq C_{0}(1+x)\qquad\text{for all $k\geq x$}.

Taking
k\to\infty
and using (
5.21
),
we conclude Lemma
5.3
(iii)(iii)
.
∎

5.4.
Compactness results

Lemma 5.4
.

There exist both a continuous function
(u,e)(r,t)\vcentcolon[a,\infty)\times(0,T]\to{\mathbb{R}}\times[0,\infty)

and a subsequence
(
still denoted
)

\{(u_{k},e_{k})\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
such that,
for each compact subset
K\Subset[a,\infty)\times[0,T]
,

\lim\limits_{k\to\infty}\sup\limits_{(r,t)\in K}\lvert(u_{k}-u,e_{k}-e)\rvert(r,t)=0.

(5.22)

Moreover, for each fixed
\varepsilon>0
, if
t\in[0,T]
and
r_{1},\,r_{2}\geq\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)
,
then

\sigma^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)\lvert u(r_{1},t)-u(r_{2},t)\rvert+\sigma(t)\lvert e(r_{1},t)-e(r_{2},t)\rvert\leq C(\varepsilon)\lvert r_{1}-r_{2}\rvert^{\frac{1}{2}},

(5.23)

and, if
0<t_{1}<t_{2}\leq T
and
r\geq\sup_{t_{1}\leq t\leq t_{2}}\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)
, then

\sigma^{\frac{1}{2}}(t_{1})\lvert u(r,t_{1})-u(r,t_{2})\rvert+\sigma(t_{1})\left\lvert e(r,t_{1})-e(r,t_{2})\right\rvert\leq C(\varepsilon)\lvert t_{2}-t_{1}\rvert^{\frac{1}{4}}.

(5.24)

In addition,

\begin{cases}\lvert u(r,t)\rvert\leq C(a)\sigma^{-\frac{1}{4}}(t),\ C^{-1}(a)\leq e(r,t)\leq C(a)\sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)&if $(r,t)\in[a,\infty)\times[0,T]$,\\
\lvert u(r,t)\rvert\leq C(\varepsilon)\sigma^{-\frac{1}{4}}(t),\ \ e(r,t)\leq C(\varepsilon)\sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)&if $t\in[0,T]$, $r\geq\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)$.\end{cases}

(5.25)

Proof.

We give our proof only for
u(r,t)
, since the proof is the same for
e(r,t)
.

Let
(r_{1},r_{2},t)\in[a,\infty)^{2}\times(0,T]
. By
Lemma
5.2
, for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\left\lvert u_{k}(r_{2},t)-u_{k}(r_{1},t)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq(r_{2}-r_{1})^{\frac{1}{2}}r_{1}^{-\frac{m}{2}}\Big(\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}\lvert\partial_{r}u_{k}\rvert^{2}(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)a^{-\frac{m}{2}}\Big(\int_{a}^{\infty}\sigma(t)\left\lvert\partial_{r}u_{k}\right\rvert^{2}(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\lvert r_{2}-r_{1}\rvert^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(a)\sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)\lvert r_{2}-r_{1}\rvert^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Let
r\in[a,\infty)
and
0<t_{1}<t_{2}\leq T
, and set
h\vcentcolon=\sqrt{t_{2}-t_{1}}
.
By the mean value theorem,

\dfrac{1}{h}\int_{r}^{r+h}\lvert u_{k}(\zeta,t_{1})-u_{k}(\zeta,t_{2})\rvert\,{\mathrm{d}}\zeta=\lvert u_{k}(\xi,t_{1})-u_{k}(\xi,t_{2})\rvert\qquad\ \text{for some $\xi\in(r,r+h)$}.

(5.26)

By the triangle inequality and the fundamental theorem of calculus, for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\lvert u_{k}(r,t_{1})-u_{k}(r,t_{2})\rvert-\lvert u_{k}(\xi,t_{1})-u_{k}(\xi,t_{2})\rvert\leq\Big\lvert\int_{r}^{\xi}\big(\partial_{r}u_{k}(\zeta,t_{1})-\partial_{r}u_{k}(\zeta,t_{2})\big)\,{\mathrm{d}}\zeta\Big\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\left\lvert\xi-r\right\rvert^{\frac{1}{2}}r^{-\frac{m}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{2}\Big(\int_{r}^{\xi}\lvert\partial_{r}u_{k}(\zeta,t_{i})\rvert^{2}\,\zeta^{m}{\mathrm{d}}\zeta\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq a^{-\frac{m}{2}}\sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t_{1})\sqrt{h}\sum_{i=1}^{2}\Big(\int_{a}^{\infty}\sigma(t_{i})\lvert\partial_{r}u_{k}(\zeta,t_{i})\rvert^{2}\,\zeta^{m}{\mathrm{d}}\zeta\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(a)\sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t_{1})\sqrt{h}.

Thus, it follows from (
5.26
) that, for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\lvert u_{k}(r,t_{1})-u_{k}(r,t_{2})\rvert

\displaystyle\leq C(a)\sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t_{1})\sqrt{h}+\dfrac{1}{h}\int_{r}^{r+h}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\lvert\partial_{t}u_{k}(\zeta,t)\rvert\,{\mathrm{d}}t{\mathrm{d}}\zeta

\displaystyle\leq C(a)\sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t_{1})\sqrt{h}+\dfrac{\sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t_{1})a^{-\frac{m}{2}}}{h}\Big(\int_{r}^{r+h}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}{\mathrm{d}}t{\mathrm{d}}\zeta\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{a}^{\infty}\sigma(t)\lvert\partial_{t}u_{k}\rvert^{2}\,\zeta^{m}{\mathrm{d}}\zeta{\mathrm{d}}t\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(a)\sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t_{1})\sqrt{h}+C(a)\sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t_{1})h^{-\frac{1}{2}}\lvert t_{2}-t_{1}\rvert^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(a)\sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t_{1})\lvert t_{2}-t_{1}\rvert^{\frac{1}{4}}.

Therefore, we have

\begin{cases}\sup\limits_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\,\lvert u_{k}(r_{2},t)-u_{k}(r_{1},t)\rvert\leq C(a)\sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)\lvert r_{2}-r_{1}\rvert^{\frac{1}{2}}&\quad if $(r_{1},r_{2},t)\in[a,\infty)^{2}\times(0,T]$,\\
\sup\limits_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\,\lvert u_{k}(r,t_{2})-u_{k}(r,t_{1})\rvert\leq C(a)\sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t_{*})\lvert t_{2}-t_{1}\rvert^{\frac{1}{4}}&\quad if $(r,t_{1},t_{2})\in[a,\infty)\times(0,T]^{2}$,\end{cases}

(5.27)

where
t_{*}\equiv\min\{t_{1},t_{2}\}
. Let
S_{l}=[0,\infty)\times[l^{-1},T]
for each
l\in\mathbb{N}
.
Then we can apply Proposition
A.1
with (
5.27
) and
S_{l}

to obtain a continuous function
u(r,t)\vcentcolon[a,\infty)\times(0,T]\to{\mathbb{R}}
and a subsequence (still denoted)
\{u_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
such that (
5.22
) holds.

Next, let
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
. Since
x\to\widetilde{r}(x,t)
is strictly increasing and
(x,t)\mapsto\widetilde{r}(x,t)
is continuous,
it follows that
d\vcentcolon=\inf_{t\in[0,T]}\{\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)-\widetilde{r}(\frac{\varepsilon}{2},t)\}>0
.
By Lemma
5.3
, there exists
N_{\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\lvert\widetilde{r}_{k}(\frac{\varepsilon}{2},t)-\widetilde{r}(\frac{\varepsilon}{2},t)\rvert\leq\frac{d}{2}

if
k\geq N_{\varepsilon}
, which implies that

\widetilde{r}_{k}(\frac{\varepsilon}{2},t)<\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)\qquad\text{for each $t\in[0,T]$ and $k\geq N_{\varepsilon}$}.

(5.28)

Let
(r_{1},r_{2},t)
be a triplet such that
t\in(0,T]
and
r_{1},\,r_{2}\in[\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t),\infty)
.
Then (
5.28
) implies that
r_{1},r_{2}>\widetilde{r}_{k}(\frac{\varepsilon}{2},t)
if
k\geq N_{\varepsilon}
.
By
Lemma
5.2
, it follows that, for all
k\geq N_{\varepsilon}
,

\displaystyle\lvert u_{k}(r_{2},t)-u_{k}(r_{1},t)\rvert\leq\dfrac{\sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)}{\widetilde{r}_{k}^{\,\frac{m}{2}}(\frac{\varepsilon}{2},t)}\Big(\int_{\widetilde{r}_{k}(\frac{\varepsilon}{2},t)}^{\infty}\sigma(t)\lvert\partial_{r}u_{k}\rvert^{2}(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\lvert r_{2}-r_{1}\rvert^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(\varepsilon)\sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)\lvert r_{2}-r_{1}\rvert^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Moreover, let
(r,t_{1},t_{2})\in[a,\infty)\times(0,T]^{2}
be a triplet such that

0<t_{1}<t_{2}\leq T
and
r\geq\sup_{t_{1}\leq t\leq t_{2}}\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)
.
Then, by (
5.28
),
r>\widetilde{r}_{k}(\frac{\varepsilon}{2},t)\geq C^{-1}(\varepsilon)
for all
t\in[0,T]
if
k\geq N_{\varepsilon}
.
Set
h\vcentcolon=\sqrt{t_{2}-t_{1}}
. Then, by the mean value theorem,

\dfrac{1}{h}\int_{r}^{r+h}\lvert u_{k}(\zeta,t_{1})-u_{k}(\zeta,t_{2})\rvert\,{\mathrm{d}}\zeta=\lvert u_{k}(\xi,t_{1})-u_{k}(\xi,t_{2})\rvert\qquad\text{for some $\xi\in(r,r+h)$}.

By the triangle inequality,
for all
k\geq N_{\varepsilon}
,

\displaystyle\lvert u_{k}(r,t_{1})-u_{k}(r,t_{2})\rvert-\lvert u_{k}(\xi,t_{1})-u_{k}(\xi,t_{2})\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\lvert\xi-r\rvert}r^{-\frac{m}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{2}\Big(\int_{r}^{\xi}\lvert\partial_{r}u_{k}(\zeta,t_{i})\rvert^{2}\,\zeta^{m}{\mathrm{d}}\zeta\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\varepsilon)\sqrt{h}\sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t_{1})\sum_{i=1}^{2}\Big(\int_{\widetilde{r}_{k}(\frac{\varepsilon}{2},t_{i})}^{\infty}\sigma(t_{i})\lvert\partial_{r}u_{k}(\zeta,t_{i})\rvert^{2}\zeta^{m}{\mathrm{d}}\zeta\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(\varepsilon)\sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t_{1})\sqrt{h}

It follows that, for all
k\geq N_{\varepsilon}
,

\displaystyle\lvert u_{k}(r,t_{1})-u_{k}(r,t_{2})\rvert

\displaystyle\leq C(\varepsilon)\sqrt{h}\sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t_{1})+\dfrac{1}{h}\int_{r}^{r+h}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\lvert\partial_{t}u_{k}(\zeta,t)\rvert\,{\mathrm{d}}t{\mathrm{d}}\zeta

\displaystyle\leq C(\varepsilon)\sqrt{h}\sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t_{1})+\dfrac{\sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t_{1})r^{-\frac{m}{2}}}{h}\Big(\int_{r}^{r+h}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}{\mathrm{d}}t{\mathrm{d}}\zeta\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\widetilde{r}_{k}(\frac{\varepsilon}{2},t)}^{\infty}\sigma\lvert\partial_{t}u_{k}\rvert^{2}\,\zeta^{m}{\mathrm{d}}\zeta{\mathrm{d}}t\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\varepsilon)\sqrt{h}\sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t_{1})+C(\varepsilon)h^{-\frac{1}{2}}\lvert t_{2}-t_{1}\rvert^{\frac{1}{2}}\sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t_{1})\leq C(\varepsilon)\sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t_{1})\lvert t_{2}-t_{1}\rvert^{\frac{1}{4}}.

Then

\begin{cases}\sup\limits_{k\geq N_{\varepsilon}}\lvert u_{k}(r_{2},t)-u_{k}(r_{1},t)\rvert\leq C(\varepsilon)t^{-\frac{1}{2}}\lvert r_{2}-r_{1}\rvert^{\frac{1}{2}}&if $t\in(0,T]$, $r_{1},r_{2}\geq\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)$,\\
\sup\limits_{k\geq N_{\varepsilon}}\lvert u_{k}(r,t_{2})-u_{k}(r,t_{1})\rvert\leq C(\varepsilon)\sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t_{*})\lvert t_{2}-t_{1}\rvert^{\frac{1}{4}}&if $0<t_{1}<t_{2}\leq T$, $r\geq R(\varepsilon,t_{1},t_{2})$,\end{cases}

where
R(\varepsilon,t_{1},t_{2})\vcentcolon=\sup_{t_{1}\leq t\leq t_{2}}\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)
.
Taking
k\to\infty
and using (
5.22
), we obtain the continuity estimates for
u
in (
5.23
)–(
5.24
).

Finally, Lemma
5.2
and (
5.22
) lead to
\eqref{temp:klimue3}_{1}
.
In addition, for each
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
, (
5.28
) implies that
r\geq\widetilde{r}_{k}(\frac{\varepsilon}{2},t)

if
r\geq\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)
and
k\geq N_{\varepsilon}
.
Hence,
\left\lvert u_{k}(r,t)\right\rvert\leq C(\varepsilon)\sigma^{-\frac{1}{4}}(t)
for all
(r,t,k)
such that
k\geq N_{\varepsilon}
,
t\in[0,T]
,
and
r\geq\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)
.
Letting
k\to\infty
and using (
5.22
), we obtain
\eqref{temp:klimue3}_{2}
.
∎

Proposition 5.1
.

Let
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
, and let
(r,t)\in[a,\infty)\times(0,T]
be such that
r>\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)
.
Denote
{\mathrm{D}}_{\delta}(r,t)\vcentcolon=\{(\zeta,s)\vcentcolon\sqrt{(\zeta-r)^{2}+(s-t)^{2}}<\delta\}
.
Then there exist
N(\varepsilon,r,t)\in\mathbb{N}
and
\delta(\varepsilon,r,t)>0
such that,
for all
\delta\in(0,\delta(\varepsilon,r,t))
and
k\geq N(\varepsilon,r,t)
,

{\mathrm{D}}_{\delta}(r,t)\subset\{(\zeta,s)\in[a,\infty)\times[0,T]\,\vcentcolon\,\zeta>\widetilde{r}_{k}(\varepsilon,s)\}.

Proof.

Let
\eta\vcentcolon=r-\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)>0
.
Since
(\zeta,s)\mapsto\zeta-\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,s)
is continuous,
there exists
\delta(\varepsilon,r,t)>0
such that, for all
\delta\leq\delta(\varepsilon,r,t)
,
if
(\zeta,s)\in{\mathrm{D}}_{\delta}(r,t)
, then

\lvert\zeta-\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,s)-r+\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)\rvert<\frac{\eta}{2}
which implies that

\zeta>\frac{\eta}{2}+\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,s)
.
Therefore,

{\mathrm{D}}_{\delta}(r,t)\subset\{(\zeta,s)\in[a,\infty)\times[0,T]\,\vcentcolon\,\zeta>\frac{\eta}{2}+\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,s)\}.

(5.29)

By Lemma
5.3
,
\lim_{k\to\infty}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\lvert\widetilde{r}_{k}(\varepsilon,s)-\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,s)\rvert=0
.
Thus, there exists
N(\varepsilon,r,t)\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,s)+\frac{\eta}{4}>\widetilde{r}_{k}(\varepsilon,s)
for all
s\in[0,T]
and
k\geq N(\varepsilon,r,t)
.
If
(\zeta,s)
is such that
\zeta>\frac{\eta}{2}+\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,s)
,
then
\zeta>\frac{\eta}{4}+\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,s)>\widetilde{r}_{k}(\varepsilon,s)
for all
k\geq N(\varepsilon,r,t)
.
We combining this with (
5.29
) to conclude the proof.
∎

Lemma 5.5
.

There exist
\rho\geq 0

and a subsequence
(
still denoted
)

\{\rho_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
such that

(\rho_{k}-1)\overset{\ast}{\rightharpoonup}(\rho-1)\qquad\text{in $L^{\infty}\big(0,T;L^{2}([a,\infty),r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)\big)$}.

(5.30)

Moreover, for each
\varepsilon>0
,

\begin{cases}C^{-1}(\varepsilon)\leq\rho(r,t)\leq C(\varepsilon)&\,\, for a.e. $(r,t)\in\{(\zeta,s)\in[a,\infty)\times[0,T]\,\vcentcolon\,\zeta\geq\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)\}$,\\
C^{-1}(a)\leq\rho(r,t)\leq C(a)&\,\, for a.e. $(r,t)\in[a,\infty)\times[0,T]$.\end{cases}

(5.31)

Proof.

From Lemma
5.2
,

\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{a}^{\infty}\lvert\rho_{k}-1\rvert^{2}(r,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t\leq a^{-m}\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{a}^{\infty}\lvert\rho_{k}-1\rvert^{2}(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t\leq C(a).

(5.32)

Thus there exist
(f,\rho-1)\in L^{\infty}\big(0,T;L^{2}([a,\infty),{\mathrm{d}}r)\big)
and a subsequence (still denoted)
\{\rho_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
such that, as
k\to\infty
,

\rho_{k}-1\overset{\ast}{\rightharpoonup}(\rho-1),\quad r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\rho_{k}-1)\overset{\ast}{\rightharpoonup}f\qquad\,\text{ in $L^{\infty}\big(0,T;L^{2}([a,\infty),{\mathrm{d}}r)\big)$}.

(5.33)

Let
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{\rm c}([a,\infty)\times[0,T])
.
It follows from (
5.33
) that, as
k\to\infty
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{a}^{\infty}f\phi\,{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t\leftarrow\int_{0}^{T}\int_{a}^{\infty}r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\rho_{k}-1)\phi\,{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t\to\int_{0}^{T}\int_{a}^{\infty}(\rho-1)r^{\frac{m}{2}}\phi\,{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t.

By the fundamental lemma of calculus,

f(r,t)=r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\rho(r,t)-1)
for
a.e.

(r,t)\in[a,\infty)\times[0,T]
.
By (
5.33
) again, the weak-star convergence (
5.30
) is obtained.

Next, fix
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
and a point
(r,t)\in[a,\infty)\times(0,T]
such that
r>\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)
.
Let
\delta(\varepsilon,r,t)>0
and
N(\varepsilon,r,t)\in\mathbb{N}
be obtained in Proposition
5.1
.
For
0<\delta\leq\delta(\varepsilon,r,t)
, set

\phi_{\delta}^{(r,t)}(\zeta,s)\vcentcolon=\dfrac{\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathrm{D}}_{\delta}(r,t)}(\zeta,s)}{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{\delta}(r,t)\rvert}=\begin{cases}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{\delta}(r,t)\rvert^{-1}&if $(\zeta,s)\in{\mathrm{D}}_{\delta}(r,t)$,\\
0&if $(\zeta,s)\in[a,\infty)\times[0,T]\backslash{\mathrm{D}}_{\delta}(r,t)$,\end{cases}

(5.34)

where
{\mathrm{D}}_{\delta}(r,t)
is defined in Proposition
5.1
. It follows that

\phi_{\delta}^{(r,t)}\in L^{1}\big(0,T;L^{2}([a,\infty),{\mathrm{d}}r)\big)
.
Moreover, by Proposition
5.1
, for all
k\geq N(\varepsilon,r,t)
and
\delta\leq\delta(\varepsilon,r,t)
,

{\mathrm{supp}}(\phi_{\delta}^{(r,t)})\subset\{(\zeta,s)\in[a,\infty)\times(0,T]\,\vcentcolon\,\zeta>\widetilde{r}_{k}(\varepsilon,s)\}.

(5.35)

For
0<\delta\leq\delta(\varepsilon,r,t)
and
\eta>0
,
there exists
N_{\eta,\delta}\in\mathbb{N}
by (
5.33
) such that, for all
k\geq N_{\eta,\delta}
,

-\eta+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{a}^{\infty}\rho_{k}\phi_{\delta}^{(r,t)}{\mathrm{d}}\zeta{\mathrm{d}}s\leq\int_{0}^{T}\int_{a}^{\infty}\rho\phi_{\delta}^{(r,t)}{\mathrm{d}}\zeta{\mathrm{d}}s\leq\eta+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{a}^{\infty}\rho_{k}\phi_{\delta}^{(r,t)}{\mathrm{d}}\zeta{\mathrm{d}}t.

(5.36)

Let
N_{1}\vcentcolon=\max\{N_{\eta,\delta},N(\varepsilon,r,t)\}
. Then, by (
5.35
) and Lemma
5.2
,
it follows that, for all
k\geq N_{1}
,
C^{-1}(\varepsilon)\leq\rho_{k}(\zeta,s)\leq C(\varepsilon)
if
(\zeta,s)\in{\mathrm{supp}}(\phi_{\delta}^{(r,t)})
.
Using this in (
5.36
) yields

\displaystyle C^{-1}(\varepsilon)-\eta\leq\dfrac{1}{\lvert{\mathrm{D}}_{\delta}(r,t)\rvert}\iint_{{\mathrm{D}}_{\delta}(r,t)}\rho(\zeta,s)\,{\mathrm{d}}\zeta{\mathrm{d}}s\leq C(\varepsilon)+\eta\qquad\text{for $\eta>0$ and $\delta\in(0,\delta(\varepsilon,r,t)]$}.

By the Lebesgue differentiation theorem over
\delta
and the fact that
\eta>0
is arbitrarily chosen,

\eqref{temp:rhoWeak}_{1}
is proved.

Finally, we consider the proof for
\eqref{temp:rhoWeak}_{2}
. For
(r,t)\in[a,\infty)\times[0,T]
and
\delta>0
,
we use
\phi_{\delta}^{(r,t)}
defined in (
5.34
).
Then
\eqref{temp:rhoWeak}_{2}
can be proved by repeating the same argument as before
and using
C^{-1}(a)\leq\rho_{k}(r,t)\leq C(a)
for all
(k,r,t)\in\mathbb{N}\times[a,\infty)\times[0,T]

from Lemma
5.2
.
∎

We now introduce the following function spaces: for each connected interval
I\subseteq[0,\infty)
, define the space
\widetilde{H}_{0}^{1}(I,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)
to be the closure of

\mathcal{D}_{0}(I)\vcentcolon=\{\phi\in C^{\infty}(I)\,\vcentcolon\,\exists N>0\ \text{such that}\ [0,N]\subset I\ \text{and}\ \phi(r)=0\ \text{for}\ r\in I\cap[N,\infty)\}

(5.37)

via the
H^{1}(I,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)
–norm. We also denote
\widetilde{H}^{-1}(I,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)
as its dual space.

Lemma 5.6
.

Let
(\rho,u,e)
be the limit function obtained in
Lemma
5.4
–
5.5
.
Then

(i)

For all
L\in\mathbb{N}
,
\rho\in C^{0}\big([0,T];\widetilde{H}^{-1}([a,L],r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)\big)
and
there exists a further subsequence
(
still denoted
)

\rho_{k}(r,t)
such that

\lim\limits_{k\to\infty}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\lVert\rho_{k}(\cdot,t)-\rho(\cdot,t)\rVert_{\widetilde{H}^{-1}([a,L],r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)}=0.

(ii)

For each
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
, define
\rho^{(\varepsilon)}(r,t)\vcentcolon=\rho(r,t)\chi_{\varepsilon}(r,t)
with
\chi_{\varepsilon}(r,t)

given by

\chi_{\varepsilon}(r,t)\vcentcolon=\begin{cases}1&if $t\in[0,T]$ and $r\in[\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t),\infty)$,\\
0&otherwise.\end{cases}

(5.38)

Then, for all
L\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\rho^{(\varepsilon)}\in C^{0}\big([0,T];\widetilde{H}^{-1}([0,L],r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)\big),\\
\lVert\rho^{(\varepsilon)}(\cdot,t_{1})-\rho^{(\varepsilon)}(\cdot,t_{2})\rVert_{\widetilde{H}^{-1}([0,L],r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)}\leq C(\varepsilon)\lvert t_{1}-t_{2}\rvert\qquad\text{for $t_{1},t_{2}\in[0,T]$}.\end{cases}

Proof.

Let
\phi\in\widetilde{H}^{1}_{0}([a,\infty),r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)
.
Then, by (
3.1
) and (
5.3
)–(
5.7
),

\displaystyle\int_{a}^{\infty}\big\{\rho_{k}(r,t_{2})-\rho_{k}(r,t_{1})\big\}\phi(r)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r=\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{a}^{\infty}\partial_{t}\rho_{k}(r,t)\phi(r)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle=\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{a}^{\infty}(\varphi_{k}\partial_{t}\overline{v}_{k}^{-1})(r,t)\phi(r)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t=\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{a}^{\widetilde{r}_{k}(k,t)}(\varphi_{k}\partial_{t}\overline{v}_{k}^{-1})(r,t)\phi(r)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle=-\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{0}^{k}(\varphi_{k}\,\phi)(\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,t),t)\big(\widetilde{v}_{k}^{-1}{\mathrm{D}}_{t}\widetilde{v}_{k}+\widetilde{r}_{k}^{m}\widetilde{u}_{k}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}\widetilde{v}_{k}^{-1}\big)(x,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t.

Integrating by parts and applying the continuity equation
\eqref{eqs:LFNS-k}_{1}
, the boundary condition

\widetilde{u}_{k}(0,t)=\widetilde{u}_{k}(k,t)
, and Theorem
4.1
(iii)(i)
–
(iii)(ii)
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{a}^{\infty}\big(\rho_{k}(r,t_{2})-\rho_{k}(r,t_{1})\big)\phi(r)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r

\displaystyle=\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{0}^{k}\widetilde{u}_{k}(x,t)\big(\partial_{r}\varphi_{k}\,\phi+\partial_{r}\phi\,\varphi_{k}\big)(\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,t),t)\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\Big(\int_{0}^{k}\dfrac{\lvert\widetilde{u}_{k}\rvert^{2}}{\widetilde{v}_{k}}{\mathrm{d}}x\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\int_{0}^{k}\widetilde{v}_{k}(x,t)\big(\lvert\phi\rvert^{2}+\lvert\partial_{r}\phi\rvert^{2}\big)(\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,t))\,{\mathrm{d}}x\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\leq C(a)\left\lVert\phi(\cdot)\right\rVert_{H^{1}([a,\infty),r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)}\lvert t_{2}-t_{1}\rvert.

Thus, the following equicontinuity inequality holds:

\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\lVert\rho_{k}(\cdot,t_{2})-\rho_{k}(\cdot,t_{1})\rVert_{\widetilde{H}^{-1}([a,\infty),r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)}\leq C(a)\lvert t_{2}-t_{1}\rvert.

By the same calculation,
\rho_{k}\in L^{\infty}(0,T;\widetilde{H}^{-1}([a,L],r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r))
for all
(k,L)\in\mathbb{N}^{2}
.
In addition, it follows from Lemma
5.2
that

\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{a}^{\infty}\lvert\rho_{k}-1\rvert^{2}(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\leq C(a).

Then we can apply Proposition
A.2

to obtain
f
such that, for all
L\in\mathbb{N}
,

\begin{cases}f\in C^{0}\big([0,T];\widetilde{H}^{-1}([a,L],r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)\big),\ \ f-1\in L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}([a,\infty),r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)),\\
\lim\limits_{k\to\infty}\sup\limits_{t\in[0,T]}\lVert\rho_{k}(\cdot,t)-f(\cdot,t)\rVert_{\widetilde{H}^{-1}([a,L],r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)}=0,\\
\lVert f(\cdot,t_{1})-f(\cdot,t_{2})\rVert_{\widetilde{H}^{-1}([a,L],r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)}\leq C(a)\lvert t_{1}-t_{2}\rvert\qquad\text{for all $t_{1},\,t_{2}\in[0,T]$.}\end{cases}

By Lemma
5.5
and the fundamental lemma of calculus, we see that
\rho=f
for
a.e.

(r,t)\in[a,\infty)\times[0,T]
.
This completes the proof of
(iii)(i)
.

For each
\varepsilon>0
and
k\in\mathbb{N}
, define
\rho_{k}^{(\varepsilon)}(r,t)\vcentcolon=\rho_{k}(r,t)\chi_{\varepsilon}^{k}(r,t)

with

\chi_{\varepsilon}^{k}(r,t)\vcentcolon=\begin{cases}1&if $t\in[0,T]$ and $r\in[\widetilde{r}_{k}(\varepsilon,t),\infty)$,\\
0&otherwise.\end{cases}

(5.39)

If
k\geq M(\varepsilon,a)\vcentcolon=\varepsilon C^{2}(a)+\frac{2^{n}-1}{n}a^{n}C(a)>0
,
then it follows from construction (
5.5
) that
\varphi_{k}(\widetilde{r}_{k}(\varepsilon,t),t)=1
for all
t\in[0,T]
.
Thus, by (
3.1
) and (
5.4
),

(\rho_{k},u_{k},e_{k})(\widetilde{r}_{k}(\varepsilon,t),t)=(\overline{v}_{k}^{-1},\overline{u}_{k},\overline{e}_{k})(\widetilde{r}_{k}(\varepsilon,t),t)=(\widetilde{v}_{k}^{-1},\widetilde{u}_{k},\widetilde{e}_{k})(\varepsilon,t).

(5.40)

Let
\phi\in\widetilde{H}^{1}_{0}([0,\infty),r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)
. Then, by (
5.40
) and the Leibniz rule,
for all
k\geq M(\varepsilon,a)
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\big(\rho_{k}^{(\varepsilon)}(r,t_{2})-\rho_{k}^{(\varepsilon)}(r,t_{1})\big)\phi(r)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r

\displaystyle=\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\widetilde{r}_{k}(\varepsilon,t)}^{\infty}\phi\partial_{t}\rho_{k}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t-\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\widetilde{r}_{k}^{\,m}(\varepsilon,t)\phi(\widetilde{r}_{k}(\varepsilon,t))\widetilde{u}_{k}(\varepsilon,t)\widetilde{v}_{k}^{-1}(\varepsilon,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle=\vcentcolon I_{1}+I_{2}.

(5.41)

Using (
3.1
) and (
5.3
)–(
5.4
),
I_{1}
can be written as

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle=\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\widetilde{r}_{k}(\varepsilon,t)}^{\widetilde{r}_{k}(k,t)}\varphi_{k}\partial_{t}\overline{v}_{k}^{-1}(r,t)\phi(r)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle=\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\varepsilon}^{k}\big(\varphi_{k}\,\phi\big)(\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,t),t)\big\{\widetilde{v}_{k}{\mathrm{D}}_{t}\widetilde{v}_{k}^{-1}-\widetilde{r}_{k}^{m}\widetilde{u}_{k}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}\widetilde{v}_{k}^{-1}\big\}(x,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t.

Integrating by parts and using the boundary condition
\widetilde{u}_{k}(k,t)=0
, the continuity equation
\eqref{eqs:LFNS-k}_{1}
, and Theorem
4.1
(iii)(i)
–
(iii)(ii)
,
it follows that

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle=\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\varepsilon}^{k}(\varphi_{k}\cdot\phi)(\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,t),t)\big\{-\widetilde{v}_{k}^{-1}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(\widetilde{r}_{k}^{m}\widetilde{u}_{k})-\widetilde{r}_{k}^{m}\widetilde{u}_{k}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}\widetilde{v}_{k}^{-1}\big\}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle=\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\phi(\widetilde{r}_{k}(\varepsilon,t))(\widetilde{r}_{k}^{m}\widetilde{u}_{k}\widetilde{v}_{k}^{-1})(\varepsilon,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}t+\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\varepsilon}^{k}\widetilde{u}_{k}(x,t)\big(\partial_{r}\varphi_{k}\,\phi+\partial_{r}\phi\,\varphi_{k}\big)(\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,t),t)\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\leq-I_{2}+C\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\Big(\int_{\varepsilon}^{k}\dfrac{\lvert\widetilde{u}_{k}\rvert^{2}}{\widetilde{v}_{k}}{\mathrm{d}}x\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\int_{\varepsilon}^{k}\widetilde{v}_{k}(x,t)\big(\lvert\phi\rvert^{2}+\lvert\partial\phi\rvert^{2}\big)(\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,t))\,{\mathrm{d}}x\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\leq-I_{2}+C(\varepsilon)\lVert\phi\rVert_{H^{1}([0,\infty),r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)}\lvert t_{2}-t_{1}\rvert.

Substituting this back into (
5.4
), it follows that
the following equicontinuity inequality holds:

\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\lVert\rho_{k}^{(\varepsilon)}(\cdot,t_{2})-\rho_{k}^{(\varepsilon)}(\cdot,t_{1})\rVert_{\widetilde{H}^{-1}([0,\infty),r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)}\leq C(\varepsilon)\lvert t_{2}-t_{1}\rvert.

(5.42)

Let
L\in\mathbb{N}
be such that
\widetilde{r}_{k}(1,t)<L
for all
(k,t)\in\mathbb{N}\times[0,T]
.
By the same derivation,
\rho_{k}^{(\varepsilon)}\in L^{\infty}(0,T;\widetilde{H}^{-1}([0,L],r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r))
for all
(k,L)\in\mathbb{N}
. Moreover, set
r_{\varepsilon}=\frac{1}{2}\inf_{t\in[0,T]}\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)>0
.
Then, by Lemma
5.3
, there exists
N_{\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{N}
such that
r_{\varepsilon}<\widetilde{r}_{k}(\varepsilon,t)

for all
k\geq N_{\varepsilon}
and
t\in[0,T]
. From (
5.42
) and Lemma
5.2
, it follows that

\begin{cases}\sup_{k\geq N_{\varepsilon}}\sup\limits_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{r_{\varepsilon}}^{\infty}\lvert\rho^{(\varepsilon)}_{k}-1\rvert^{2}(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\leq C(\varepsilon),\\
\sup_{k\geq N_{\varepsilon}}\lVert\rho_{k}^{(\varepsilon)}(\cdot,t_{2})-\rho_{k}^{(\varepsilon)}(\cdot,t_{1})\rVert_{\widetilde{H}^{-1}([r_{\varepsilon},L],r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)}\leq C(\varepsilon)\lvert t_{2}-t_{1}\rvert.\end{cases}

Thus, we can apply Proposition
A.2

to obtain
f_{\varepsilon}

such that, for all
L\in\mathbb{N}
,

\begin{cases}f_{\varepsilon}\in C^{0}\big([0,T];\widetilde{H}^{-1}([r_{\varepsilon},L],r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)\big),\ \ f_{\varepsilon}-1\in L^{\infty}\big(0,T;L^{2}([r_{\varepsilon},\infty),r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)\big),\\
\lim\limits_{k\to\infty}\sup\limits_{t\in[0,T]}\lVert\rho_{k}^{(\varepsilon)}(\cdot,t)-f_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,t)\rVert_{\widetilde{H}^{-1}([r_{\varepsilon},L],r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)}=0,\\
\lVert f_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,t_{1})-f_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,t_{2})\rVert_{\widetilde{H}^{-1}([r_{\varepsilon},L],r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)}\leq C(\varepsilon)\lvert t_{1}-t_{2}\rvert\qquad\text{for all $t_{1},\,t_{2}\in[0,T]$.}\end{cases}

(5.43)

Let
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}([a,\infty)\times[0,T])
. Then

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{r_{\varepsilon}}^{\infty}\phi\big(\rho^{(\varepsilon)}-f_{\varepsilon}\big)\,{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{r_{\varepsilon}}^{\infty}\phi\chi_{\varepsilon}(\rho-\rho_{k})\,{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{r_{\varepsilon}}^{\infty}\phi\rho_{k}(\chi_{\varepsilon}-\chi_{\varepsilon}^{k})\,{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{r_{\varepsilon}}^{\infty}\phi(\rho_{k}^{(\varepsilon)}-f_{\varepsilon})\,{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle=:\sum_{i=1}^{3}\text{II}_{i}^{k}.

By Lemma
5.5
,
\text{II}_{1}^{k}\to 0
as
k\to\infty
.
By (
5.38
)–(
5.39
) and Lemma
5.3
,

\chi_{\varepsilon}^{k}\to\chi_{\varepsilon}

a.e.
as
k\to\infty
.
Since
\rho_{k}\leq C(a)
from Lemma
5.2
,
by the dominated convergence theorem,

\text{II}_{2}^{k}\to 0
as
k\to\infty
.
Finally,
\text{II}_{3}^{k}\to 0
as
k\to\infty
by (
5.43
).
Therefore,

\rho^{(\varepsilon)}=f_{\varepsilon}
for
a.e.

(r,t)\in[r_{\varepsilon},\infty)\times[0,T]
.
Since
\rho^{(\varepsilon)}=\rho^{(\varepsilon)}_{k}=0
for
r\in[0,r_{\varepsilon}]
and
k\geq N_{\varepsilon}
,
Lemma
5.6
(iii)(ii)
is proved.
∎

Lemma 5.7
.

The following relations hold
:

\displaystyle x=\int_{a}^{\widetilde{r}(x,t)}\rho(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r

\displaystyle\text{ for a.e. $t\in[0,T]$ and for each $x>0$,}

(5.44)

\displaystyle\widetilde{r}(x,t)=\widetilde{r}_{a}^{0}(x)+\int_{0}^{t}u(\widetilde{r}(x,s),s)\,{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\text{ for all $(x,t)\in[0,\infty)\times[0,T]$,}

where the initial data
\widetilde{r}_{a}^{0}(x)
is the function constructed in
§
3.4
,
which satisfies

x=\int_{a}^{\widetilde{r}_{a}^{0}(x)}\rho_{a}^{0}(r)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\qquad\text{for all $x\in[0,\infty)$}.

Proof.

Let
(x,t)\in[0,\infty)\times[0,T]
. Then, for
k\geq M(a,x)\vcentcolon=\frac{2^{n}-1}{n}C(a)a^{n}+2^{n}C(a)^{2}x
,

2\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,t)\leq\widetilde{r}_{k}(k,t)
.
Thus, by (
3.1
), if
k\geq M(a,x)
and
r\in[a,\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,t)]
, then
\rho_{k}(r,t)=\varphi_{k}\overline{v}_{k}^{-1}(r,t)+(1-\varphi_{k}(r,t))=\overline{v}_{k}^{-1}(r,t)
.
Using this and (
5.2
)–(
5.4
), we have

\int_{a}^{\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,t)}\rho_{k}(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r=\int_{a}^{\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,t)}\overline{v}_{k}^{-1}(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r=\int_{0}^{x}\widetilde{v}_{k}^{-1}(y,t)\widetilde{v}_{k}(y,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}y=x.

Using the above identity leads to

\displaystyle x-\int_{a}^{\widetilde{r}(x,t)}\rho(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r=\int_{\widetilde{r}(x,t)}^{\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,t)}\rho_{k}(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r+\int_{a}^{\widetilde{r}(x,t)}(\rho_{k}-\rho)(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r.

Let
\chi(t)\in C^{\infty}_{c}([0,T])
. Multiplying it to the above and integrating in time,
it follows that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\Big(x-\int_{a}^{\widetilde{r}(x,t)}\rho(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\Big)\chi(t){\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\widetilde{r}(x,t)}^{\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,t)}\rho_{k}(r,t)\chi(t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{a}^{\widetilde{r}(x,t)}(\rho_{k}-\rho)(r,t)\chi(t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle=:I_{k}^{(1)}+I_{k}^{(2)}.

I_{k}^{(1)}
is estimated by using Lemma
5.3
and the upper bound
\rho_{k}\leq C(a)

from Lemma
5.2
:

\displaystyle\lvert I_{k}^{(1)}\rvert\leq\dfrac{C(a)T}{n}\lVert\chi(\cdot)\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\lvert\widetilde{r}_{k}^{n}(x,t)-\widetilde{r}^{n}(x,t)\rvert\to 0\qquad\text{as $k\to\infty$}.

For
I_{k}^{(2)}
, observe that

\chi(t)r^{m}\mathbbm{1}_{[a,\widetilde{r}(x,t)]}(r)\in L^{1}(0,T;L^{2}([a,\infty),{\mathrm{d}}r))
, where
\mathbbm{1}_{E}(r)
denotes the indicator function for spatial set
E\subset[a,\infty)
.
By Lemma
5.5
, it follows that

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{k\to\infty}I_{k}^{(2)}=\lim\limits_{k\to\infty}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{a}^{\infty}(\rho_{k}-\rho)(r,t)\chi(t)\mathbbm{1}_{[a,\widetilde{r}(x,t)]}(r)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t=0.

Since
|I_{k}^{(1)}|+|I_{k}^{(2)}|\to 0
as
k\to\infty
, then

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\Big(x-\int_{a}^{\widetilde{r}(x,t)}\rho(r,t)r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\Big)\chi(t)\,{\mathrm{d}}t=0\qquad\text{for each $\chi(t)\in C_{c}^{\infty}([0,T])$}

so that
\eqref{temp:relation}_{1}
follows.

Next, by
\eqref{eqs:LFNS-k}_{1}
and
\eqref{eqs:LFNS-k}_{4}
,
\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,t)
satisfies

\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,t)=\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,0)+\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{u}_{k}(x,s)\,{\mathrm{d}}s\qquad\text{ for $(x,t)\in[0,k]\times[0,T]$},

where, from
\eqref{eqs:LFNS-k}_{4}
and §
3.4
,
\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,0)
is given by

\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,0)=\Big(a^{n}+n\int_{0}^{x}\widetilde{v}_{a,k}^{0}(y)\,{\mathrm{d}}y\Big)^{\frac{1}{n}}=\Big(a^{n}+n\int_{0}^{x}\dfrac{1}{\rho_{a,k}^{0}(\widetilde{r}_{a}^{0}(y))}{\mathrm{d}}y\Big)^{\frac{1}{n}}.

Fix
(x,t)\in[0,\infty)\times[0,T]
. If
k\geq M(a,x)
, then, by (
3.1
),

u_{k}(\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,t),t)=\widetilde{u}_{k}(x,t)
and

\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,t)=\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,0)+\int_{0}^{t}u_{k}(\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,s),s)\,{\mathrm{d}}s.

(5.45)

Moreover, by Proposition
3.2
in §
3.4
,
if
k\geq N(x)\vcentcolon=\frac{2^{n}-1}{n}C_{0}a^{n}+2^{n}C_{0}^{2}x
, then

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{2}\widetilde{r}_{a}^{0}(k)=

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{2}\Big(a^{n}+n\int_{0}^{k}\dfrac{1}{\rho_{a,k}^{0}(\widetilde{r}_{a}^{0}(y))}{\mathrm{d}}y\Big)^{\frac{1}{n}}\geq\dfrac{1}{2}\big(a^{n}+nC_{0}^{-1}k\big)^{\frac{1}{n}}

\displaystyle\geq(a^{n}+nC_{0}x)^{\frac{1}{n}}\geq\Big(a^{n}+n\int_{0}^{x}\dfrac{1}{\rho_{a}^{0}(\widetilde{r}_{a}^{0}(y))}{\mathrm{d}}y\Big)^{\frac{1}{n}}=\widetilde{r}_{a}^{0}(x).

By the construction of
\rho_{k}^{0}(r)
in (
3.12
),
it follows that, if
k\geq N(x)
, then

\displaystyle\widetilde{v}_{a,k}^{0}(x)=\dfrac{1}{\rho_{a,k}^{0}(\widetilde{r}_{a}^{0}(x))}=\Big(\rho_{a}^{0}(\widetilde{r}_{a}^{0}(x))\varphi_{a,k}^{0}(\widetilde{r}_{a}^{0}(x))+1-\varphi_{a,k}^{0}(\widetilde{r}_{a}^{0}(x))\Big)^{-1}=\dfrac{1}{\rho_{a}^{0}(\widetilde{r}_{a}^{0}(x))}.

Thus, by Proposition
3.2
, for all
k\geq N(x)
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,0)=\Big(a^{n}+n\int_{0}^{x}\dfrac{1}{\rho_{a,k}^{0}(\widetilde{r}_{a}^{0}(y))}{\mathrm{d}}y\Big)^{\frac{1}{n}}=\Big(a^{n}+n\int_{0}^{x}\dfrac{1}{\rho_{a}^{0}(\widetilde{r}_{a}^{0}(y))}{\mathrm{d}}y\Big)^{\frac{1}{n}}=\widetilde{r}_{a}^{0}(x).

This shows that, for each
x\in[0,\infty)
,
\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,0)\to\widetilde{r}_{a}^{0}(x)
as
k\to\infty
.
Moreover, by Lemma
5.3
and inequality (
5.27
) obtained in Lemma
5.4
,
it follows that, for all
(x,s)\in[0,\infty)\times[0,T]
,

\lvert u_{k}(\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,s),s)-u_{k}(\widetilde{r}(x,s),s)\rvert\leq C(a)\lvert\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,s)-\widetilde{r}(x,s)\rvert\to 0\qquad\text{as $k\to\infty$.}

By Lemma
5.4
,
\lvert u_{k}(\widetilde{r}(x,s),s)-u(\widetilde{r}(x,s),s)\rvert\to 0
for all
(x,s)\in[0,\infty)\times[0,T]
as
k\to\infty
.
Combining these limits in (
5.45
),

\eqref{temp:relation}_{2}
is shown by the dominated convergence theorem.
∎

Lemma 5.8
.

There exists a subsequence
(
still denoted
)

(\rho_{k},u_{k},e_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
such that, as
k\to\infty
,

\begin{cases}(\sqrt{\sigma}\partial_{r}u_{k},\sigma\partial_{r}e_{k})\overset{\ast}{\rightharpoonup}(\sqrt{\sigma}\partial_{r}u,\sigma\partial_{r}e)&in $L^{\infty}\big(0,T;L^{2}([a,\infty),r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)\big)$,\\
(\partial_{r}u_{k},\partial_{r}e_{k},\sqrt{\sigma}\partial_{t}u_{k},\sigma\partial_{t}e_{k})\rightharpoonup(\partial_{r}u,\partial_{r}e,\sqrt{\sigma}\partial_{t}u,\sigma\partial_{t}e)&in $L^{2}\big(0,T;L^{2}([a,\infty),r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)\big)$,\end{cases}

where
\sigma=\min\{1,t\}
. In addition,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{a}^{\infty}\big((\rho-1)^{2}+u^{4}+(e-1)^{2}+\sigma\lvert\partial_{r}u\rvert^{2}+\sigma^{2}\lvert\partial_{r}e\rvert^{2}\big)(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{a}^{\infty}\Big\{\lvert\partial_{r}u\rvert^{2}+\lvert u\partial_{r}u\rvert^{2}+\lvert\partial_{r}e\rvert^{2}+\sigma\lvert\partial_{t}u\rvert^{2}+\sigma^{2}\lvert\partial_{t}e\rvert^{2}\Big\}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t\leq C(a).

(5.46)

Furthermore, for each
\varepsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)}^{\infty}\big((\rho-1)^{2}+u^{4}+(e-1)^{2}+\sigma\lvert\partial_{r}u\rvert^{2}+\sigma^{2}\lvert\partial_{r}e\rvert^{2}\big)(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)}^{\infty}\Big\{\lvert\partial_{r}u\rvert^{2}+\lvert u\partial_{r}u\rvert^{2}+\lvert\partial_{r}e\rvert^{2}+\sigma\lvert\partial_{t}u\rvert^{2}+\sigma^{2}\lvert\partial_{t}e\rvert^{2}\Big\}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t\leq C(\varepsilon).

(5.47)

Proof.

The proof only for
\sqrt{\sigma}\partial_{r}u_{k}\overset{\ast}{\rightharpoonup}\sqrt{\sigma}\partial_{r}u

is presented, since the other limits can be shown via the same argument.

By Lemma
5.2
,

\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{a}^{\infty}(\sigma\lvert\partial_{r}u_{k}\rvert^{2})(r,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}r\leq a^{-m}\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{a}^{\infty}(\sigma\lvert\partial_{r}u_{k}\rvert^{2})(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\leq a^{-m}C(a).

Thus, there exists a subsequence (still denoted)
\{\partial_{r}u_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
such that

(\sqrt{\sigma}\partial_{r}u_{k},\,r^{\frac{m}{2}}\sqrt{\sigma}\partial_{r}u_{k})\,\overset{\ast}{\rightharpoonup}\,(w_{1},\,w_{2})\qquad\text{ in $L^{\infty}\big(0,T;L^{2}([a,\infty),{\mathrm{d}}r)\big)$}.

(5.48)

By (
5.48
), Lemma
5.4
,
and the dominated convergence theorem,
we see that the spatial weak derivative of
\sqrt{\sigma}u
exists and
w_{1}=\sqrt{\sigma}\partial_{r}u
.
As a result,
by (
5.48
) and the fundamental lemma of calculus,

w_{2}=r^{\frac{m}{2}}\sqrt{\sigma}\partial_{r}u
for
a.e.

(r,t)\in[a,\infty)\times[0,T]
.
Then (
5.48
) implies that

\sqrt{\sigma}\partial_{r}u_{k}\overset{\ast}{\rightharpoonup}\sqrt{\sigma}\partial_{r}u

in
L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}([a,\infty),r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r))
.
By the weak-star lower semi-continuity of the Sobolev norm,

\lVert\sqrt{\sigma}\partial_{r}u\rVert_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}([a,\infty),r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r))}\leq\liminf_{k\to\infty}\lVert\sqrt{\sigma}\partial_{r}u_{k}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}([a,\infty),r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r))}\leq C(a).

The other terms in (
5.8
) can be proved by the same argument.

Next, we show (
5.8
). Only the proof for
\sqrt{\sigma}\partial_{r}u
is provided, since the other terms
in (
5.8
) can be obtained in the same way.
Let
\phi\in L^{1}(0,T;L^{2}([a,\infty),r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r))
. Then, by Lemma
5.2
,

\displaystyle\Big\lvert\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\widetilde{r}_{k}(\varepsilon,t)}^{\infty}\sqrt{\sigma}\partial_{r}u_{k}\phi\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)}^{\infty}\sqrt{\sigma}\partial_{r}u\phi\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t\Big\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\Big\lvert\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\widetilde{r}_{k}(\varepsilon,t)}^{\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)}\sqrt{\sigma}\partial_{r}u_{k}\phi\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t\Big\rvert+\Big\lvert\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)}^{\infty}\sqrt{\sigma}(\partial_{r}u_{k}-\partial_{r}u)\phi\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t\Big\rvert

\displaystyle\leq C(a)\int_{0}^{T}\Big(\int_{\widetilde{r}_{k}(\varepsilon,t)}^{\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)}\lvert\phi\rvert^{2}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}{\mathrm{d}}t+\Big\lvert\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)}^{\infty}\sqrt{\sigma}(\partial_{r}u_{k}-\partial_{r}u)\phi\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t\Big\rvert

\displaystyle=:I_{k}^{(1)}+I_{k}^{(2)}.

I_{k}^{(2)}\to 0
as
k\to\infty
by (
5.48
).
For
I_{k}^{(1)}
, define

\chi^{(k,\varepsilon)}(r,t)\vcentcolon=\begin{cases}1&if $r\in[\widetilde{r}_{k}(\varepsilon,t),\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)]$ or $r\in[\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t),\widetilde{r}_{k}(\varepsilon,t)]$,\\
0&otherwise.\end{cases}

By
Lemma
5.3
, for each fixed
\varepsilon>0
,
\chi^{(k,\varepsilon)}(r,t)\to 0
as
k\to\infty

for
a.e.

(r,t)\in[a,\infty)\times[0,T]
.
Moreover, since
\lvert\chi^{(k,\varepsilon)}\phi\rvert\leq\lvert\phi\rvert
,
by the dominated convergence theorem, as
k\to\infty
,

I_{k}^{(1)}\vcentcolon=C(a)\int_{0}^{T}\Big(\int_{\widetilde{r}_{k}(\varepsilon,t)}^{\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)}\lvert\phi\rvert^{2}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}{\mathrm{d}}t=C(a)\int_{0}^{T}\Big(\int_{a}^{\infty}\chi^{(\varepsilon,k)}\lvert\phi\rvert^{2}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}{\mathrm{d}}t\to 0.

Let
\chi_{\varepsilon}
and
\chi_{\varepsilon}^{k}
be defined in (
5.38
)–(
5.39
).
Then the limits:
I_{k}^{(1)}
,
I_{k}^{(2)}\to 0
imply the weak-star convergence:

\chi_{\varepsilon}^{k}\sqrt{\sigma}\partial_{r}u_{k}\overset{\ast}{\rightharpoonup}\chi_{\varepsilon}\sqrt{\sigma}\partial_{r}u

as
k\to\infty
in
L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}([a,\infty),r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r))
.
By the weak-star lower semi-continuity of the Sobolev norm,
it follows that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)}^{\infty}(\sigma\lvert\partial_{r}u\rvert^{2})(r,t)r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r

\displaystyle=\lVert\chi_{\varepsilon}\sqrt{\sigma}\partial_{r}u\rVert_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}([a,\infty),r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r))}

\displaystyle\leq\liminf_{k\to\infty}\lVert\chi_{\varepsilon}^{k}\sqrt{\sigma}\partial_{r}u_{k}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}([a,\infty),r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r))}

\displaystyle=\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{\widetilde{r}_{k}(\varepsilon,t)}^{\infty}\sigma\lvert\partial_{r}u_{k}\rvert^{2}(r,t)r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\leq C(\varepsilon).

∎

In §
5.5
–§
5.7
below,
we denote
(\rho,u,e)(r,t)
in
[a,\infty)\times[0,T]
as the limit functions obtained in Lemma
5.4
–
5.5
and keep the simplified notation (
5.15
)
as in §5.4.

5.5.
Entropy estimate for the limit solution

Lemma 5.9
.

There exists
C(T)>0
such that

\displaystyle\esssup\limits_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{a}^{\infty}\Big(\rho\big(\frac{1}{2}\lvert u\rvert^{2}+\psi(e)\big)+G(\rho)\Big)(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r+\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\int_{a}^{\infty}\dfrac{\lvert\partial_{r}e\rvert^{2}}{e^{2}}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{a}^{\infty}\Big\{\big(\dfrac{2\mu}{n}+\lambda\big)\dfrac{1}{e}\Big\lvert\partial_{r}u+m\dfrac{u}{r}\Big\rvert^{2}+\dfrac{2m\mu}{n}\dfrac{1}{e}\Big\lvert\partial_{r}u-\dfrac{u}{r}\Big\rvert^{2}\Big\}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t\leq C(T).

(5.49)

Proof.

We divide the proof into four steps.

1. For each integer
L\geq 2
, let
\phi_{L}\in C^{\infty}([a,\infty))
be such that
\phi_{L}(r)=1
if
r\in[a,L-1]
and
\phi_{L}(r)=0
if
r\in[L,\infty)
.
Then
\phi_{L}\in\widetilde{H}^{1}_{0}([a,L],r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)
, where
\widetilde{H}_{0}^{1}
is the Sobolev space defined in (
5.37
). Moreover,

\partial_{r}\psi(e_{k})=\frac{e_{k}-1}{e_{k}}\partial_{r}e_{k}
.
Thus, for each fixed
t\in(0,T]
,

(\psi(e_{k})\phi_{L})(\cdot,t)\in\widetilde{H}^{1}_{0}([a,L],r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)

for all
(k,t)\in\mathbb{N}\times(0,T]
and

\displaystyle\int_{a}^{\infty}\rho_{k}\psi(e_{k})\phi_{L}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r-\int_{a}^{\infty}\rho\psi(e)\phi_{L}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r

\displaystyle\leq\int_{a}^{L}(\rho_{k}-\rho)\psi(e_{k})\phi_{L}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r+\int_{a}^{\infty}\rho(\psi(e_{k})-\psi(e))\phi_{L}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r

\displaystyle=\vcentcolon I_{k}^{(1)}+I_{k}^{(2)}.

For all
t\in(0,T]
,
I_{k}^{(1)}
is estimated by using Lemmas
5.2
and
5.6
as

\displaystyle I_{k}^{(1)}

\displaystyle\leq\lVert\rho_{k}-\rho\rVert_{\widetilde{H}^{-1}([a,L];r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)}\lVert\psi(e_{k})\phi_{L}\rVert_{H^{1}([a,L];r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)}

\displaystyle=\lVert\rho_{k}-\rho\rVert_{\widetilde{H}^{-1}}\Big\{\lVert\phi_{L}\rVert_{H^{1}}\lVert\psi(e_{k})\rVert_{L^{\infty}}+\sigma^{-1}(t)\Big\lVert\dfrac{e_{k}-1}{e_{k}}\phi_{L}\Big\rVert_{\infty}\lVert\sigma\partial_{r}e_{k}\rVert_{L^{2}}\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq\lVert\rho_{k}-\rho\rVert_{\widetilde{H}^{-1}}\big\{C(a)\lVert\phi_{L}\rVert_{H^{1}}+C(a)\sigma^{-1}(t)\big\}\to 0\qquad\ \text{as $k\to\infty$}.

From Lemma
5.4
,

e_k(r,t)→e(r,t)
as
k\to\infty
for
(r,t)\in[a,\infty)\times[0,T]
,

and, from Lemmas
5.4
–
5.5
,

C^-1(a)≤
ρ
(r,t) ≤C(a),    C^-1(a) ≤e_k(r,t),  e(r,t) ≤C(a)
σ
^-

1

2

(t)

for
a.e.

(r,t)\in[a,\infty)\times(0,T]
.
Using these and the dominated convergence theorem, it follows that

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{k\to\infty}I_{k}^{(2)}\vcentcolon=\lim\limits_{k\to\infty}\int_{a}^{\infty}\rho\phi_{n}(\psi(e_{k})-\psi(e))(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r=0\qquad\text{ for each $t\in(0,T]$}.

Using Lemma
5.1
, the limits:
I_{k}^{(1)},I_{k}^{(2)}\to 0
then imply that, for each
t>0
,

\displaystyle\int_{a}^{\infty}\phi_{L}(r,t)(\rho\psi(e))(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r=\lim\limits_{k\to\infty}\int_{a}^{\infty}\phi_{L}(r,t)(\rho_{k}\psi(e_{k}))(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\leq C(T).

Now, by construction,
\phi_{L}(r)\to 1
for all
r\in[a,\infty)
as
L\to\infty
.
Since
\rho\psi(e)>0
, we apply the monotone convergence theorem to obtain

\displaystyle\int_{a}^{\infty}(\rho\psi(e))(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r=\lim\limits_{L\to\infty}\int_{a}^{\infty}\phi_{L}(r,t)(\rho\psi(e))(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\leq C(T)\qquad\text{ for each $t\in(0,T]$}.

2. From Lemma
5.2
,
u_{k}^{2}\in L^{2}([a,\infty),r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)
and, for all
(k,t)\in\mathbb{N}\times(0,T]
,

\lVert\partial_{r}u_{k}^{2}(\cdot,t)\rVert_{L^{2}([a,\infty),r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)}=\lVert u_{k}\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lVert\partial_{r}u_{k}(\cdot,t)\rVert_{L^{2}([a,\infty),r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)}\leq\sigma^{-\frac{3}{4}}(t)C(a).

From these,
u_{k}\phi_{L}\in\widetilde{H}_{0}^{1}([a,L],r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)
and

\displaystyle\Big\lvert\int_{a}^{\infty}\rho_{k}u_{k}^{2}\phi_{L}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r-\int_{a}^{\infty}\phi_{L}\rho u^{2}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\Big\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\rho_{k}-\rho\right\rVert_{\widetilde{H}^{-1}([a,L],r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)}\lVert u_{k}^{2}\phi_{L}\rVert_{H^{1}([a,L],r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)}+\int_{a}^{\infty}\rho(u_{k}^{2}-u^{2})\phi_{L}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r

\displaystyle\leq\int_{a}^{\infty}\phi_{L}\rho(u_{k}^{2}-u^{2})\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r+C(a)\big(1+\sigma^{-\frac{3}{4}}(t)\big)\lVert\rho_{k}-\rho\rVert_{\widetilde{H}^{-1}([a,L],r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)}.

Since
u_{k}(r,t)\to u(r,t)
from Lemma
5.4
,

\lvert u_{k}(r,t)\rvert+\lvert u(r,t)\rvert\leq C(a)\sigma^{-\frac{1}{4}}(t)

from Lemmas
5.2
and
5.4
,
and
\rho_{k}\to\rho
strongly in
\widetilde{H}^{-1}
from Lemma
5.6
,
then, by the dominated convergence theorem,

\displaystyle\int_{a}^{\infty}\rho u^{2}\phi_{L}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r=\lim\limits_{k\to\infty}\int_{a}^{\infty}\rho_{k}u_{k}^{2}\phi_{L}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\leq\limsup\limits_{k\to\infty}\int_{a}^{\infty}\rho_{k}u_{k}^{2}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\leq C(T).

Then
\lVert(\rho u^{2})(\cdot,t)\rVert_{L^{1}([a,\infty),r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)}\leq C(T)
follows
from the monotone convergence theorem for
L\to\infty
.

3. Let
\phi\in L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}([a,\infty),r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r))
. By Lemma
5.2
,

\displaystyle\Big\lvert\int_{0}^{T}\int_{a}^{\infty}\dfrac{\partial_{r}e_{k}}{e_{k}}\phi\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{a}^{\infty}\dfrac{\partial_{r}e}{e}\phi\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t\Big\rvert

\displaystyle\leq C(a)\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{a}^{\infty}\big(e_{k}^{-1}-e^{-1}\big)^{2}\lvert\phi\rvert^{2}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\Big\lvert\int_{0}^{T}\int_{a}^{\infty}(\partial_{r}e_{k}-\partial_{r}e)\dfrac{\phi}{e}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t\Big\rvert

\displaystyle=\vcentcolon\textrm{II}_{k}^{(1)}+\textrm{II}_{k}^{(2)}.

Using
e_{k}\to e

from Lemma
5.4
,
and the estimates:
C(a)\leq e_{k}(r,t),e(r,t)\leq C(a)\sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)
from Lemmas
5.2
and
5.4
,
we use the dominated convergence theorem to obtain

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{k\to\infty}\textrm{II}_{k}^{(1)}\vcentcolon=\lim\limits_{k\to\infty}C(a)\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{a}^{\infty}\big(e_{k}^{-1}-e^{-1}\big)^{2}\lvert\phi\rvert^{2}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}=0.

For
\textrm{II}_{k}^{(2)}
, since
e(r,t)\geq C^{-1}(a)

and
\partial_{r}e_{k}\rightharpoonup\partial_{r}e
in Lemma
5.8
, we have

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{k\to\infty}\textrm{II}_{k}^{(2)}\vcentcolon=\lim\limits_{k\to\infty}\Big\lvert\int_{0}^{T}\int_{a}^{\infty}(\partial_{r}e_{k}-\partial_{r}e)\dfrac{\phi}{e}(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t\Big\rvert=0.

The above limits imply that
\frac{\partial_{r}e_{k}}{e_{k}}\rightharpoonup\frac{\partial_{r}e}{e}
as
k\to\infty

weakly in
L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}([a,\infty),r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r))
.
By Lemma
5.1
and the weak-star lower semi-continuity of the Sobolev norm,
it follows that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{a}^{\infty}\dfrac{\lvert\partial_{r}e\rvert^{2}}{e^{2}}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t\leq\liminf_{k\to\infty}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{a}^{\infty}\dfrac{\lvert\partial_{r}e_{k}\rvert^{2}}{e_{k}^{2}}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t\leq C(T).

By the same argument,
we can also obtain the estimates
for
\lVert(\partial_{r}u+m\frac{u}{r})e^{-1}\rVert_{L^{2}}
and
\lVert(\partial_{r}u-\frac{u}{r})e^{-1}\rVert_{L^{2}}

in (
5.9
).

4. From Lemmas
5.1
–
5.2
and
5.5
, we have

\begin{cases}\rho_{k}\rightharpoonup\rho\ \text{ weakly in }\ L^{2}\big(0,T;L^{2}([a,\infty),r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)\big)\qquad\text{ as $k\to\infty$}.\\
\sup\limits_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\esssup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{a}^{\infty}G(\rho_{k})(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\leq C(T),\\
C^{-1}(a)\leq\rho_{k}(r,t)\leq C(a)\qquad\text{for {\it a.e.} \ $(r,t)\in[a,\infty)\times[0,T]$ and for each
$k\in\mathbb{N}$}.\end{cases}

(5.50)

By Proposition
B.2
,

\lVert G(\rho)(\cdot,t)\rVert_{L^{1}([a,\infty),r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)}\leq C(T)
for
a.e.

t\in[0,T]
.
∎

5.6.
Weak forms of the exterior problem

We now show that
(\rho,u,e)
is indeed a weak solution of problem (
1.6
) and (
3.9
).

Lemma 5.10
.

Let
(\rho,u,e)(r,t)
be the limit function obtained
in
Lemmas
5.4
–
5.5
.
Then
(\rho,u,e)(r,t)
is a weak solution of problem (
1.6
) and (
3.9
) as stated in
Definition
3.1
.

Proof.

We divide the proof into three steps.

1. We start with the
continuity equation
.
For any
\phi\in\mathcal{D}^{a}
, then there exists an integer
N\in\mathbb{N}

such that
\phi(r,t)=0
for
r\geq N
and
t\in[0,T]
.
Take
k\in\mathbb{N}
to be
k\geq M(\phi,a)\vcentcolon=2^{n}N^{n}\max\{C_{0},C(a)\}
.
Then, for all
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{r}_{k}(k,t)=\Big(a^{n}+\int_{0}^{k}\widetilde{v}_{k}(x,t){\mathrm{d}}x\Big)^{\frac{1}{n}}\geq\Big(a^{n}+\frac{k}{C(a)}\Big)^{\frac{1}{n}}\geq(a^{n}+2^{n}N^{n})^{\frac{1}{n}}>2N,

\displaystyle\widetilde{r}_{a}^{0}(k)=\Big(a^{n}+\int_{0}^{k}\dfrac{1}{\rho_{a}^{0}(\widetilde{r}_{a}^{0}(x))}{\mathrm{d}}x\Big)^{\frac{1}{n}}\geq\Big(a^{n}+\frac{k}{C_{0}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{n}}\geq(a^{n}+2^{n}N^{n})^{\frac{1}{n}}>2N.

Thus, it follows by construction that, if
(r,t)\in{\mathrm{supp}}(\phi)
and
k\geq M(\phi,a)
,

\begin{split}\rho_{k}(r,t)=&\,\overline{v}_{k}^{-1}\varphi_{k}(r,t)+1-\varphi_{k}(r,t)=\overline{v}_{k}^{-1}(r,t),\ \ u_{k}(r,t)=\overline{u}_{k}\varphi_{k}(r,t)=\overline{u}_{k}(r,t),\\
e_{k}(r,t)=&\,\overline{e}_{k}\varphi_{k}(r,t)+1-\varphi_{k}(r,t)=\overline{e}_{k}(r,t).\end{split}

(5.51)

Moreover, using that
\widetilde{r}_{a}^{0}(k)>2N

for
k\geq M(\phi,a)\vcentcolon=2^{n}N^{n}\max\{C_{0},C(a)\}
,
we see from construction (
3.12
) in §
3.4
that,
if
r\in{\mathrm{supp}}(\phi(\cdot,0))
, then

\begin{split}\rho_{a,k}^{0}(r)=&\,\rho_{a}^{0}(r)\varphi_{a,k}^{0}(r)+1-\varphi_{a,k}^{0}(r)=\rho_{a}^{0}(r),\ \ u_{a,k}^{0}(r)=u_{a}^{0}(r)\varphi_{a,k}^{0}(r)=u_{a}^{0}(r),\\
e_{a,k}^{0}(r)=&\,e_{a}^{0}(r)\varphi_{a,k}^{0}(r)+1-\varphi_{a,k}^{0}(r)=e_{a}^{0}(r).\end{split}

(5.52)

With this, if
x\in[0,\infty)
is such that
\widetilde{r}_{a}^{0}(x)\in{\mathrm{supp}}(\phi(\cdot,0))
, then
it follows from Proposition
3.2
that,
for all
k\geq M(\phi,a)
,

\widetilde{r}_{a}^{0}(x)=\Big(a^{n}+n\int_{0}^{x}\dfrac{1}{\rho_{a}^{0}(\widetilde{r}_{a}^{0}(y))}{\mathrm{d}}y\Big)^{\frac{1}{n}}=\Big(a^{n}+n\int_{0}^{x}\widetilde{v}_{a,k}^{0}(y){\mathrm{d}}y\Big)^{\frac{1}{n}}=\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,0).

(5.53)

Thus, from the coordinate transformation (
5.3
) and the continuity equation for which the approximate Lagrangian functions satisfy, we obtain that, for all
k\geq M(\phi,a)
,

\displaystyle\int_{a}^{\infty}\Big\{\rho_{k}(r,t)\phi(r,t)-\rho_{a}^{0}(r)\phi(r,0)\Big\}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r

\displaystyle=\int_{a}^{\widetilde{r}_{k}(k,t)}\overline{v}_{k}^{-1}\phi(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r-\int_{a}^{\widetilde{r}_{a}^{0}(k)}\phi(r,0)\rho_{a}^{0}(r)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{k}\phi(\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,t),t)\,{\mathrm{d}}x-\int_{0}^{k}\phi(\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,0),0)\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\big(\widetilde{u}_{k}\partial_{r}\phi+\partial_{t}\phi\big)(\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,s),s)\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{a}^{\widetilde{r}_{k}(k,t)}\big(\overline{v}_{k}^{-1}\overline{u}_{k}\partial_{r}\phi+\widetilde{v}_{k}^{-1}\partial_{t}\phi\big)(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{a}^{\infty}\big(\rho_{k}u_{k}\partial_{r}\phi+\rho_{k}\partial_{t}\phi\big)(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}s,

where we have used
\widetilde{r}_{k}(k,t)\geq 2N
and (
5.51
) in the last equality.
Also, by Lemma
5.4
–
5.6
, we have

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{k\to\infty}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\lVert\rho_{k}(\cdot,t)-\rho(\cdot,t)\rVert_{\widetilde{H}^{-1}([a,L],r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)}=0

\displaystyle\text{ for all $L\in\mathbb{N}$,}

(5.54)

\displaystyle\rho_{k}-1\overset{\ast}{\rightharpoonup}\rho-1

\displaystyle\text{ in $L^{\infty}\big(0,T;L^{2}([a,\infty),r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)\big)$,}

\displaystyle u_{k}(r,t)\to u(r,t)\ \text{ and }\ \rho_{k}(r,t),\,\rho(r,t)\leq C(a)

\displaystyle\text{ for {\it a.e.} $(r,t)\in[a,\infty)\times[0,T]$.}

Using these, we can apply the dominated convergence theorem to obtain

\displaystyle\int_{a}^{\infty}\rho(r,t)\phi(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r-\int_{a}^{\infty}\rho_{a}^{0}(r)\phi(r,0)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{a}^{\infty}\big(\rho u\partial_{r}\phi+\rho\partial_{t}\phi\big)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}s.

2. We show the weak form for the
momentum equation
.
Let
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}_{0}^{a}
.
Then there exists
N\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\varphi(r,t)=0
for
r\geq N
and
t\in[0,T]
.
If
k\in\mathbb{N}
is such that
k\geq M(\varphi,a)
, then, as previously shown,

\widetilde{r}_{k}(k,t)>2N
for all
t\in[0,T]
, and
\widetilde{r}_{a}^{0}(k)>2N
.
By construction, if
(r,t)\in{\mathrm{supp}}(\varphi)
and
r\in{\mathrm{supp}}(\varphi(\cdot,0))
,
then (
5.51
)–(
5.52
) hold so that

\displaystyle\int_{a}^{\infty}(\rho_{k}u_{k})(r,t)\varphi(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r-\int_{a}^{\infty}(\rho_{a}^{0}u_{a}^{0})(r)\varphi(r,0)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r

\displaystyle=\int_{a}^{\widetilde{r}_{k}(k,t)}(\overline{v}_{k}^{-1}\overline{u}_{k})(r,t)\varphi(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r-\int_{a}^{\widetilde{r}_{a}^{0}(k)}u_{a,k}^{0}(r)\varphi(r,0)\rho_{a}^{0}(r)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{k}\widetilde{u}_{k}(x,t)\varphi(\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,t),t)\,{\mathrm{d}}x-\int_{0}^{k}\widetilde{u}_{a,k}^{0}(x)\varphi(\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,0),0)\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\big(\varphi(\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,s),s){\mathrm{D}}_{t}\widetilde{u}_{k}+\partial_{t}\varphi(\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,s),s)\widetilde{u}_{k}+\partial_{r}\varphi(\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,s),s)\widetilde{u}_{k}^{2}\big)\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s.

By
\eqref{eqs:LFNS-k}_{2}

and the boundary condition:
\varphi(a,t)=0
,
\widetilde{u}_{k}(k,t)=0
for
t\in[0,T]
,
it follows that

\displaystyle\int_{a}^{\infty}(\rho_{k}u_{k})(r,t)\varphi(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r-\int_{a}^{\infty}(\rho_{a}^{0}u_{a}^{0})(r)\varphi(r,0)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\big(\varphi(\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,s),s){\mathrm{D}}_{t}\widetilde{u}_{k}+\partial_{t}\varphi(\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,s),s)\widetilde{u}_{k}+\partial_{r}\varphi(\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,s),s)\widetilde{u}_{k}^{2}\big)\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\big(\widetilde{u}_{k}(x,s)\,\partial_{t}\varphi(\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,s),s)+\widetilde{u}_{k}^{2}(x,s)\,\partial_{r}\varphi(\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,s),s)\big)\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\widetilde{r}_{k}^{m}(x,s)\varphi(\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,s),s){\mathrm{D}}_{x}\Big(\beta\dfrac{{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(\widetilde{r}_{k}^{m}\widetilde{u}_{k})}{\widetilde{v}_{k}}-(\gamma-1)\dfrac{\widetilde{e}_{k}}{\widetilde{v}_{k}}\Big)\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\big(\widetilde{u}_{k}^{2}(x,s)\,\partial_{r}\varphi(\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,s),s)+\widetilde{u}_{k}(x,s)\,\partial_{t}\varphi(\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,s),s)\big)\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\Big((\gamma-1)\widetilde{e}_{k}-\beta\widetilde{r}_{k}^{m}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}\widetilde{u}_{k}-m\beta\dfrac{\widetilde{v}_{k}\widetilde{u}_{k}}{\widetilde{r}_{k}}\Big)\Big(m\dfrac{\varphi(\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,s),s)}{\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,s)}+\partial_{r}\varphi(\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,s),s)\Big)\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{a}^{\widetilde{r}_{k}(k,t)}\big(\overline{v}_{k}^{-1}\overline{u}_{k}^{2}\partial_{r}\varphi+\overline{v}_{k}^{-1}\overline{u}_{k}\partial_{t}\varphi\big)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{a}^{\widetilde{r}_{k}(k,t)}\Big((\gamma-1)\overline{v}_{k}^{-1}\overline{e}_{k}-\beta\partial_{r}\overline{u}_{k}-m\beta\dfrac{\overline{u}_{k}}{r}\Big)\Big(\partial_{r}\varphi+m\dfrac{\varphi}{r}\Big)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{a}^{\infty}\big(\rho_{k}u_{k}^{2}\partial_{r}\varphi+\rho_{k}u_{k}\partial_{t}\varphi\big)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{a}^{\infty}\Big((\gamma-1)\rho_{k}e_{k}-\beta\partial_{r}u_{k}-m\beta\dfrac{u_{k}}{r}\Big)\Big(\partial_{r}\varphi+m\dfrac{\varphi}{r}\Big)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}s.

In addition, from Lemmas
5.2
and
5.4
–
5.8
, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{r}u_{k}\rightharpoonup\partial_{r}u

\displaystyle\text{ in $L^{2}\big(0,T;L^{2}([a,\infty),r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)\big)$,}

(5.55)

\displaystyle e_{k}(r,t)\to e(r,t)

\displaystyle\text{ for $(r,t)\in[a,\infty)\times[0,T]$,}

\displaystyle\sigma^{-\frac{1}{4}}(t)\lvert u(r,t)\rvert+\sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)e(r,t)\leq C(a)

\displaystyle\text{ for $(r,t)\in[a,\infty)\times[0,T]$,}

\displaystyle\sigma^{-\frac{1}{4}}(t)\lvert u_{k}(r,t)\rvert+\sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)e_{k}(r,t)\leq C(a)

\displaystyle\text{ for $(k,r,t)\in\mathbb{N}\times[a,\infty)\times[0,T]$.}

Using (
5.54
) and (
5.55
),
we conclude Definition
3.1
(iii)(ii)
.

3. We now show the
energy equation
.
Let
\phi\in\mathcal{D}^{a}
. Then, for
k\geq M(\phi,a)
,

\displaystyle\int_{a}^{\infty}(\rho_{k}e_{k})(r,t)\phi(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r-\int_{a}^{\infty}(\rho_{a}^{0}e_{a}^{0})(r)\phi(r,0)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{k}\widetilde{e}_{k}(x,t)\phi(\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,t),t)\,{\mathrm{d}}x-\int_{0}^{k}\widetilde{e}_{a,k}^{0}(x)\phi(\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,0),0)\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\Big\{\phi(\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,s),s){\mathrm{D}}_{t}\widetilde{e}_{k}(x,s)+\widetilde{e}_{k}(x,s)\big(\partial_{t}\phi+\widetilde{u}_{k}(x,s)\partial_{r}\phi\big)(\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,s),s)\Big\}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\phi(\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,s),s){\mathrm{D}}_{t}\widetilde{e}_{k}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{a}^{\infty}\rho_{k}e_{k}(\partial_{t}\phi+u_{k}\partial_{r}\phi)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle=\vcentcolon I_{1}+I_{2}.

(5.56)

Denote
\widetilde{F}_{k}\vcentcolon=\beta\widetilde{v}_{k}^{-1}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(\widetilde{r}_{k}^{m}\widetilde{u}_{k})-(\gamma-1)\widetilde{v}_{k}^{-1}\widetilde{e}_{k}
.
It follows from the momentum equation that
{\mathrm{D}}_{t}\widetilde{u}_{k}=\widetilde{r}_{k}^{m}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}\widetilde{F}_{k}
.
Then substituting this into
\eqref{eqs:LFNS-k}_{3}
yields

\displaystyle{\mathrm{D}}_{t}\widetilde{e}_{k}={\mathrm{D}}_{x}\big(\widetilde{r}_{k}^{m}\widetilde{u}_{k}\widetilde{F}_{k}\big)-\dfrac{1}{2}{\mathrm{D}}_{t}\lvert\widetilde{u}_{k}\rvert^{2}-2m\mu{\mathrm{D}}_{x}\big(\widetilde{r}_{k}^{m-1}\widetilde{u}_{k}^{2}\big)+\kappa{\mathrm{D}}_{x}\Big(\dfrac{\widetilde{r}_{k}^{2m}}{\widetilde{v}_{k}}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}\widetilde{e}_{k}\Big)

for
I_{1}
which, along with
{\mathrm{D}}_{x}\widetilde{e}_{k}(0,t)={\mathrm{D}}_{x}\widetilde{e}_{k}(k,t)=0
for all
t\in[0,T]
, leads to

\displaystyle I_{1}=

\displaystyle\,\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\phi(\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,s),s)\Big\{{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(\widetilde{r}_{k}^{m}\widetilde{u}_{k}\widetilde{F}_{k})-\dfrac{1}{2}{\mathrm{D}}_{t}\lvert\widetilde{u}_{k}\rvert^{2}\Big\}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\,+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\phi(\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,s),s)\Big\{-2m\mu{\mathrm{D}}_{x}(\widetilde{r}_{k}^{m-1}\widetilde{u}_{k}^{2})+\kappa{\mathrm{D}}_{x}\Big(\dfrac{\widetilde{r}_{k}^{2m}}{\widetilde{v}_{k}}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}\widetilde{e}_{k}\Big)\Big\}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\big\{-m\beta\widetilde{r}_{k}^{-1}\widetilde{v}_{k}\lvert\widetilde{u}_{k}\rvert^{2}-\beta\widetilde{r}_{k}^{m}\widetilde{u}_{k}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}\widetilde{u}_{k}+(\gamma-1)\widetilde{u}_{k}\widetilde{e}_{k}\big\}\partial_{r}\phi(\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,s),s)\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\,+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\lvert\widetilde{u}_{k}\rvert^{2}\big\{\partial_{t}\phi(\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,s),s)+\widetilde{u}_{k}(x,s)\partial_{r}\phi(\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,s),s)\big\}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\,-\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{k}\lvert\widetilde{u}_{k}\rvert^{2}(x,t)\phi(\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,t),t)\,{\mathrm{d}}x+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{k}\lvert\widetilde{u}_{a,k}^{0}(x)\rvert^{2}\phi(\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,0),0)\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\,+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{k}\Big\{2m\mu\widetilde{r}_{k}^{-1}\widetilde{v}_{k}\lvert\widetilde{u}_{k}\rvert^{2}\,\partial_{r}\phi(\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,s),s)-\kappa\widetilde{r}_{k}^{m}{\mathrm{D}}_{x}\widetilde{e}_{k}\,\partial_{r}\phi(\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,s),s)\Big\}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s.

From (
5.53
),
\widetilde{r}_{k}(x,0)=\widetilde{r}_{a}^{0}(x)

if
\widetilde{r}_{a}^{0}(x)\in{\mathrm{supp}}(\phi(\cdot,0))
and
k\geq M(\phi,a)
.
By the coordinate transformation (
5.1
)–(
5.3
),
and (
5.51
)–(
5.52
), it follows that

\displaystyle I_{1}
