\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{a}^{\widetilde{r}_{k}(k,t)}\Big\{m(2\mu-\beta)\dfrac{\lvert\overline{u}_{k}\rvert^{2}}{r}-\beta\overline{u}_{k}\partial_{r}\overline{u}_{k}+(\gamma-1)\overline{u}_{k}\overline{v}_{k}^{-1}\overline{e}_{k}-\kappa\partial_{r}\overline{e}_{k}\Big\}\partial_{r}\phi\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{a}^{\widetilde{r}_{k}(k,t)}\overline{v}_{k}^{-1}\lvert\overline{u}_{k}\rvert^{2}\big\{\partial_{t}\phi+\overline{u}_{k}\partial_{r}\phi\big\}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2}\int_{a}^{\widetilde{r}_{k}(k,t)}(\overline{v}_{k}^{-1}\lvert\overline{u}_{k}\rvert^{2})(r,t)\phi(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r+\frac{1}{2}\int_{a}^{\widetilde{r}_{a}^{0}(k)}(\rho_{a}^{0}\lvert u_{a}^{0}\rvert^{2})(r)\phi(r,0)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{a}^{\infty}\Big\{-m\lambda\dfrac{\lvert u_{k}\rvert^{2}}{r}-\beta u_{k}\partial_{r}u_{k}+(\gamma-1)\rho_{k}u_{k}e_{k}-\kappa\partial_{r}e_{k}\Big\}\partial_{r}\phi\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{a}^{\infty}\rho_{k}\lvert u_{k}\rvert^{2}\big\{\partial_{t}\phi+u_{k}\partial_{r}\phi\big\}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2}\int_{a}^{\infty}(\rho_{k}\lvert u_{k}\rvert^{2})(r,t)\phi(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r+\frac{1}{2}\int_{a}^{\infty}(\rho_{a}^{0}\lvert u_{a}^{0}\rvert^{2})(r)\phi(r,0)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r.

Define
E_{k}(r,t)\vcentcolon=(\frac{1}{2}\lvert u_{k}\rvert^{2}+e_{k})(r,t)
. Then the above identity becomes:

\displaystyle I_{1}=

\displaystyle\,\int_{0}^{t}\int_{a}^{\infty}\Big\{-m\lambda\dfrac{\lvert u_{k}\rvert^{2}}{r}-\beta u_{k}\partial_{r}u_{k}+(\gamma-1)\rho_{k}u_{k}e_{k}-\kappa\partial_{r}e_{k}\Big\}\partial_{r}\phi\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\,+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{a}^{\infty}\big\{\rho_{k}E_{k}(\partial_{t}\phi+u_{k}\partial_{r}\phi)-\rho_{k}e_{k}(\partial_{t}\phi+u_{k}\partial_{r}\phi)\big\}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\,-\frac{1}{2}\int_{a}^{\infty}(\rho_{k}\lvert u_{k}\rvert^{2})(r,t)\phi(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r+\frac{1}{2}\int_{a}^{\infty}(\rho_{a}^{0}\lvert u_{a}^{0}\rvert^{2})(r)\phi(r,0)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r.

Denote
P_{k}\vcentcolon=(\gamma-1)\rho_{k}e_{k}
and
E_{a}^{0}\vcentcolon=\frac{1}{2}\lvert u_{a}^{0}\rvert^{2}+e_{a}^{0}
.
Substituting
I_{1}
into (
5.6
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{a}^{\infty}(\rho_{k}E_{k})(r,t)\phi(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r-\int_{a}^{\infty}(\rho_{a}^{0}E_{a}^{0})(r)\phi(r,0)r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r

(5.57)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{a}^{\infty}\Big\{\Big((\rho_{k}E_{k}+P_{k})u_{k}-m\lambda\dfrac{\lvert u_{k}\rvert^{2}}{r}-\beta u_{k}\partial_{r}u_{k}-\kappa\partial_{r}e_{k}\Big)\partial_{r}\phi+\rho_{k}E_{k}\partial_{t}\phi\Big\}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}s.

In addition to (
5.55
), we also uses the weak convergence from Lemma
5.8

that
\partial_{r}e_{k}\rightharpoonup\partial_{r}e
in
L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}([a,\infty),r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r))
.
Then (
5.57
) becomes:

\displaystyle\int_{a}^{\infty}(\rho E)(r,t)\phi(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r-\int_{a}^{\infty}(\rho_{a}^{0}E_{a}^{0})(r)\phi(r,0)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{a}^{\infty}\Big\{\Big((\rho E+P)u-m\lambda\dfrac{\lvert u\rvert^{2}}{r}-\beta u\partial_{r}u-\kappa\partial_{r}e\Big)\partial_{r}\phi+\rho E\partial_{t}\phi\Big\}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}s,

where
P\vcentcolon=(\gamma-1)\rho e
and
E\vcentcolon=\frac{1}{2}\left\lvert u\right\rvert^{2}+e
.
∎

5.7.
Estimates away from the origin, independent of the particle path function

The main aim of this subsection is to obtain
the uniform estimates, independent of
a\in(0,1)
, which do not involve the particle path
function
\widetilde{r}(x,t)
.

Lemma 5.11
.

For each
\eta\in(a,1)
,

\begin{cases}\int_{0}^{T}\sup_{r\geq\eta}\,\dfrac{\lvert u\rvert}{\sqrt{e}}(r,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}t\leq C(T)\big(\eta^{\frac{2-n}{2}}+\eta^{2-n}\big)\ \ &if $n=2$, $3$,\\
\int_{0}^{T}\sup_{r\geq\eta}\log(\max\{1,e^{\pm 1}(r,t)\})\,{\mathrm{d}}t\leq C(T)\eta^{2-n}&if $n=3$,\\
\int_{0}^{T}\sup_{r\geq\eta}\log(\max\{1,e(r,t)\})\,{\mathrm{d}}t\leq C(T)\big(1+\sqrt{|\log\eta|}\big)&if $n=2$.\end{cases}

(5.58)

Proof.

We divide the proof into three steps.

1. Fix a point
r\in[\eta,\infty)
and a small constant
0<\delta<1
. Since
\lim_{r\to\infty}u_{k}(r,t)=0

and
\lim_{r\to\infty}e_{k}(r,t)=1
for each
t\in[0,T]
and
k\in\mathbb{N}
, we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{u_{k}}{r^{\delta}\sqrt{e_{k}}}(r,t)

\displaystyle=\lim\limits_{r\to\infty}\dfrac{u_{k}}{r^{\delta}\sqrt{e_{k}}}(r,t)-\int_{r}^{\infty}\Big\{\zeta^{-\delta}\dfrac{\partial_{r}u_{k}}{\sqrt{e_{k}}}-\delta\zeta^{-\delta-1}\dfrac{u_{k}}{\sqrt{e_{k}}}-\dfrac{1}{2}\zeta^{-\delta}u_{k}e_{k}^{-\frac{3}{2}}\partial_{r}e_{k}\Big\}(\zeta,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}\zeta

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\int_{r}^{\infty}\zeta^{-2\delta-m}{\mathrm{d}}\zeta\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big\{\delta\Big(\int_{r}^{\infty}\dfrac{\lvert u_{k}\rvert^{2}}{\zeta^{2}e_{k}}\,\zeta^{m}{\mathrm{d}}\zeta\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\Big(\int_{r}^{\infty}\dfrac{\lvert\partial_{r}u_{k}\rvert^{2}}{e_{k}}\,\zeta^{m}{\mathrm{d}}\zeta\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big\}

\displaystyle\quad+\dfrac{1}{2}\Big(\int_{r}^{\infty}\dfrac{\lvert u_{k}\rvert^{2}}{\zeta^{2}e_{k}}\zeta^{-\delta+1}{\mathrm{d}}\zeta\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\int_{r}^{\infty}\dfrac{\lvert\partial_{r}e_{k}\rvert}{e_{k}^{2}}\zeta^{-\delta+1}{\mathrm{d}}\zeta\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{r^{-\delta}\eta^{\frac{1-m}{2}}}{\sqrt{2\delta+m-1}}\Big\{\delta\Big(\int_{\eta}^{\infty}\dfrac{\lvert u\rvert_{k}^{2}}{\zeta^{2}e_{k}}(\zeta,t)\,\zeta^{m}{\mathrm{d}}\zeta\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\Big(\int_{\eta}^{\infty}\dfrac{\lvert\partial_{r}u_{k}\rvert^{2}}{e_{k}}\,\zeta^{m}{\mathrm{d}}\zeta\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big\}

\displaystyle\quad+\dfrac{1}{2}r^{-\delta}\eta^{1-m}\Big(\int_{\eta}^{\infty}\dfrac{\lvert u_{k}\rvert^{2}}{\zeta^{2}e_{k}}\,\zeta^{m}{\mathrm{d}}\zeta\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\int_{\eta}^{\infty}\dfrac{\lvert\partial_{r}e_{k}\rvert}{e_{k}^{2}}\,\zeta^{m}{\mathrm{d}}\zeta\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}=\vcentcolon r^{-\delta}g_{k}(\eta,t),

where we have used the fact that
-2\delta-m+1<0
and
-\delta+1-m<0
for
n=2,3
since
0<\delta<1
.
Multiplying both sides of the above inequality by
r^{\delta}
and taking the limit inferior over
k\to\infty
,
it follows by Lemma
5.4
that

\dfrac{|u|}{\sqrt{e}}(r,t)\leq\liminf\limits_{k\to\infty}g_{k}(\eta,t)\qquad\ \text{for all $(r,t)\in[\eta,\infty)\times[0,T]$.}

Taking the supremum in
r\in[\eta,\infty)
and integrating in
t\in[0,T]
, we obtain

\int_{0}^{T}\sup_{r\geq\eta}\,\dfrac{|u|}{\sqrt{e}}(r,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}t\leq\int_{0}^{T}\liminf_{k\to\infty}g_{k}(\eta,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}t\leq\liminf_{k\to\infty}\int_{0}^{T}g_{k}(\eta,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}t,

(5.59)

where we have used Fatou’s lemma since
g_{k}(\eta,t)>0
.
By definition of
g_{k}(\eta,t)

and Lemma
5.1
, we obtain that, for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}g_{k}(\eta,t){\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\leq C\eta^{\frac{1-m}{2}}\Big\{\int_{0}^{T}\Big(\int_{\eta}^{\infty}\dfrac{\lvert u\rvert_{k}^{2}}{\zeta^{2}e_{k}}\,\zeta^{m}{\mathrm{d}}\zeta\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}{\mathrm{d}}t+\int_{0}^{T}\Big(\int_{\eta}^{\infty}\dfrac{\lvert\partial_{r}u_{k}\rvert^{2}}{e_{k}}\,\zeta^{m}{\mathrm{d}}\zeta\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}{\mathrm{d}}t\Big\}

\displaystyle\quad+C\eta^{1-m}\int_{0}^{T}\Big(\int_{\eta}^{\infty}\dfrac{\lvert u_{k}\rvert^{2}}{\zeta^{2}e_{k}}\,\zeta^{m}{\mathrm{d}}\zeta\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\int_{\eta}^{\infty}\dfrac{\lvert\partial_{r}e_{k}\rvert}{e_{k}^{2}}\,\zeta^{m}{\mathrm{d}}\zeta\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\leq C\eta^{\frac{1-m}{2}}T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big\{\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\eta}^{\infty}\dfrac{\left\lvert u\right\rvert_{k}^{2}}{\zeta^{2}e_{k}}\,\zeta^{m}{\mathrm{d}}\zeta{\mathrm{d}}t\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\eta}^{\infty}\dfrac{\lvert\partial_{r}u_{k}\rvert^{2}}{e_{k}}\,\zeta^{m}{\mathrm{d}}\zeta{\mathrm{d}}t\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big\}

\displaystyle\quad+C\eta^{1-m}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\eta}^{\infty}\dfrac{\lvert u_{k}\rvert^{2}}{\zeta^{2}e_{k}}\,\zeta^{m}{\mathrm{d}}\zeta{\mathrm{d}}t\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\eta}^{\infty}\dfrac{\lvert\partial_{r}e_{k}\rvert}{e_{k}^{2}}\,\zeta^{m}{\mathrm{d}}\zeta{\mathrm{d}}t\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)(\eta^{\frac{1-m}{2}}+\eta^{1-m}),

which, together with (
5.59
), leads to
\eqref{temp:dissip0}_{1}
.

2. Consider the case:
n=3
. Let
(r,t)\in[\eta,\infty)\times[0,T]
. By
\lim_{r\to\infty}e_{k}(r,t)=1
, we have

\displaystyle\log e_{k}(r,t)

\displaystyle=-\int_{r}^{\infty}\dfrac{\partial_{r}e_{k}}{e_{k}}(\zeta,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}r\leq\Big(\int_{r}^{\infty}\zeta^{-m}{\mathrm{d}}\zeta\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\int_{r}^{\infty}\dfrac{\lvert\partial_{r}e_{k}\rvert^{2}}{e_{k}^{2}}(\zeta,t)\,\zeta^{m}{\mathrm{d}}\zeta\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{\eta^{2-n}}{\sqrt{n-2}}\Big(\int_{\eta}^{\infty}\dfrac{\lvert\partial_{r}e_{k}\rvert^{2}}{e_{k}^{2}}(\zeta,t)\,\zeta^{m}{\mathrm{d}}\zeta\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}=\vcentcolon h_{k}(\eta,t).

Thus,
0\leq\log(\max\{1,e_{k}\})(r,t)\leq h_{k}(\eta,t)
for all
(r,t)\in[\eta,\infty)\times[0,T]
.
Replacing the term
e_{k}(r,t)
with
e_{k}^{-1}(r,t)
in the above derivation,
then
0\leq\log(\max\{1,e_{k}^{-1}\})(r,t)\leq h_{k}(\eta,t)
for all
(r,t)\in[\eta,\infty)\times[0,T]
.
Taking the limit inferior over
k\to\infty
on both sides of these inequalities,
it follows from Lemma
5.4
that

\displaystyle\log(\max\{1,e^{\pm 1}\})(r,t)\leq\liminf_{k\to\infty}h_{k}(\eta,t)\qquad\ \text{for all $(r,t)\in[\eta,\infty)\times[0,T]$.}

Taking the supremum in
r\geq\eta
on the above inequality and integrating in
t\in[0,T]
, we have

\int_{0}^{T}\sup_{r\geq\eta}\log(\max\{1,e^{\pm 1}(r,t)\})\,{\mathrm{d}}t\leq\int_{0}^{T}\liminf_{k\to\infty}h_{k}(\eta,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}t\leq\liminf_{k\to\infty}\int_{0}^{T}h_{k}(\eta,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}t,

(5.60)

where we have used Fatou’s lemma since
h_{k}(\eta,t)>0
.
By Lemma
5.1
, we obtain that, for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}h_{k}(\eta,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}t\leq C\eta^{2-n}\sqrt{T}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\eta}^{\infty}\dfrac{\lvert\partial_{r}e_{k}\rvert^{2}}{e_{k}^{2}}\,\zeta^{m}{\mathrm{d}}\zeta{\mathrm{d}}t\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(T)\eta^{2-n},

which, together with (
5.60
), proves
\eqref{temp:dissip0}_{2}
.

3. We consider the case:
n=2
.
Set
M\vcentcolon=\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\widetilde{r}_{k}(1,t)

so that, by Lemma
4.1
,
M\leq(2C_{0})^{\frac{1}{n}}
.
If
r\geq M
, then

r>\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\widetilde{r}_{k}(1,t)
for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
.
By Lemma
5.2
,
e_{k}(r,t)\leq C(\varepsilon)\sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)
for all
t\in[0,T]
and
r\in[\widetilde{r}_{k}(\varepsilon,t),\infty)
.
It follows that there exists a constant
C(T)>1

such that

\log(\max\{1,e_{k}(r,t)\})\leq\log(C(T)\sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t))\qquad\,\text{for all $(r,t)\in[M,\infty)\times[0,T]$.}

(5.61)

By
(
5.61
), for all
r\in[\eta,M)
,

\displaystyle\log e_{k}(r,t)

\displaystyle=\log e_{k}(2M,t)-\int_{r}^{2M}\dfrac{\partial_{r}e_{k}}{e_{k}}(\zeta,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}\zeta

(5.62)

\displaystyle\leq\log(\max\{1,e_{k}(2M,t)\})+\Big(\int_{r}^{2M}\zeta^{-1}{\mathrm{d}}\zeta\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\int_{r}^{2M}\dfrac{\lvert\partial_{r}e_{k}\rvert^{2}}{e_{k}^{2}}(\zeta,t)\,\zeta{\mathrm{d}}\zeta\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\log(C(T)\sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t))+\sqrt{\log(C(T)/\eta)}\Big(\int_{\eta}^{\infty}\dfrac{\lvert\partial_{r}e_{k}\rvert^{2}}{e_{k}^{2}}(\zeta,t)\,\zeta{\mathrm{d}}\zeta\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Finally, in a similar way to that for the case
n=3
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\sup_{r\geq\eta}\log(\max\{1,e(r,t)\}){\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{C^{2}(T)}{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\sup_{r\in[\eta,M)}\log(\max\{1,e(r,t)\})\,{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\leq C(T)+\sqrt{T\log(C(T)/\eta)}\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\eta}^{\infty}\dfrac{\lvert\partial_{r}e_{k}\rvert^{2}}{e_{k}^{2}}\,\zeta^{m}{\mathrm{d}}\zeta{\mathrm{d}}t\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\big(1+\sqrt{|\log\eta|}\big).

∎

6.
Global Weak Solutions including the Origin

We denote
(\rho_{a},u_{a},e_{a})(r,t)
in
[a,\infty)\times[0,T]
as the solution
of problem (
1.6
) and (
3.9
), which is guaranteed by Theorem
3.1
,
with the modified initial data
(\rho_{a}^{0},u_{a}^{0},e_{a}^{0})(r)
constructed in §
3.2

for each
a\in(0,1)
. We also denote
\widetilde{r}_{a}(x,t)
as the particle path function associated
with
\rho_{a}
, which satisfies

x=\int_{a}^{\widetilde{r}_{a}(x,t)}\rho_{a}(y,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\qquad\text{for {\it a.e.} $(x,t)\in[0,\infty)\times[0,T]$}.

The main aim of this section is to take the limit:
a\searrow 0
of the solution sequence

(\rho_{a},u_{a},e_{a})(r,t)
of problems (
1.6
) and (
3.9
)
to obtain a global spherically symmetric weak solution of the original Cauchy
problem (
1.1
)–(
1.5
).

6.1.
Uniform inequalities from the entropy estimates

We obtain a set of inequalities involving measurable subsets of
[a,\infty)
, which
are uniform in
a\in(0,1)
.
In this section, we denote

G(\zeta)\vcentcolon=1-\zeta+\zeta\log\zeta,\quad\psi(\zeta)\vcentcolon=\zeta-1-\log\zeta,\quad H(\zeta)\vcentcolon=\zeta\log\zeta,

(6.1)

which are all convex functions.
Let
G^{-1}_{+}
,
\psi_{+}^{-1}
, and
H_{+}^{-1}
be the right branch inverses of
G
,
\psi
,
and
H
, respectively.
Then, by Proposition
B.1
,
the following functions are well defined for
(y,z)\in(0,\infty)^{2}\vcentcolon

f_{1}(y;z)\vcentcolon=yG_{+}^{-1}(\dfrac{z}{y}),\quad f_{2}(y;z)\vcentcolon=y\psi_{+}^{-1}(\dfrac{z}{y})-z,\quad f_{3}(y;z)\vcentcolon=yH_{+}^{-1}(\dfrac{z}{y}).

(6.2)

Some further properties of
(y,z)\mapsto f_{i}(y;z)
for
i=1,2,3
can be found
in Proposition
B.1
.

Lemma 6.1
.

For any measurable set
E\subset[a,\infty)
and each
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\sup_{a\in(0,1)}\int_{E}\rho_{a}(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\leq\omega_{1}(E;C(T)),

(6.3)

\displaystyle\sup_{a\in(0,1)}\int_{E}(\rho_{a}e_{a})(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\leq C(T)+\omega_{2}(E;C(T)),

(6.4)

where
\omega_{1}(E;z)\vcentcolon=f_{1}(\int_{E}r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r;z)

and

\omega_{2}(E;z)\vcentcolon=f_{2}(\omega_{1}(E;z);z)=f_{2}(f_{1}(\int_{E}r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r;z);z)
for
z>0
.

Proof.

Denote
\mathcal{L}_{n}(E)\vcentcolon=\int_{E}r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r
for each
E\subseteq[a,\infty)
.
By Jensen’s inequality and Theorem
3.1
(iii)(i)
, it follows that

\displaystyle G(\dfrac{1}{\mathcal{L}_{n}(E)}\int_{E}\rho_{a}(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)\leq\dfrac{1}{\mathcal{L}_{n}(E)}\int_{E}G(\rho_{a}(r,t))\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\leq\dfrac{C(T)}{\mathcal{L}_{n}(E)}.

Taking the right branch inverse
G_{+}^{-1}(z)
on both sides of the above
and using the fact that
z\mapsto G_{+}^{-1}(z)
is monotone increasing, it follows that, for all
a\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\int_{E}\rho_{a}(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\leq\mathcal{L}_{n}(E)G_{+}^{-1}(\dfrac{C(T)}{\mathcal{L}_{n}(E)})=f_{1}(\mathcal{L}_{n}(E);C(T))=\vcentcolon\omega_{1}(E;C(T)).

Now, fix
t\in[0,T]
and set
m_{a,t}(E)\vcentcolon=\int_{E}\rho_{a}(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r
.
By Jensen’s inequality and Theorem
3.1
(iii)(i)
, it follows that

\displaystyle\psi(\dfrac{1}{m_{a,t}(E)}\int_{E}(\rho_{a}e_{a})(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)\leq\dfrac{1}{m_{a,t}(E)}\int_{E}(\rho_{a}\psi(e_{a}))(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\leq\dfrac{C(T)}{m_{a,t}(E)}.

Taking
\psi_{+}^{-1}(\cdot)
on both sides of the above, it follows that

\displaystyle\int_{E}(\rho_{a}e_{a})(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\leq m_{a,t}(E)\psi_{+}^{-1}(\dfrac{C(T)}{m_{a,t}(E)})=f_{2}(m_{a,t}(E);C(T))+C(T).

Using (
6.3
),
m_{a,t}(E)\leq\omega_{1}(E;C(T))
.
Since
y\mapsto f_{2}(y;z)
is increasing in
y
for fixed
z>0
from Proposition
B.1
,
it follows that, for all
a\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\int_{E}(\rho_{a}e_{a})(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\leq C(T)+f_{2}(\omega_{1}(E;C(T));C(T))=\vcentcolon C(T)+\omega_{2}(E;C(T)).

∎

6.2.
Existence of particle paths and vacuum interfaces

Lemma 6.2
.

There exist both a subsequence
\{a_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
with
a_{j}\searrow 0
as
j\to\infty

and a continuous function
\widetilde{r}(x,t)\vcentcolon[0,\infty)\times[0,T]\to[0,\infty)
such that

(i)

for each compact subset
K\Subset(0,\infty)\times[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{j\to\infty}\sup_{(x,t)\in K}\lvert\widetilde{r}_{a_{j}}(x,t)-\widetilde{r}(x,t)\rvert=0.

(ii)

for each fixed
\varepsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\lvert\widetilde{r}(x_{1},t)-\widetilde{r}(x_{2},t)\rvert\leq C(\varepsilon)\lvert x_{1}-x_{2}\rvert

\displaystyle\text{for all $(x_{1},x_{2},t)\in[\varepsilon,\infty)^{2}\times[0,T]$},

\displaystyle\lvert\widetilde{r}(x,t_{1})-\widetilde{r}(x,t_{2})\rvert\leq C(\varepsilon)\lvert t_{1}^{\frac{3}{4}}-t_{2}^{\frac{3}{4}}\rvert

\displaystyle\text{for all $(x,t_{1},t_{2})\in[\varepsilon,\infty)\times[0,T]^{2}$}.

(iii)

for all
t\in[0,T]
, the function
:

x\mapsto\widetilde{r}(x,t)
is strictly increasing
so that
\underline{r}(t)\vcentcolon=\lim_{x\to 0^{+}}\widetilde{r}(x,t)
exists for all
t\in[0,T]

and

\lim_{t\searrow 0}\underline{r}(t)=0,\qquad t\mapsto\underline{r}(t)\ \text{is upper semi-continuous}.

In addition,
\mathring{\mathrm{F}}\vcentcolon=\{(r,t)\in(0,\infty)\times(0,T)\,\vcentcolon\,\underline{r}(t)<r\}

is an open set.

(iv)

\widetilde{r}(x,t)
satisfies the pointwise bound
:

nx\psi^{-1}_{-}(\dfrac{C_{0}}{x})\leq r^{n}(x,t)\leq C_{0}(1+x)\qquad\text{for all $(x,t)\in[0,\infty)\times[0,T]$}.

(v)

if
\underline{r}(t)>0
for some
t\in(0,T]
, then

\lim\limits_{j\to\infty}\int_{a_{j}}^{\underline{r}(t)}\rho_{a_{j}}(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r=0.

In particular, for such
t\in(0,T]
,

\liminf\limits_{j\to\infty}\rho_{a_{j}}(r,t)=0\qquad\text{for a.e. $r\in(0,\underline{r}(t))$}.

Proof.

We divide the proof into six steps.

1. For each integer
i\in\mathbb{N}
, let
S_{i}\vcentcolon=[i^{-1},i]\times[0,T]
.
From Theorem
3.1
(iii)(iii)
–
(iii)(iv)
, it follows that, for all
(x,t)\in S_{i}
,

\widetilde{r}_{a}(x,t)=\widetilde{r}_{a}^{0}(x)+\int_{0}^{t}u_{a}(\widetilde{r}(x,s),s)\,{\mathrm{d}}s\leq\widetilde{r}_{a}^{0}(x)+C(i)\int_{0}^{T}\sigma^{-\frac{1}{4}}(s)\,{\mathrm{d}}s\leq C(i)(1+T),

(6.5)

with
C(i)>0
a constant independent of
a\in(0,1)
, and

\begin{split}&\lvert\widetilde{r}_{a}(x_{1},t)-\widetilde{r}_{a}(x_{2},t)\rvert\leq C(i)\lvert x_{1}-x_{2}\rvert\qquad\text{for all $(x_{1},x_{2},t)\in[i^{-1},i]^{2}\times[0,T]$},\\
&\lvert\widetilde{r}_{a}(x,t_{1})-\widetilde{r}_{a}(x,t_{2})\rvert\leq C(i)\lvert t_{1}^{\frac{3}{4}}-t_{2}^{\frac{3}{4}}\rvert\qquad\,\text{for all $(x,t_{1},t_{2})\in[i^{-1},i]\times[0,T]^{2}$}.\end{split}

(6.6)

In addition,
S_{i}\subset S_{i+1}
,
\text{Int}(S_{i})\neq\emptyset
for each
i\in\mathbb{N}
, and
(0,\infty)\times[0,T]=\cup_{i\in\mathbb{N}}S_{i}
.
Thus, by Proposition
A.1
, it follows that there exists a continuous function
\widetilde{r}(x,t)\vcentcolon(0,\infty)\times[0,T]\to[0,\infty)
such that,
for all compact subset
K\Subset(0,\infty)\times[0,T]
,

\lim\limits_{j\to\infty}\sup\limits_{(x,t)\in K}\lvert\widetilde{r}_{a_{j}}(x,t)-\widetilde{r}(x,t)\rvert=0,

(6.7)

which, along with (
6.6
) and Theorem
3.1
(iii)(iv)
, implies Lemma
6.2
(iii)(ii)
.

2. Let
0<x_{1}<x_{2}<\infty
. It follows from Theorem
3.1
(iii)(iii)
–
(iii)(iv)
that,
for
a.e.

t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle x_{2}-x_{1}=\int_{\widetilde{r}_{a}(x_{1},t)}^{\widetilde{r}_{a}(x_{2},t)}\rho_{a}(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\leq C(x_{1})\int_{\widetilde{r}_{a}(x_{1},t)}^{\widetilde{r}_{a}(x_{2},t)}r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r=\dfrac{C(x_{1})}{n}\big(\widetilde{r}_{a}^{\,n}(x_{2},t)-\widetilde{r}_{a}^{\,n}(x_{1},t)\big),

where
we have used that
\rho_{a}(r,t)\leq C(x_{1})
for
a.e.

(r,t)

such that
r\geq\widetilde{r}_{a}(x_{1},t)
.
Taking
j\to\infty
and using (
6.7
) yield

0<x_{2}-x_{1}\leq\dfrac{C(x_{1})}{n}\big(\widetilde{r}^{\,n}(x_{2},t)-\widetilde{r}^{\,n}(x_{1},t)\big).

This shows that
x\mapsto\widetilde{r}(x,t)
is strictly increasing for
a.e.

t\in[0,T]
.
Since
(x,t)\mapsto\widetilde{r}(x,t)
is continuous, then
x\mapsto\widetilde{r}(x,t)
is strictly increasing
for all
t\in[0,T]
. Since
\widetilde{r}(x,t)\geq 0
,

\underline{r}(t)\vcentcolon=\lim\limits_{x\to 0^{+}}\widetilde{r}(x,t)\quad\text{ exists for all $t\in[0,T]$}.

(6.8)

From Theorem
3.1
(iii)(iii)
, it follows that
\widetilde{r}_{a}(x,0)=\widetilde{r}_{a}^{0}(x)

for each
a\in(0,1)
and
x\in[0,\infty)
, where
\widetilde{r}_{a}^{0}(x)
is the function constructed
in §
3.4
, satisfying

x=\int_{a}^{\widetilde{r}_{a}^{0}(x)}\rho_{a}^{0}(r)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\qquad\text{for all $x\in[0,\infty]$}.

Then, by the uniform convergence (
6.7
) and Proposition
3.1
in §
3.2
,
we have

x=\int_{0}^{\widetilde{r}(x,0)}\rho_{0}(r)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\geq\dfrac{\widetilde{r}^{\,n}(x,0)}{C_{0}n}\qquad\text{for all $x>0$}.

This implies the inequality:
\widetilde{r}(x,0)\leq(nC_{0}x)^{\frac{1}{n}}
.
Given
\delta>0
, let
x_{\delta}>0
be such that
\widetilde{r}(x_{\delta},0)\leq(nC_{0}x_{\delta})^{\frac{1}{n}}\leq\frac{\delta}{2}
.
Since
t\mapsto\widetilde{r}(x_{\delta},t)
is continuous in
[0,T]
, there exists
t_{\delta}>0
such that,
if
t\in(0,t_{\delta})
, then
\widetilde{r}(x_{\delta},t)<\widetilde{r}(x_{\delta},0)+\frac{\delta}{2}\leq\delta
. By (
6.8
),
\underline{r}(t)=\inf\{\widetilde{r}(x,t)\vcentcolon x>0\}
for each
t\in[0,T]
.
It follows that
\underline{r}(t)\leq\widetilde{r}(x_{\delta},t)<\delta
if
t\in(0,t_{\delta})
.
This shows that
\lim_{t\searrow 0}\underline{r}(t)=0
.

3. We now show that
t\mapsto\underline{r}(t)
is upper semi-continuous.
To start with, a function
f\vcentcolon{\mathbb{R}}\to{\mathbb{R}}
is upper semi-continuous if and only if
the set
\{x\in{\mathbb{R}}\vcentcolon f(x)<y\}
is open for all
y\in{\mathbb{R}}
.
Define the sets:

\displaystyle U_{\zeta}\vcentcolon=\{s\in(0,T)\vcentcolon\underline{r}(s)<\zeta\}\

\displaystyle\text{ for each $\zeta\in(0,\infty)$,}

\displaystyle U_{\zeta}^{x}\vcentcolon=\{s\in(0,T)\vcentcolon\widetilde{r}(x,s)<\zeta\}\

\displaystyle\text{ for each $(x,\zeta)\in(0,\infty)^{2}$.}

Suppose that
t\in U_{\zeta}
.
Since
\underline{r}(t)=\inf_{x>0}\widetilde{r}(x,t)
, there exists
\delta>0

such that
\zeta>\widetilde{r}(\delta,t)\geq\underline{r}(t)
.
Thus,
t\in U_{\zeta}^{\delta}\subseteq\cup_{x>0}U_{\zeta}^{x}
, which implies that

U_{\zeta}\subseteq\cup_{x>0}U_{\zeta}^{x}
.
On the other hand, if
t\in U_{\zeta}^{\delta}
for some
\delta>0
,
then
\zeta>\widetilde{r}(\delta,t)\geq\underline{r}(t)
so that
t\in U_{\zeta}
,
which implies that
U_{\zeta}=\cup_{x>0}U_{\zeta}^{x}
.
Since
t\mapsto\widetilde{r}(x,t)
is continuous,

U_{\zeta}^{x}
is an open set for each
x>0
.
Therefore,
U_{\zeta}
is also an open set as it is the union of open sets.
Since this is true for arbitrary
\zeta>0
,
and
\{s\in[0,T]\vcentcolon\underline{r}(s)<\zeta\}=\emptyset
for
\zeta\leq 0
,

t\mapsto\underline{r}(t)
is upper semi-continuous.

4. For each
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
, define

\mathring{\mathrm{F}}_{\varepsilon}\vcentcolon=\{(r,t)\in(0,\infty)\times(0,T)\,\vcentcolon\,\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)<r\}
.
Then
\mathring{\mathrm{F}}_{\varepsilon}
is an open set, since
t\mapsto\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)
is continuous in
t\in[0,T]
.
Since
\underline{r}(t)=\inf_{x>0}\widetilde{r}(x,t)
for all
t\in[0,T]
,
it follows that
\mathring{\mathrm{F}}_{\varepsilon}\subset\mathring{\mathrm{F}}
for each
\varepsilon>0
.
Furthermore, if
(r,t)\in\mathring{\mathrm{F}}
, then there exists
\varepsilon>0
such
that
\underline{r}(t)\leq\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)<r
, which implies
(r,t)\in\mathring{\mathrm{F}}_{\varepsilon}

so that
\mathring{\mathrm{F}}=\cup_{\varepsilon>0}\mathring{\mathrm{F}}_{\varepsilon}
.
Thus,
\mathring{\mathrm{F}}
is open as it is a union of open sets. This concludes the proof for Lemma
6.2
(iii)(iii)
.

5. We show the upper and lower bounds of
\widetilde{r}(x,t)
.
From Theorem
3.1
(iii)(iii)
, we have

nx\psi_{-}^{-1}(\frac{C_{0}}{x})\leq\widetilde{r}_{a}^{\,n}(x,t)\leq C_{0}(1+x)\qquad\ \text{for all $(a,x,t)\in(0,1)\times[0,\infty)\times[0,T]$,}

which, along with the uniform convergence in (
6.7
), implies Lemma
6.2
(iii)(iv)
.

6. We now prove Lemma
6.2
(iii)(v)
.
If
\underline{r}(t)>0
for some
t\in(0,T]
, then, for each
a\in(0,\underline{r}(t))
,
Theorem
3.1
(iii)(iii)
and (
6.3
) imply that

\displaystyle\Big\lvert\int_{a}^{\underline{r}(t)}\rho_{a}(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\Big\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\Big\lvert\int_{a}^{\widetilde{r}_{a}(x,t)}\rho_{a}(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\Big\rvert+\Big\lvert\int_{\widetilde{r}_{a}(x,t)}^{\widetilde{r}(x,t)}\rho_{a}(r,t)r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\Big\rvert+\Big\lvert\int_{\widetilde{r}(x,t)}^{\widetilde{r}(t)}\rho_{a}(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\Big\rvert

\displaystyle\leq x+\omega_{1}\big([\widetilde{r}(x,t),\widetilde{r}_{a}(x,t)];C(T)\big)+\omega_{1}\big([\underline{r}(t),\widetilde{r}(x,t)];C(T)\big),

where
\omega_{1}(E;C)\vcentcolon=f_{1}(\int_{E}r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r;C)
with

f_{1}(y;C)\vcentcolon=yG_{+}^{-1}(\frac{C}{y})
.
By Proposition
B.1
, for each fixed
C>0
,
y\mapsto f_{1}(y;C)
is a positive, monotone increasing,
continuous function with
\lim_{y\searrow 0}f_{1}(y;C)=0
.
Hence, given
\varepsilon>0
, there exists
\delta^{\prime}>0
such that

f_1(

\lvert\widetilde{r}^{\,n}(x,t)-\underline{r}^{\,n}(t)\rvert

n

;C)≤

ε

2

for
x\in(0,\delta^{\prime})
.

Let
\delta\vcentcolon=\min\{\delta^{\prime},\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\}
. We have

\int_{a}^{\underline{r}(t)}\rho_{a}(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\leq\varepsilon+f_{1}(\dfrac{\widetilde{r}_{a}^{\,n}(x,t)-\widetilde{r}^{\,n}(x,t)}{n};C(T))\qquad\mbox{if $x\in(0,\delta)$.}

Now, fix
x\in(0,\delta)
. It follows from (
6.7
) and
\lim_{y\searrow 0}f_{1}(y;C)=0
that

\limsup\limits_{a_{j}\searrow 0}\int_{a_{j}}^{\underline{r}(t)}\rho_{a_{j}}(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r=\varepsilon+\lim\limits_{a_{j}\searrow 0}f_{1}(\dfrac{(\widetilde{r}_{a_{j}}(x,t))^{n}-(\widetilde{r}(x,t))^{n}}{n};C(T))=\varepsilon.

Since this is true for arbitrarily small
\varepsilon>0
, taking
\varepsilon\searrow 0
yields

\limsup\limits_{j\to\infty}\int_{a_{j}}^{\underline{r}(t)}\rho_{a_{j}}(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r=0.

(6.9)

Since
\rho_{a}(r,t)\geq C^{-1}(a)
for
a.e.

(r,t)\in[a,\infty)\times[0,T]
from
Theorem
3.1
(iii)(ii)
,

\displaystyle\int_{a}^{\underline{r}(t)}\rho_{a}(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\geq\frac{1}{C(a)}\int_{a}^{\underline{r}(t)}r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r>0\qquad\text{for all $a\in(0,\underline{r}(t))$},

which, together with (
6.9
),
implies the first statement of Lemma
6.2
(iii)(v)
.
Since
\rho_{a}(r,t)>0
for each
a\in(0,1)
, by Fatou’s lemma,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\liminf\limits_{a_{j}\searrow 0}\rho_{a_{j}}(r,t)\mathbbm{1}_{(a_{j},\underline{r}(t))}(r)r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\leq\liminf\limits_{a_{j}\searrow 0}\int_{a_{j}}^{\underline{r}(t)}\rho_{a_{j}}(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r=0.

Thus,
\liminf_{j\to\infty}\rho_{a_{j}}(r,t)\mathbbm{1}_{(a_{j},\underline{r}(t))}(r)=0
for
a.e.

r\in(0,\underline{r}(t))
.
Now fix such
r\in(0,\underline{r}(t))
.
It follows that
\rho_{a_{j}}(r,t)=\rho_{a_{j}}(r,t)\mathbbm{1}_{(a_{j},\underline{r}(t))}(r)
for all
a_{j}\in(0,r)
.
Taking the limit inferior as
j\to\infty
on both sides, we obtain the second statement
of Lemma
6.2
(iii)(v)
.
∎

In what follows, we denote the fluid region
\mathrm{F}

as the set:

\displaystyle\mathrm{F}\vcentcolon=\{(r,t)\in(0,\infty)\times[0,T]\,\vcentcolon\,r>\underline{r}(t)\}.

6.3.
Compactness results in the exterior domain

In this subsection, we show the convergence of the approximate solutions in the exterior domain
and obtain several properties of the limit solutions.

Lemma 6.3
.

There exist both a subsequence
a_{j}\searrow 0
as
j\to\infty

and Hölder continuous functions

(u,e)\vcentcolon\mathrm{F}\backslash\{t=0\}\to\mathbb{R}\times[0,\infty)

such that, for any compact subset
K\Subset\mathrm{F}\backslash\{t=0\}
,

\lim\limits_{j\to\infty}\sup\limits_{(r,t)\in K}\lvert(u_{a_{j}}-u,e_{a_{j}}-e)(r,t)\rvert=0.

Moreover, for each
\varepsilon>0
, there exists
C(\varepsilon)>0
such that

(i)

If
t\in[0,T]
and
r\in[\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t),\infty)
,

\sigma^{\frac{1}{4}}(t)\lvert u(r,t)\rvert+\sigma^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)e(r,t)\leq C(\varepsilon);

(6.10)

(ii)

If
t\in(0,T]
and
r_{1},\,r_{2}\geq\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)
,

\sigma^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)\lvert u(r_{1},t)-u(r_{2},t)\rvert+\sigma(t)\lvert e(r_{1},t)-e(r_{2},t)\rvert\leq C(\varepsilon)\lvert r_{1}-r_{2}\rvert^{\frac{1}{2}};

(6.11)

(iii)

If
0<t_{1}<t_{2}\leq T
and
r\geq\sup_{t_{1}\leq t\leq t_{2}}\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)
,

\sigma^{\frac{1}{2}}(t_{1})\lvert u(r,t_{1})-u(r,t_{2})\rvert+\sigma(t_{1})\lvert e(r,t_{1})-e(r,t_{2})\rvert\leq C(\varepsilon)\lvert t_{2}-t_{1}\rvert^{\frac{1}{4}}.

(6.12)

(iv)

(u,e)(r,t)
satisfy

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)}^{\infty}\big(\lvert(u,e-1)\rvert^{2}+\lvert(\sqrt{\sigma}\partial_{r}u,\sigma\partial_{r}e)\rvert^{2}\big)(r,t)r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r

\displaystyle\,+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)}^{\infty}\big(\lvert(\partial_{r}u,\partial_{r}e,u\partial_{r}u)\rvert^{2}+\lvert(\sqrt{\sigma}\partial_{t}u,\sigma\partial_{t}e)\rvert^{2}\big)r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t\leq C(\varepsilon).

(6.13)

Proof.

We present the proof only for
u(r,t)
, since the case for
e(r,t)
follows in the same way.

First, fix
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
and define the domain:

\mathrm{F}_{\varepsilon}\vcentcolon=\big\{(r,t)\in(0,\infty)\times[0,T]\,\vcentcolon\,t\in[\varepsilon,T],\,\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)\leq r\leq\varepsilon^{-1}\big\}.

By Lemma
6.2
(iii)(iv)
, there exists
\varepsilon_{0}>0
such that

\text{Int}(\mathrm{F}_{\varepsilon})\neq\emptyset
for each
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})
.
Since
x\mapsto\widetilde{r}(x,t)
is strictly increasing, then
\mathrm{F}_{\varepsilon_{1}}\subset\mathrm{F}_{\varepsilon_{2}}
if
\varepsilon_{1}>\varepsilon_{2}
.

We claim:

\mathrm{F}\backslash\{t=0\}=\bigcup\limits_{0<\varepsilon<1}\mathrm{F}_{\varepsilon}.

(6.14)

This can be seen as follows:
If
(r,t)\in\mathrm{F}_{\varepsilon}
for some
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, then
r>\underline{r}(t)
since
\widetilde{r}(x,t)\searrow\underline{r}(t)

as
x\searrow 0
for each
t\in[0,T]
, which implies that
(r,t)\in\mathrm{F}
.
On the other hand, if
(r,t)\in\mathrm{F}\backslash\{t=0\}
, then
\underline{r}(t)<r
and
there exists
\varepsilon_{1}>0
such that
t>\varepsilon_{1}
.
Since
\lim_{x\searrow 0}\widetilde{r}(x,t)=\underline{r}(t)
for all
t\in[0,T]
, there exists
\varepsilon_{2}\in(0,1)

such that
\underline{r}(t)\leq\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon_{2},t)\leq r
.
Thus,
(r,t)\in\mathrm{F}_{\varepsilon}
for
\varepsilon\vcentcolon=\min\{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}\}
.
Then (
6.14
) follows.

Next, since
x\mapsto\widetilde{r}(x,t)
is strictly increasing for each
t\in[0,T]
,
and
(x,t)\mapsto\widetilde{r}(x,t)
is continuous, then

{\mathrm{D}}_{\varepsilon}\vcentcolon=\inf_{t\in[0,T]}\{\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)-\widetilde{r}(\frac{\varepsilon}{2},t)\}>0
by the compactness of
[0,T]
.
By Lemma
6.2
, there exists
N_{\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\lvert\widetilde{r}_{a_{j}}(\frac{\varepsilon}{2},t)-\widetilde{r}(\frac{\varepsilon}{2},t)\rvert\leq\frac{{\mathrm{D}}_{\varepsilon}}{2}
for all
j\geq N_{\varepsilon}
.
Hence,
\widetilde{r}_{a_{j}}(\frac{\varepsilon}{2},t)<\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)

for all
j\geq N_{\varepsilon}
and
t\in[0,T]
, which implies that,
if
(r,t)\in\mathrm{F}_{\varepsilon}
, then
\widetilde{r}_{a_{j}}(\frac{\varepsilon}{2},t)<r
for all
j\geq N_{\varepsilon}
.
Combining this with Theorem
3.1
(iii)(iv)
, we have

\begin{cases}\sup\limits_{j\geq N_{\varepsilon}}\lvert u_{a_{j}}(r_{1},t)-u_{a_{j}}(r_{2},t)\rvert\leq C(\frac{\varepsilon}{2})\sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)\lvert r_{1}-r_{2}\rvert^{\frac{1}{2}}&for all $(r_{1},t),(r_{2},t)\in\mathrm{F}_{\varepsilon}$,\\
\sup\limits_{j\geq N_{\varepsilon}}\lvert u_{a_{j}}(r,t_{1})-u_{a_{j}}(r,t_{2})\rvert\leq C(\frac{\varepsilon}{2})\sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t_{1})\lvert t_{2}-t_{1}\rvert^{\frac{1}{4}}&for all $(r,t_{1}),(r,t_{2})\in\mathrm{F}_{\varepsilon}$.\end{cases}

(6.15)

In addition, it also follows from Theorem
3.1
(iii)(iv)
that

\sup_{j\geq N_{\varepsilon}}\lvert u_{a_{j}}(r,t)\rvert\leq C(\varepsilon)\sigma^{-\frac{1}{4}}(t)\leq\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{4}}C(\varepsilon)\qquad\text{ for all $(r,t)\in\mathrm{F}_{\varepsilon}$}.

(6.16)

In light of (
6.14
)–(
6.16
), we can apply Proposition
A.1
to obtain a further
subsequence (still denoted)
a_{j}
and a continuous function

u(r,t)\vcentcolon\mathrm{F}\backslash\{t=0\}\to\mathbb{R}

such that

\lim\limits_{j\to\infty}\sup\limits_{(r,t)\in K}\lvert u_{a_{j}}(r,t)-u(r,t)\rvert=0\qquad\text{for all compact subset $K\Subset\mathrm{F}\backslash\{t=0\}$},

(6.17)

which together with Theorem
3.1
(iii)(iv)
implies (
6.10
)–(
6.12
).

Similarly, we can establish similar estimates for
e(r,t)
in the same way.

The proof for
(
6.13
) follows the same as that of Lemma
5.8
.
∎

Lemma 6.4
.

For each
\varepsilon>0
, define

\rho_{a}^{(\varepsilon)}(r,t)\vcentcolon=\rho_{a}(r,t)\chi_{\varepsilon}^{a}(r,t),\quad\rho^{(\varepsilon)}(r,t)\vcentcolon=\rho(r,t)\chi_{\varepsilon}(r,t),

where
\chi_{\varepsilon}^{a}
and
\chi_{\varepsilon}
are the indicator functions defined as

\chi_{\varepsilon}(r,t)\vcentcolon=\begin{cases}1&if $r\in[\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t),\infty),$\\
0&otherwise,\end{cases}\qquad\ \chi_{\varepsilon}^{a}(r,t)\vcentcolon=\begin{cases}1&if $r\in[\widetilde{r}_{a}(\varepsilon,t),\infty)$,\\
0&otherwise.\end{cases}

(6.18)

Then there exists
C(\varepsilon)>0
such that the following statements hold
:

(i)

There exist both a nonnegative function
\rho(r,t)\in L^{2}_{\text{\rm loc}}(\mathrm{F},r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t)

and a subsequence
a_{j}\searrow 0
as
j\to\infty
such that, for each
\varepsilon>0
,

\begin{cases}\rho_{a_{j}}^{(\varepsilon)}-1\overset{\ast}{\rightharpoonup}\rho^{(\varepsilon)}-1\qquad\text{in $L^{\infty}\big(0,T;L^{2}([0,\infty),r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)\big)$ \,\, as $j\to\infty$},\\
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)}^{\infty}\lvert\rho^{(\varepsilon)}-1\rvert^{2}(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\leq C(\varepsilon).\end{cases}

(ii)

For all
L\in\mathbb{N}
with
X_{L}\vcentcolon=\widetilde{H}^{-1}([0,L],r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)
,

\begin{cases}\rho^{(\varepsilon)}\in C^{0}([0,T];X_{L}),\qquad\lim\limits_{j\to\infty}\sup\limits_{t\in[0,T]}\lVert\rho_{a_{j}}^{(\varepsilon)}(\cdot,t)-\rho^{(\varepsilon)}(\cdot,t)\rVert_{X_{L}}=0,\\
\lVert\rho^{(\varepsilon)}(\cdot,t_{1})-\rho^{(\varepsilon)}(\cdot,t_{2})\rVert_{X_{L}}\leq C(\varepsilon)\lvert t_{1}-t_{2}\rvert\qquad\text{for all $t_{1},t_{2}\in[0,T]$}.\end{cases}

(iii)

For all
x>0
,

x=\int_{\underline{r}(t)}^{\widetilde{r}(x,t)}\rho(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r

for
t\in[0,T]
almost everywhere,
where
\widetilde{r}(x,t)
and
\underline{r}(t)
are the functions obtained in
Lemma
6.2
.

(iv)

C^{-1}(\varepsilon)\leq\rho(r,t)\leq C(\varepsilon)
for almost every
t\in[0,T]
and
r\in[\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t),\infty)
.

Proof.

We divide the proof into four steps, showing Lemma
6.4
(iii)(i)
–
(iii)(iv)

in chronological order.

1. Proof of Lemma
6.4
(iii)(i)
.
From Theorem
3.1
(iii)(iv)
, we obtain that, for all
j\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{0}^{\infty}\lvert\rho_{a_{j}}^{(\varepsilon)}-1\rvert^{2}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\leq\dfrac{\widetilde{r}_{a_{j}}^{\,n}(\varepsilon,t)}{n}+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{\widetilde{r}_{a_{j}}(\varepsilon,t)}^{\infty}\lvert\rho_{a_{j}}-1\rvert^{2}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\leq C(\varepsilon).

Then there exists
\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}-1\in L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}([0,\infty),r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r))
such that

\rho_{a_{j}}^{(\varepsilon)}-1\overset{\ast}{\rightharpoonup}\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}-1\qquad\text{weakly star in $L^{\infty}\big(0,T;L^{2}([0,\infty),r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)\big)\,\,$ as $j\to\infty$}.

(6.19)

For each
l\in\mathbb{N}
, denote
\varepsilon_{l}\vcentcolon=l^{-1}
and
\widetilde{w}^{(l)}\vcentcolon=\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon_{l}}
.
Fix
l,\,q\in\mathbb{N}
such that
l<q
.
Then
\widetilde{r}_{a}(\varepsilon_{q},t)<\widetilde{r}_{a}(\varepsilon_{l},t)
for each
(a,t)\in(0,1)\times[0,T]
.
Thus, by construction, it follows that, for all
j\in\mathbb{N}
,

\chi_{\varepsilon_{l}}^{a_{j}}(r,t)=1=\chi_{\varepsilon_{q}}^{a_{j}}(r,t)
if
r\in[\widetilde{r}_{a_{j}}(\varepsilon_{l},t),\infty)
so that

\rho_{a_{j}}^{(\varepsilon_{q})}(r,t)=\rho_{a_{j}}^{(\varepsilon_{l})}(r,t)\ \ \text{for {\it a.e.} $t\in[0,T]$},\qquad\,\,\,r\in[\widetilde{r}_{a_{j}}(\varepsilon_{l},t),\infty)\ \ \text{for each $j\in\mathbb{N}$}.

(6.20)

Let
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{\rm c}(\mathrm{F}^{(l)})
for

\mathrm{F}^{(l)}\vcentcolon=\{(r,t)\vcentcolon t\in[0,T],r\in[\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon_{l},t),\infty)\}
.
Then it follows that
\phi\chi_{\varepsilon_{l}}\in L^{1}(0,T;L^{2}([0,\infty),r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r))
and

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon_{l},t)}^{\infty}\{\widetilde{w}^{(q)}-\widetilde{w}^{(l)}\}\phi\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\{\widetilde{w}^{(q)}-\rho_{a_{j}}^{(\varepsilon_{q})}\}\chi_{\varepsilon_{l}}\phi\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon_{l},t)}^{\widetilde{r}_{a_{j}}(\varepsilon_{l},t)}\{\rho_{a_{j}}^{(\varepsilon_{q})}-\rho_{a_{j}}^{(\varepsilon_{l})}\}\phi\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\widetilde{r}_{a_{j}}(\varepsilon_{l},t)}^{\infty}\{\rho_{a_{j}}^{(\varepsilon_{q})}-\rho_{a_{j}}^{(\varepsilon_{l})}\}\phi\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\{\rho_{a_{j}}^{(\varepsilon_{l})}-\widetilde{w}^{(l)}\}\chi_{\varepsilon_{l}}\phi\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{4}I_{i}^{(j)}.

The limits of
I_{1}^{(j)}
and
I_{4}^{(j)}
are evaluated by the weak-star convergence (
6.19
).
As
j\to\infty
,

\displaystyle I_{1}^{(j)}+I_{4}^{(j)}\vcentcolon=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\{\widetilde{w}^{(q)}-\rho_{a_{j}}^{(\varepsilon_{q})}\}\chi_{\varepsilon_{l}}\phi\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\{\rho_{a_{j}}^{(\varepsilon_{l})}-\widetilde{w}^{(l)}\}\chi_{\varepsilon_{l}}\phi\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t\to 0.

Using (
6.20
), we also see that

\displaystyle I_{3}^{(j)}\vcentcolon=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\widetilde{r}_{a_{j}}(\varepsilon_{l},t)}^{\infty}\{\rho_{a_{j}}^{(\varepsilon_{q})}-\rho_{a_{j}}^{(\varepsilon_{l})}\}\phi\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t=0.

For
I_{2}^{(j)}
, observe that

\lvert\chi_{\varepsilon_{l}}^{a_{j}}-\chi_{\varepsilon_{l}}\rvert(r,t)=\begin{cases}1&if $r\in[\widetilde{r}_{a_{j}}(\varepsilon_{l},t),\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon_{l},t)]$ or $r\in[\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon_{l},t),\widetilde{r}_{a_{j}}(\varepsilon_{l},t)]$,\\
0&otherwise.\end{cases}

By the uniform convergence from Lemma
6.2
,
it follows that
\lvert\chi_{\varepsilon_{l}}^{a_{j}}-\chi_{\varepsilon_{l}}\rvert(r,t)\to 0
as
j\to\infty
for
a.e.

(r,t)\in[0,\infty)\times[0,T]
.
Using the estimates from Theorem
3.1
(iii)(iv)
and the dominated convergence theorem yield
that, as
j\to\infty
,

\displaystyle\lvert I_{2}^{(j)}\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\lvert\chi_{\varepsilon_{l}}^{a_{j}}-\chi_{\varepsilon_{l}}\rvert\lvert\rho_{a_{j}}^{(\varepsilon_{q})}-\rho_{a_{j}}^{(\varepsilon_{l})}\rvert\lvert\phi\rvert\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\Big(\int_{0}^{\infty}\lvert\chi_{\varepsilon_{l}}^{a_{j}}-\chi_{\varepsilon_{l}}\rvert^{2}\lvert\phi\rvert^{2}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\lVert\rho_{a_{j}}^{(\varepsilon_{q})}(\cdot,t)-1\rVert_{L^{2}}+\lVert\rho_{a_{j}}^{(\varepsilon_{l})}(\cdot,t)-1\rVert_{L^{2}}\big)\,{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\leq\big(C(\varepsilon_{l})+C(\varepsilon_{q})\big)\int_{0}^{T}\Big(\int_{0}^{\infty}\lvert\chi_{\varepsilon_{l}}^{a_{j}}-\chi_{\varepsilon_{l}}\rvert^{2}\lvert\phi\rvert^{2}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}{\mathrm{d}}t\to 0.

Combining the convergence for
I_{i}^{(j)}
as
j\to\infty
for
i=1,2,3,4
, it follows that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon_{l},t)}^{\infty}\{\widetilde{w}^{(q)}-\widetilde{w}^{(l)}\}\phi\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t=0\qquad\text{for all $\phi\in C^{\infty}_{\rm c}(\mathrm{F}^{(l)})$.}

It follows that, if
l\leq q\in\mathbb{N}
, then

\widetilde{w}^{(q)}(r,t)=\widetilde{w}^{(l)}(r,t)\qquad\text{for almost every $t\in[0,T]$ and $r\in[\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon_{l},t),\infty)$.}

(6.21)

In light of above,
\widetilde{w}^{(q)}(r,t)
is truncated in domain
\mathrm{F}
for each
q\in\mathbb{N}
as

w^{(q)}(r,t)\vcentcolon=\begin{cases}\widetilde{w}^{(q)}(r,t)&if $t\in[0,T]$ and $r\in[\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon_{q},t),\infty)$,\\
0&otherwise.\end{cases}

Using this, for each
N\in\mathbb{N}
, define

h_{N}(r,t)\vcentcolon=w^{(1)}(r,t)+\sum_{i=1}^{N}\{w^{(i+1)}(r,t)-w^{(i)}(r,t)\}\qquad\text{for $(r,t)\in\mathrm{F}$.}

Fix
(r,t)\in\mathrm{F}
.
Lemma
6.2
(iii)(iii)
–
(iii)(iv)
imply that

\lim_{x\searrow 0}\widetilde{r}(x,t)=\underline{r}(t)
and
\lim_{x\to\infty}\widetilde{r}(x,t)=\infty
.
Since
\underline{r}(t)<r
, there exists
q\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon_{q},t)\leq r<\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon_{q-1},t)
.
Then (
6.21
) implies

h_{N}(r,t)=w^{(1)}(r,t)+\sum_{i=1}^{N}\{w^{(i+1)}(r,t)-w^{(i)}(r,t)\}=w^{(q)}(r,t)=\widetilde{w}^{(q)}(r,t)\quad\text{ if $N\geq q+1$.}

Thus, for
a.e.

(r,t)\in\mathrm{F}
,

\lim_{N\to\infty}h_{N}(r,t)=\widetilde{w}^{(q)}(r,t)
exists for some
q\in\mathbb{N}
,
and the following function is well-defined almost everywhere in
(0,\infty)\times[0,T]
:

\rho(r,t)\vcentcolon=\begin{cases}\lim\limits_{N\to\infty}h_{N}(r,t)&for {\it a.e.} $(r,t)\in\mathrm{F}$,\\
0&for $(r,t)\in(0,\infty)\times[0,T]\backslash\mathrm{F}$.\end{cases}

Now let
\varepsilon>0
. Then there exists some integer
q\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\varepsilon_{q}\leq\varepsilon
and

\rho^{(\varepsilon)}(r,t)=\chi_{\varepsilon}(r,t)\rho(r,t)=\chi_{\varepsilon}(r,t)\widetilde{w}^{(q)}(r,t)\quad\ \text{for {\it a.e.} $(r,t)\in(0,\infty)\times[0,T]$},

from the above construction.

Let
\phi\in L^{1}(0,T;L^{2}([0,\infty);r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r))
. Using
\widetilde{r}_{a_{j}}(\varepsilon_{q},t)<\widetilde{r}_{a_{j}}(\varepsilon,t)
for each
j\in\mathbb{N}
, one has

\chi_{\varepsilon}^{(a_{j})}=\chi_{\varepsilon_{q}}^{(a_{j})}\chi_{\varepsilon}^{(a_{j})}\qquad\text{for all $(r,t)\in[0,\infty)\times[0,T]$ and $j\in\mathbb{N}$.}

(6.22)

Using this, it then follows that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\{\rho^{(\varepsilon)}-\rho_{a_{j}}^{(\varepsilon)}\}\phi\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\chi_{\varepsilon}\phi\{\widetilde{w}^{(q)}-\rho_{a_{j}}^{(\varepsilon_{q})}\}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\{\chi_{\varepsilon}-\chi_{\varepsilon}^{a_{j}}\}\rho_{a_{j}}^{(\varepsilon_{q})}\phi\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle=\vcentcolon J_{1}^{(j)}+J_{2}^{(j)}.

By the weak-star convergence for each
q\in\mathbb{N}
in (
6.19
),

J_{1}^{(j)}\to 0
as
j\to\infty
.
For
J_{2}^{(j)}
, one uses the pointwise bound of
\rho_{a}
from Theorem
3.1
(iii)(iv)
to obtain

\lvert(\chi_{\varepsilon}-\chi_{\varepsilon}^{a_{j}})\rho_{a_{j}}^{(\varepsilon_{q})}\varphi\rvert\leq C(\varepsilon_{q})\lvert\varphi\rvert\in L^{1}\big(0,T;L^{2}([0,\infty),r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)\big).

Moreover, due to the uniform convergence from Lemma
6.2
,

(\chi_{\varepsilon}-\chi_{\varepsilon}^{(a_{j})})(r,t)\to 0
as
j\to\infty
for
a.e.

(r,t)\in[0,\infty)\times[0,T]
.
Hence, by the dominated convergence theorem,

\displaystyle J_{2}^{(j)}\vcentcolon=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}(\chi_{\varepsilon}-\chi_{\varepsilon}^{a_{j}})\rho_{a_{j}}^{(\varepsilon_{q})}\varphi\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t\to 0\qquad\text{as $j\to\infty$.}

Combining the limits of
J_{1}^{(j)}
and
J_{2}^{(j)}
as
j\to\infty
, it follows that there exists

\rho\in L^{2}_{\text{loc}}(\mathrm{F})
such that, for each
\varepsilon>0
,

\rho^{(\varepsilon)}_{a_{j}}\overset{\ast}{\rightharpoonup}\rho^{(\varepsilon)}\qquad\text{in $L^{\infty}\big(0,T;L^{2}([0,\infty),r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)\big)\,\,$ as $j\to\infty$}.

(6.23)

This completes the proof
of Lemma
6.4
(iii)(i)
.

2. Proof of Lemma
6.4
(iii)(ii)
.
Define
r_{\varepsilon}\vcentcolon=\frac{1}{2}\inf_{t\in[0,T]}\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)
.
By Lemma
6.2
, there exists
N_{\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{N}
such that

r_{\varepsilon}<\widetilde{r}_{a_{j}}(\varepsilon,t)
for all
j\geq N_{\varepsilon}
and
t\in[0,T]
.
By Theorem
3.1
(iii)(v)
, we see that, for each
\varepsilon>0
and
t_{1},t_{2}\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{j\geq N_{\varepsilon}}\lVert\rho_{a_{j}}^{(\varepsilon)}(\cdot,t_{1})-\rho_{a_{j}}^{(\varepsilon)}(\cdot,t_{2})\rVert_{\widetilde{H}^{-1}([r_{\varepsilon},L],r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)}\leq C(\varepsilon)\lvert t_{1}-t_{2}\rvert\qquad\text{for all $L\in\mathbb{N}$.}

Moreover, it follows that, for all
j\geq N_{\varepsilon}
and
L\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\rho_{a_{j}}^{(\varepsilon)}\in C^{0}\big([0,T];\widetilde{H}^{-1}([r_{\varepsilon},L],r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)\big),\quad\sup_{j\geq N_{\varepsilon}}\lVert\rho_{a_{j}}^{(\varepsilon)}-1\rVert_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}([r_{\varepsilon},\infty),r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r))}\leq C(\varepsilon).

Therefore, by Proposition
A.2
,
there exists
\widetilde{h}_{\varepsilon}
such that, for all
L\in\mathbb{N}
,

\begin{cases}\widetilde{h}_{\varepsilon}-1\in L^{\infty}\big(0,T;L^{2}([r_{\varepsilon},\infty),r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)\big),\quad\widetilde{h}_{\varepsilon}\in C^{0}\big([0,T];\widetilde{H}^{-1}([r_{\varepsilon},L],r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)\big),\\
\lim\limits_{j\to\infty}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\lVert\rho_{a_{j}}^{(\varepsilon)}(\cdot,t)-\widetilde{h}_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,t)\rVert_{H^{-1}([r_{\varepsilon},L],r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)}=0,\\
\lVert\widetilde{h}_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,t_{1})-\widetilde{h}_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,t_{2})\rVert_{H^{-1}([r_{\varepsilon},L],r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)}\leq C(\varepsilon)\lvert t_{1}-t_{2}\rvert\quad\text{ for each $t_{1},t_{2}\in[0,T]$.}\end{cases}

(6.24)

We claim that
\rho^{(\varepsilon)}=\widetilde{h}_{\varepsilon}
for
a.e.

(r,t)\in[r_{\varepsilon},\infty)\times[0,T]
.
Let
\phi\in C_{c}^{\infty}([r_{\varepsilon},\infty)\times[0,T])
. Then there exists
L\in\mathbb{N}
such that
{\mathrm{supp}}(\phi)\subseteq[r_{\varepsilon},L]\times[0,T]
. Using (
6.23
)–(
6.24
), we obtain that, as
j\to\infty
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{r_{\varepsilon}}^{\infty}(\rho^{(\varepsilon)}-\widetilde{h}_{\varepsilon})\phi\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{r_{\varepsilon}}^{\infty}(\rho^{(\varepsilon)}-\rho^{(\varepsilon)}_{a_{j}})\phi\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{r_{\varepsilon}}^{L}(\rho_{a_{j}}^{(\varepsilon)}-\widetilde{h}_{\varepsilon})\phi\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\int_{r_{\varepsilon}}^{\infty}\{\rho^{(\varepsilon)}-\rho^{(\varepsilon)}_{a_{j}}\}\phi\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t+\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\rho_{a_{j}}^{(\varepsilon)}(\cdot,t)-\widetilde{h}_{\varepsilon}(\cdot,t)\rVert_{\widetilde{H}^{-1}}\lVert\phi\rVert_{H^{1}}{\mathrm{d}}t\to 0.

This implies that

\rho^{(\varepsilon)}=\widetilde{h}_{\varepsilon}
for
a.e.

(r,t)\in[r_{\varepsilon},\infty)\times[0,T]
.
Lemma
6.4
(iii)(ii)
then follows since
{\mathrm{supp}}(\rho^{(\varepsilon)}(\cdot,t))=[\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t),\infty)
,
and
r_{\varepsilon}<\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)
for all
t\in[0,T]
.

3. Proof of Lemma
6.4
(iii)(iii)
.
Let
\xi\in C^{\infty}_{\rm c}(0,T)
and
0<y<x
. By (
6.23
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\xi(t)\Big(\int_{\widetilde{r}_{a_{j}}(y,t)}^{\widetilde{r}(x,t)}\rho_{a_{j}}(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\Big){\mathrm{d}}t-\int_{0}^{T}\xi(t)\Big(\int_{\widetilde{r}(y,t)}^{\widetilde{r}(x,t)}\rho(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\Big){\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\widetilde{r}(x,t)}\xi(\chi_{y}^{a_{j}}\rho_{a_{j}}-\chi_{y}\rho)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}(1-\chi_{x})\xi\{\rho_{a_{j}}^{(y)}-\rho^{(y)}\}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t\to 0

as
j\to\infty
, where we have used the fact that
(1-\chi_{x})\xi\in L^{1}(0,T;L^{2}([0,\infty),r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r))
.
Then

\lim\limits_{j\to\infty}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\widetilde{r}_{a_{j}}(y,t)}^{\widetilde{r}(x,t)}\xi(t)\rho_{a_{j}}(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t=\int_{0}^{T}\xi(t)\Big(\int_{\widetilde{r}(y,t)}^{\widetilde{r}(x,t)}\rho(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\Big){\mathrm{d}}t.

(6.25)

Moreover, using (
6.3
),
\lim_{y\searrow 0}\widetilde{r}(y,t)=\underline{r}(t)
, and the dominated convergence theorem,

\lim\limits_{y\searrow 0}\sup\limits_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\Big\lvert\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\underline{r}(t)}^{\widetilde{r}(y,t)}\xi\rho_{a_{j}}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t\Big\rvert\leq\lim\limits_{y\searrow 0}\int_{0}^{T}\omega_{1}([\underline{r}(t),\widetilde{r}(y,t)];C(T))\lvert\xi(t)\rvert\,{\mathrm{d}}t=0.

For a given
\delta>0
, the above limit implies that there exists
z_{\delta}>0
such that, if
y\in(0,z_{\delta})
, then

\sup_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\Big\lvert\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\underline{r}(t)}^{\widetilde{r}(y,t)}\xi(t)\rho_{a_{j}}(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t\Big\rvert\leq\delta.

(6.26)

Now fix
y\in(0,z_{\delta})
. By Theorem
3.1
(iii)(iii)
, for
a.e.

t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle x=\int_{a_{j}}^{\widetilde{r}_{a_{j}}(x,t)}\rho_{a_{j}}(r,t)r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r=\Big(\int_{a_{j}}^{\underline{r}(t)}+\int_{\underline{r}(t)}^{\widetilde{r}(y,t)}+\int_{\widetilde{r}(y,t)}^{\widetilde{r}_{a_{j}}(y,t)}+\int_{\widetilde{r}_{a_{j}}(y,t)}^{\widetilde{r}(x,t)}+\int_{\widetilde{r}(x,t)}^{\widetilde{r}_{a_{j}}(x,t)}\Big)\rho_{a_{j}}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r.

Multiplying the above equality with
\xi(t)
, then integrating in
t\in[0,T]
and letting
j\to\infty
,
it follows from (
6.25
)–(
6.26
) that

-\delta\leq\lim\limits_{j\to\infty}\sum_{i=1}^{3}\mathcal{I}_{i}^{(j)}+\int_{0}^{T}\xi(t)\Big\{-x+\int_{\widetilde{r}(y,t)}^{\widetilde{r}(x,t)}\rho(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\Big\}\,{\mathrm{d}}t\leq\delta.

(6.27)

Repeating the same proof for Lemma
6.2
(iii)(v)
, we have

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{j\to\infty}\lvert\mathcal{I}_{1}^{(j)}\rvert=\lim\limits_{j\to\infty}\Big\lvert\int_{0}^{T}\xi(t)\big(\int_{a_{j}}^{\underline{r}(t)}\rho_{a_{j}}(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\big){\mathrm{d}}t\Big\rvert=0.

Moreover, using (
6.3
), Lemma
6.2
, and the dominated convergence theorem,
we see that

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{j\to\infty}\lvert\mathcal{I}_{2}^{(j)}\rvert+\lvert\mathcal{I}_{3}^{(j)}\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\lim\limits_{j\to\infty}\int_{0}^{T}\lvert\xi(t)\rvert\Big(\int_{\widetilde{r}(y,t)}^{\widetilde{r}_{a_{j}}(y,t)}+\int_{\widetilde{r}(x,t)}^{\widetilde{r}_{a_{j}}(x,t)}\Big)\rho_{a_{j}}(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\leq\lim\limits_{j\to\infty}\int_{0}^{T}\lvert\xi(t)\rvert\Big\{\omega_{1}\big([\widetilde{r}(y,t),\widetilde{r}_{a_{j}}(y,t)];C(T)\big)+\omega_{1}\big([\widetilde{r}(x,t),\widetilde{r}_{a_{j}}(x,t)];C(T)\big)\Big\}\,{\mathrm{d}}t=0.

It follows from (
6.27
) that, for each
\delta>0
, there exists
z_{\delta}>0
such that, if
y\in(0,z_{\delta})
, then

-\delta\leq\int_{0}^{T}\xi(t)\Big\{-x+\int_{\widetilde{r}(y,t)}^{\widetilde{r}(x,t)}\rho(r,t)r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\Big\}\,{\mathrm{d}}t\leq\delta.

Let
\{\xi_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\in C^{\infty}_{\rm c}(0,T)
be such that
\xi_{k}\geq 0
,

\lVert\xi_{k}\rVert_{L^{1}([0,T])}=1
, and
\{\xi_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
is a sequence of kernels approximating
the Dirac measure.
Then, for all
(k,y)\in\mathbb{N}\times(0,z_{\delta})
,

\displaystyle-\delta\leq-x+\int_{0}^{T}\xi_{k}(t)\Big(\int_{\widetilde{r}(y,t)}^{\widetilde{r}(x,t)}\rho(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\Big){\mathrm{d}}t\leq\delta.

Since
\rho(r,t)\geq 0
, we apply the monotone convergence theorem for
y\searrow 0
to obtain

\displaystyle-\delta\leq-x+\int_{0}^{T}\xi_{k}(t)\Big(\int_{\underline{r}(t)}^{\widetilde{r}(x,t)}\rho(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\Big)\,{\mathrm{d}}t\leq\delta.

Since this is true for arbitrarily small
\delta>0
, it follows that

\displaystyle x=\int_{0}^{T}\xi_{k}(t)\Big(\int_{\underline{r}(t)}^{\widetilde{r}(x,t)}\rho(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\Big)\,{\mathrm{d}}t\qquad\ \text{for each $i\in\mathbb{N}$}.

Finally, since
\xi_{k}
converges to the Dirac measure in the sense of distributions, we take
k\to\infty
to
conclude that

\displaystyle x=\int_{\underline{r}(t)}^{\widetilde{r}(x,t)}\rho(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\qquad\ \text{for $t\in[0,T]$ almost everywhere.}

4. The proof for
(iii)(iv)

is the same as that of Proposition
5.1
and Lemma
5.5
.
∎

In light of Lemmas
6.2
(iii)(v)
and
6.4
(iii)(iii)
,
we extend
\rho\in L^{2}_{\text{loc}}(\mathrm{F})
obtained above by setting
\rho(r,t)=0

for
(r,t)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\times[0,T]\backslash\mathrm{F}
.

Lemma 6.5
.

Let
(\rho,u,e)(r,t)
be the limit function obtained in
Lemmas
6.3
–
6.4
.
Then

\displaystyle\esssup_{t\in(0,T)}\int_{0}^{\infty}\big(\rho\lvert u\rvert^{2}+G(\rho)\big)(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\leq C(T),

\displaystyle\esssup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{E}(\rho e)(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\leq C(T)+\omega_{2}(E;C(T))\quad\text{for bounded measurable $E\subset[0,\infty)$,}

where
\omega_{2}(E,z)
for
z>0
is the set function given in
Lemma
6.1
.

Proof.

Let
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
. Denote
\rho^{(\varepsilon)}
and
\rho^{(\varepsilon)}_{a_{j}}
as in Lemma
6.4
.
Let
E\subset[0,\infty)
be a bounded measurable set, and let
\mathbbm{1}_{E}(r)
be the indicator function.
Since
x\to\widetilde{r}(x,t)
is strictly increasing and
(x,t)\mapsto\widetilde{r}(x,t)
is a continuous function from Lemma
6.2
, combining with the fact that
[0,T]
is a compact set yields that

{\mathrm{D}}_{\varepsilon}=\vcentcolon 2\inf_{t\in[0,T]}\{\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)-\widetilde{r}(\frac{\varepsilon}{2},t)\}>0
.
By Lemma
6.2
, there exists
N_{\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{N}
such that,
if
j\geq N_{\varepsilon}
,
\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\lvert\widetilde{r}_{a_{j}}(\varepsilon,t)-\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)\rvert\leq\frac{d_{\varepsilon}}{2}
.
Then

\widetilde{r}_{a_{j}}(\varepsilon,t)>\widetilde{r}(\frac{\varepsilon}{2},t)\qquad\ \text{for all $t\in[0,T]$ and $j\geq N_{\varepsilon}$}.

(6.28)

Let
[t_{1},t_{2}]\subset(0,T]
. Then it follows from (
6.28
) that, as
j\to\infty
,

\displaystyle\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\widetilde{r}_{a_{j}}(\varepsilon,t)}^{\infty}\rho_{a_{j}}\lvert u_{a_{j}}\rvert^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{E}(r)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t-\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)}^{\infty}\rho\lvert u\rvert^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{E}(r)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle=\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\widetilde{r}(\frac{\varepsilon}{2},t)}^{\infty}\rho_{a_{j}}^{(\varepsilon)}\lvert u_{a_{j}}\rvert^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{E}(r)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t-\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\widetilde{r}(\frac{\varepsilon}{2},t)}^{\infty}\rho^{(\varepsilon)}\lvert u\rvert^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{E}(r)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle=\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\widetilde{r}(\frac{\varepsilon}{2},t)}^{\infty}\rho_{a_{j}}^{(\varepsilon)}\big\{\lvert u_{a_{j}}\rvert^{2}-\lvert u\rvert^{2}\big\}\mathbbm{1}_{E}(r)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t+\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\widetilde{r}(\frac{\varepsilon}{2},t)}^{\infty}\mathbbm{1}_{E}\big\{\rho_{a_{j}}^{(\varepsilon)}-\rho^{(\varepsilon)}\big\}\lvert u\rvert^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{E}(r)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t\to 0,

where the first-term convergence follows from the dominated convergence theorem,
Lemma
6.3
, and the pointwise bound for
\lvert u_{a_{j}}\rvert
and
\rho_{a_{j}}^{(\varepsilon)}

from Theorem
3.1
(iii)(iv)
, while the second-term convergence follows from
Lemma
6.4
.

Let
L\in\mathbb{N}
and set
E\equiv[0,L]
. By Theorem
3.1
(iii)(i)
,

\displaystyle\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)}^{L}\rho\lvert u\rvert^{2}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t=

\displaystyle\,\lim_{j\to\infty}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\widetilde{r}_{a_{j}}(\varepsilon,t)}^{L}\rho_{a_{j}}\lvert u_{a_{j}}\rvert^{2}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,\lvert t_{2}-t_{1}\rvert\esssup_{t\in(0,T)}\int_{a_{j}}^{\infty}\rho_{a_{j}}\lvert u_{a_{j}}\rvert^{2}(r,t)r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r=C(T)\lvert t_{2}-t_{1}\rvert,

where
C(T)=C(T,C_{0})
is independent of
a\in(0,1)
,
\varepsilon>0
, and
L\in\mathbb{N}
. Thus,
by the Lebesgue differentiation theorem, it follows that

\displaystyle\esssup_{t\in(0,T)}\int_{\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)}^{L}(\rho\lvert u\rvert^{2})(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\leq C(T).

Using the monotone convergence theorem with the consecutive limits
L\to\infty
and then
\varepsilon\searrow 0
,

\displaystyle\esssup_{t\in(0,T)}\int_{\underline{r}(t)}^{\infty}(\rho\lvert u\rvert^{2})(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r=\lim\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}L\to\infty\\
\varepsilon\to 0^{+}\end{subarray}}\esssup_{t\in(0,T)}\int_{\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)}^{L}(\rho\lvert u\rvert^{2})(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\leq C(T).

Next, let
[t_{1},t_{2}]\subseteq(0,T]
, and let
E\subset[0,\infty)
be a bounded measurable set.
Using (
6.28
), the convergence results from Lemma
6.3
–
6.4
,
and the bound:
e(r,t)\leq C(\varepsilon)\sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)
for
r\geq\widetilde{r}(\frac{\varepsilon}{2},t)
from Lemma
6.3
,
we see that, as
j\to\infty
,

\displaystyle\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\widetilde{r}_{a_{j}}(\varepsilon,t)}^{\infty}\rho_{a_{j}}e_{a_{j}}\mathbbm{1}_{E}(r)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t-\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)}^{\infty}\rho e\,\mathbbm{1}_{E}(r)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle=\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\widetilde{r}(\frac{\varepsilon}{2},t)}^{\infty}\rho_{a_{j}}^{(\varepsilon)}e_{a_{j}}\mathbbm{1}_{E}(r)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t-\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\widetilde{r}(\frac{\varepsilon}{2},t)}^{\infty}\rho^{(\varepsilon)}e\,\mathbbm{1}_{E}(r)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle=\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\widetilde{r}(\frac{\varepsilon}{2},t)}^{\infty}\rho_{a_{j}}^{(\varepsilon)}(e_{a_{j}}-e)\mathbbm{1}_{E}(r)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t+\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\widetilde{r}(\frac{\varepsilon}{2},t)}^{\infty}\big(\rho_{a_{j}}^{(\varepsilon)}-\rho^{(\varepsilon)}\big)e\,\mathbbm{1}_{E}(r)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t\to 0.

Let
E_{j}^{\varepsilon}(t)\vcentcolon=E\cap[\widetilde{r}_{a_{j}}(\varepsilon,t),\infty)
. Then, by (
6.4
),

\displaystyle\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)}^{\infty}\rho e\,\mathbbm{1}_{E}(r)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle=\lim\limits_{j\to\infty}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\widetilde{r}_{a_{j}}(\varepsilon,t)}^{\infty}\rho_{a_{j}}e_{a_{j}}\,\mathbbm{1}_{E}(r)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\leq\sup\limits_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{E_{j}^{\varepsilon}(t)}\rho_{a_{j}}e_{a_{j}}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\leq\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\big(C(T)+\omega_{2}(E_{j}^{\varepsilon}(t);C(T))\big){\mathrm{d}}t,

where
\omega_{2}\big(E_{j}^{\varepsilon}(t);C(T)\big)=f_{2}(f_{1}(\int_{E_{j}^{\varepsilon}(t)}r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r;C(T));C(T))
.

Since
E_{j}^{\varepsilon}(t)\subseteq E
for all
(j,\varepsilon,t)\in\mathbb{N}\times(0,1)\times[0,T]
,
and
f_{1}(y;C)
and
f_{2}(y;C)
are monotone increasing functions in
y
(see Proposition
B.1
),
then, for all
(j,\varepsilon,t)\in\mathbb{N}\times(0,1)\times[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\omega_{2}(E_{j}^{\varepsilon}(t);C(T))\leq f_{2}(f_{1}(\int_{E}r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r;C(T));C(T))=\omega_{2}(E;C(T)).

Using this, (
6.3
) becomes

\displaystyle\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)}^{\infty}(\rho e)(r,t)\,\mathbbm{1}_{E}(r)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t\leq\lvert t_{2}-t_{1}\rvert\big(C(T)+\omega_{2}(E;C(T))\big).

Thus, by the Lebesgue differentiation theorem, it follows that

\displaystyle\esssup\limits_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)}^{\infty}(\rho e)(r,t)\mathbbm{1}_{E}(r)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\leq C(T)+\omega_{2}(E;C(T)).

Since
\rho=0
in
\{r<\underline{r}(t)\}
and
\lim_{\varepsilon\searrow 0}\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)=\underline{r}(t)
,
by the monotone convergence theorem,

\displaystyle\esssup\limits_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{E}(\rho e)(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\leq C(T)+\omega_{2}(E;C(T)).

Let
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
and
r_{\varepsilon}\vcentcolon=\frac{1}{2}\inf_{t\in[0,T]}\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)
.
By Lemma
6.2
, there exists
M_{\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\inf_{t\in[0,T]}\widetilde{r}_{a_{j}}(\varepsilon,t)>r_{\varepsilon}\qquad\ \text{for all $j\geq M_{\varepsilon}$}.

(6.29)

Moreover, by Theorem
3.1
(iii)(iii)
,
\widetilde{r}_{a_{j}}(\varepsilon,t)\leq\widetilde{r}_{a_{j}}(1,t)\leq C_{0}

for all
(j,t)\in\mathbb{N}\times[0,T]
.
By (
6.29
), Theorem
3.1
(iii)(i)
, Lemma
6.4
,
and the fact that
G(0)=1
, we have

\begin{cases}\rho_{a_{j}}^{(\varepsilon)}-1\rightharpoonup\rho^{(\varepsilon)}-1\quad\text{ weakly in $L^{2}\big(0,T;L^{2}([r_{\varepsilon},\infty),r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)\big)$ as $j\to\infty$},\\
\sup_{j\geq M_{\varepsilon}}\esssup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{r_{\varepsilon}}^{\infty}G(\rho_{a_{j}}^{(\varepsilon)})(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\leq C(T),\\
0\leq\rho_{a_{j}}^{(\varepsilon)}(r,t)\leq C(\varepsilon)\quad\text{ for {\it a.e.}\, $(r,t)\in[r_{\varepsilon},\infty)\times[0,T]$
and for each $j\in\mathbb{N}$}.\end{cases}

Thus, applying Proposition
B.2
,
it follows that

\esssup_{t\in(0,T)}\int_{r_{\varepsilon}}^{\infty}G(\rho^{(\varepsilon)})(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\leq C(T).

By Lemma
6.2
(iii)(iv)
,

r_{\varepsilon}<\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)\leq C_{0}(1+\varepsilon)^{\frac{1}{n}}\leq 2^{\frac{1}{n}}C_{0}
for
t\in[0,T]
.
Since
G(\rho^{(\varepsilon)})=G(0)=1
for
r\in[r_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)]
and
\rho^{(\varepsilon)}=\rho

for
a.e.

r\in[\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t),\infty)
, then, for
a.e.

t\in[0,T]
,

\int_{\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)}^{\infty}G(\rho)(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\leq\int_{r_{\varepsilon}}^{\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r+\int_{\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)}^{\infty}G(\rho)(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\leq C(T).

Since the above is true for all
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
,
using
\lim_{\varepsilon\searrow 0}\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)=\underline{r}(t)
,
\rho\equiv 0
for
r<\underline{r}(t)
,
and the monotone convergence Theorem, it follows that

\esssup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{0}^{\infty}G(\rho)(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r=\lim\limits_{\varepsilon\searrow 0}\esssup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)}^{\infty}G(\rho)(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\leq C(T).

∎

Lemma 6.6
.

Let
(\rho,u,e)(r,t)
be the limit function obtained in
Lemmas
6.3
–
6.4
. Then for
\eta>0
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\sup_{r\geq\underline{r}(t)+\eta}\dfrac{\lvert u\rvert}{\sqrt{e}}(r,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}t\leq C(T)\big(\eta^{\frac{2-n}{2}}+\eta^{2-n}\big)

\displaystyle\text{if }\ n=2,\,3,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\sup_{r\geq\underline{r}(t)+\eta}\log\big(\max\big\{1,e(r,t)\big\}\big)\,{\mathrm{d}}t\leq C(T)\big(1+\sqrt{|\log\eta|}\big)

\displaystyle\text{if }\ n=2,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\sup_{r\geq\underline{r}(t)+\eta}\log\big(\max\big\{1,e^{\pm 1}(r,t)\big\}\big)\,{\mathrm{d}}t\leq C(T)\eta^{2-n}

\displaystyle\text{if }\ n=3.

Proof.

We will only prove the first estimate, as the other two statements follow the exact same proof. Fix
\eta>0
. Since
a_{j}\searrow 0
as
j\to\infty
, there exists
N_{1}\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\eta>a_{j}
for
j\geq N_{1}
. Let
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
. It follows from Theorem
3.1
(iii)(vi)
that,

\int_{0}^{T}\sup\limits_{r\geq\widetilde{r}(2\varepsilon,t)+\eta}\dfrac{|u_{a_{j}}|}{\sqrt{e_{a_{j}}}}(r,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}t\leq\int_{0}^{T}\sup\limits_{r\geq\eta}\dfrac{|u_{a_{j}}|}{\sqrt{e_{a_{j}}}}(r,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}t\leq C(T)\big(\eta^{\frac{2-n}{2}}+\eta^{2-n}\big)\qquad\text{for all }\ j\geq N_{1}.

By Lemma
6.2
(iii)(i)
and the fact that
\varepsilon\mapsto\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)
is increasing,
there exists
N_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\widetilde{r}(2\varepsilon,t)\geq\widetilde{r}_{a_{j}}(\varepsilon,t)
for all
j\geq N_{2}
.
Thus, by the uniform convergence in Lemma
6.3
and Fatou’s lemma, we have

\int_{0}^{T}\sup\limits_{r\geq\widetilde{r}(2\varepsilon,t)+\eta}\dfrac{|u|}{\sqrt{e}}(r,t)\,{\mathrm{d}}t\leq C(T)\big(\eta^{\frac{2-n}{2}}+\eta^{2-n}\big).

Finally, since
\widetilde{r}(\varepsilon,t)\searrow\underline{r}(t)
as
\varepsilon\searrow 0
, the desired estimate is obtained by an application of Monotone Convergence theorem with the limit
\varepsilon\searrow 0
.
∎

6.4.
Weak forms away from the vacuum

In this subsection, we denote
(\rho,u,e)(r,t)
on
\mathrm{F}
and
\widetilde{r}(x,t)
on
[0,\infty)\times[0,T]

as the limit functions obtained in Lemmas
6.2
–
6.4
.
The main aim is to show that they satisfy the weak forms described in Definition
2.1
.
Moreover, we also define a class of test functions
\mathcal{D}_{T}
as

\mathcal{D}_{T}\vcentcolon=\{\phi\in C^{1}([0,\infty)\times[0,T])\,\vcentcolon\,\exists L\in\mathbb{N}\text{ such that }\phi(r,t)=0\text{ for $(r,t)\in[L,\infty)\times[0,T]$}\}.

Lemma 6.7
.

Let
(\rho_{0},u_{0},e_{0})(r)
be the spherically symmetric initial data in
Theorem
2.1
,
and let
(\rho_{a}^{0},u_{a}^{0},e_{a}^{0})(r)
be the modified initial data constructed in
§
3.2
.
Then, for all
\phi\in\mathcal{D}_{T}
,

\begin{split}&\lim\limits_{a\searrow 0}\int_{a}^{\infty}\rho_{a}^{0}(r)\phi(r,0)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r=\int_{0}^{\infty}\rho_{0}(r)\phi(r,0)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r,\\
&\lim\limits_{a\searrow 0}\int_{a}^{\infty}(\rho_{a}^{0}u_{a}^{0})(r)\phi(r,0)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r=\int_{0}^{\infty}(\rho_{0}u_{0})(r)\phi(r,0)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r,\\
&\lim\limits_{a\searrow 0}\int_{a}^{\infty}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\rho_{a}^{0}\left\lvert u_{a}^{0}\right\rvert^{2}+\rho_{a}^{0}e_{a}^{0}\Big)(r)\phi(r,0)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r=\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\dfrac{1}{2}\rho_{0}\lvert u_{0}\rvert^{2}+\rho_{0}e_{0}\Big)(r)\phi(r,0)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r.\end{split}

Proof.

By construction (
3.6
) in §
3.2
,

(\rho_{a}^{0},u_{a}^{0},e_{a}^{0})(r)=(1,0,1)
for
r\in[0,a]
. Then
we use Proposition
3.1
to obtain

\displaystyle\Big\lvert\int_{a}^{\infty}\rho_{a}^{0}(r)\phi(r,0)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r-\int_{0}^{\infty}\rho_{0}(r)\phi(r,0)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\Big\rvert

\displaystyle\quad\leq\int_{0}^{a}\lvert\phi(r,0)\rvert\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r+\lVert\phi(\cdot,0)\rVert_{L^{2}([0,\infty),r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)}\lVert\rho_{a}^{0}-\rho_{0}\rVert_{L^{2}([0,\infty),r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)}\to 0\qquad\text{ as $a\searrow 0$},

\displaystyle\Big\lvert\int_{a}^{\infty}(\rho_{a}^{0}u_{a}^{0})(r)\phi(r,0)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r-\int_{0}^{\infty}(\rho_{0}u_{0})(r)\phi(r,0)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\Big\rvert

\displaystyle\quad\leq\lVert\rho_{a}^{0}\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lVert\phi(\cdot,0)\rVert_{L^{2}([0,\infty),r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)}\lVert u_{a}^{0}-u_{0}\rVert_{L^{2}([0,\infty),r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\,+\lVert\phi(\cdot,0)\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lVert u_{0}\rVert_{L^{2}([0,\infty),r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)}\lVert\rho_{a}^{0}-\rho_{0}\rVert_{L^{2}([0,\infty),r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)}\to 0\qquad\text{ as $a\searrow 0$}.

Furthermore, it follows from (
3.6
) and Proposition
3.1
that,
as
a\searrow 0
,

\displaystyle\Big\lvert\int_{a}^{\infty}\rho_{a}^{0}\lvert u_{a}^{0}\rvert^{2}(r)\phi(r,0)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r-\int_{0}^{\infty}\rho_{0}\lvert u_{0}\rvert^{2}(r)\phi(r,0)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\Big\rvert

\displaystyle\quad\leq\lVert\rho_{a}^{0}\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lVert\phi(\cdot,t)\rVert_{L^{2}([0,\infty),r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)}\lVert\lvert u_{a}^{0}\rvert^{2}-\lvert u_{0}\rvert^{2}\rVert_{L^{2}([0,\infty),r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\lVert\phi(\cdot,0)\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lVert u_{0}\rVert_{L^{4}([0,\infty),r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)}^{2}\lVert\rho_{a}^{0}-\rho_{0}\rVert_{L^{2}([0,\infty),r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)}\to 0\qquad\text{ as $a\searrow 0$}.

\displaystyle\Big\lvert\int_{a}^{\infty}\rho_{a}^{0}e_{a}^{0}(r)\phi(r,0)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r-\int_{0}^{\infty}\rho_{0}e_{0}(r)\phi(r,0)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\Big\rvert

\displaystyle\quad\leq\Big\lvert\int_{0}^{a}\phi(r,0)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\Big\rvert+\Big\lvert\int_{0}^{\infty}\phi(r,0)\big(\rho_{a}^{0}(e_{a}^{0}-e_{0})+e_{0}(\rho_{a}^{0}-\rho_{0})\big)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\Big\rvert

\displaystyle\quad\leq\lVert\phi(\cdot,0)\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\frac{a^{n}}{n}+\lVert\rho_{a}^{0}\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lVert\phi(\cdot,0)\rVert_{L^{2}([0,\infty),r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)}\lVert e_{a}^{0}-e_{0}\rVert_{L^{2}([0,\infty),r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\,+\big(\lVert\phi(\cdot,0)\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lVert e_{0}-1\rVert_{L^{2}([0,\infty),r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)}+\lVert\phi(\cdot,0)\rVert_{L^{2}([0,\infty),r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)}\big)\lVert\rho_{a}^{0}-\rho_{0}\rVert_{L^{2}([0,\infty),r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r)}\to 0.

This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 6.8
.

For any
\phi\in\mathcal{D}_{T}
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\rho(r,t)\phi(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r-\int_{0}^{\infty}\rho_{0}(r)\phi(r,0)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{\infty}\big(\rho\partial_{t}\phi+\rho u\partial_{r}\phi\big)(r,s)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}s.

Proof.

First, from the continuity equation in Theorem
3.1
,
we obtain that, for all
j\in\mathbb{N}
and
a_{j}\in(0,1)
,

\int_{a_{j}}^{\infty}\rho_{a_{j}}(r,s)\phi(r,s)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\Big|_{s=0}^{s=t}=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{a_{j}}^{\infty}\big(\rho_{a_{j}}\partial_{t}\phi+\rho_{a_{j}}u_{a_{j}}\partial_{r}\phi\big)(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}t.

(6.30)

Since
\rho\equiv 0
in
\{r<\underline{r}(t)\}
by construction, it follows from Lemmas
6.1
and
6.4
that

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1}^{j}\vcentcolon=

\displaystyle\,\Big\lvert\int_{a_{j}}^{\infty}\rho_{a_{j}}(r,t)\phi(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r-\int_{\underline{r}(t)}^{\infty}\rho(r,t)\phi(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\Big\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\,\Big\lvert\int_{\widetilde{r}_{a_{j}}(x,t)}^{\infty}\rho_{a_{j}}(r,t)\phi(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r-\int_{\widetilde{r}(x,t)}^{\infty}\rho(r,t)\phi(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\Big\rvert+\lVert\phi\rVert_{\infty}\int_{\underline{r}(t)}^{\widetilde{r}(x,t)}\rho(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r

\displaystyle+\lVert\phi\rVert_{\infty}\bigg(\int_{a_{j}}^{\underline{r}(t)}+\int_{\underline{r}(t)}^{\widetilde{r}(x,t)}+\int_{\widetilde{r}(x,t)}^{\widetilde{r}_{a_{j}}(x,t)}\bigg)\rho_{a_{j}}(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,\Big\lvert\int_{0}^{\infty}\big(\rho_{a_{j}}^{(x)}-\rho^{(x)}\big)(r,t)\phi(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\Big\rvert+\lVert\phi\rVert_{\infty}x+\lVert\phi\rVert_{\infty}\int_{a_{j}}^{\underline{r}(t)}\rho_{a_{j}}(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r

\displaystyle+\lVert\phi\rVert_{\infty}\omega_{1}([\underline{r}(t),\widetilde{r}(x,t)];C)+\lVert\phi\rVert_{\infty}\omega_{1}([\widetilde{r}(x,t),\widetilde{r}_{a_{j}}(x,t)];C).

Since
\widetilde{r}(x,t)\searrow\underline{r}(t)
as
x\searrow 0
, for any
\delta>0
,
there exists
y\in(0,1)
such that

\lVert\phi\rVert_{\infty}x+\lVert\phi\rVert_{\infty}\omega_{1}([\underline{r}(t),\widetilde{r}(x,t)];C)\leq\delta\qquad\ \text{ for all $0<x<y$.}

Then it follows that, for all
x\in(0,y)
,

\mathcal{I}_{1}^{j}\leq\delta+\Big\lvert\int_{0}^{\infty}\!\!\phi(r,t)\big(\rho_{a_{j}}^{(x)}-\rho^{(x)}\big)(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\Big\rvert+\lVert\phi\rVert_{\infty}\int_{a_{j}}^{\underline{r}(t)}\!\!\rho_{a_{j}}(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r+\lVert\phi\rVert_{\infty}\omega_{1}([\widetilde{r}(x,t),\widetilde{r}_{a_{j}}(x,t)];C).

Fix a point
x\in(0,y)
. Taking the limit
j\to\infty
on the above inequality, then using
Lemmas
6.4
(iii)(i)
and
6.2
(iii)(v)
,
we have

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{j\to\infty}\Big\lvert\int_{a_{j}}^{\infty}\rho_{a_{j}}(r,t)\phi(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r-\int_{\underline{r}(t)}^{\infty}\rho(r,t)\phi(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\Big\rvert\leq\delta,

which implies that
\lim_{j\to\infty}\mathcal{I}_{1}^{j}=0
.

Next, for each
x>0
, we rewrite the term:

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{2}^{j}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\Big\lvert\int_{0}^{t}\int_{a_{j}}^{\infty}\rho_{a_{j}}u_{a_{j}}\partial_{r}\phi(r,s)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}s-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\underline{r}(s)}^{\infty}\rho u\,\partial_{r}\phi(r,s)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}s\Big\rvert

\displaystyle\,\leq\lVert\partial_{r}\phi\rVert_{\infty}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{a_{j}}^{\widetilde{r}_{a_{j}}(x,s)}\lvert\rho_{a_{j}}u_{a_{j}}\rvert\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}s+\lVert\partial_{r}\phi\rVert_{\infty}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\underline{r}(s)}^{\widetilde{r}(x,s)}\lvert\rho u\rvert\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\,\quad+\Big\lvert\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\widetilde{r}_{a_{j}}(x,s)}^{\infty}\rho_{a_{j}}u_{a_{j}}\partial_{r}\phi\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}s-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\widetilde{r}(x,s)}^{\infty}\rho u\partial_{r}\phi\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}s\Big\rvert

\displaystyle=\vcentcolon\mathcal{I}_{2,1}^{j}(x)+\mathcal{I}_{2,2}^{j}(x)+\mathcal{I}_{2,3}^{j}(x).

Using Lemma
6.1
, the Cauchy-Schwartz inequality, and
Theorem
3.1
(iii)(iii)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{2,1}^{j}(x)\leq\lVert\partial_{r}\phi\rVert_{\infty}\sqrt{x}\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\int_{a_{j}}^{\widetilde{r}_{a_{j}}(x,t)}\rho_{a_{j}}u_{a_{j}}^{2}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}{\mathrm{d}}s\leq\lVert\partial_{r}\phi\rVert_{\infty}C(T)t\sqrt{x}.

Moreover, using Lemmas
6.4
(iii)(iii)
and
6.5
leads to

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{2,2}^{j}(x)\leq\lVert\partial_{r}\phi\rVert_{\infty}\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\int_{\underline{r}(s)}^{\widetilde{r}(x,s)}\rho\lvert u\rvert^{2}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\int_{\underline{r}(s)}^{\widetilde{r}(x,s)}\rho\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}{\mathrm{d}}s\leq\lVert\partial_{r}\phi\rVert_{\infty}C(T)t\sqrt{x}.

Thus, for any
\delta>0
, there exists
y_{\delta}\in(0,1)
such that, if
x\in(0,y_{\delta})
,
then
\mathcal{I}_{2}^{j}(x)\leq\delta+\mathcal{I}_{2,3}^{j}(x)
.
By Lemma
6.2
,
x\to\widetilde{r}(x,t)
is increasing,
and
(x,t)\mapsto\widetilde{r}(x,t)
is continuous. Since
[0,T]
is compact,

d:=\inf_{t\in[0,T]}\{\widetilde{r}(x,t)-\widetilde{r}(\frac{x}{2},t)\}>0
.
By Lemma
6.2
(iii)(i)
, there exists
N_{x}\in\mathbb{N}

such that
\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\lvert\widetilde{r}_{a_{j}}(x,t)-\widetilde{r}(x,t)\rvert\leq\frac{d}{2}
if
j\geq N_{x}
.
Combining with the previous assertion, we have

\widetilde{r}(\frac{x}{2},t)<\widetilde{r}_{a_{j}}(x,t)\,\qquad\text{ for each $t\in[0,T]$ and $j\geq N_{x}$.}

(6.31)

Then
\chi_{x}^{a_{j}}=\chi_{\frac{x}{2}}\chi_{x}^{a_{j}}
for all
j\geq N_{x}
,
where
\chi_{\varepsilon}
and
\chi_{\varepsilon}^{a}
are defined in (
6.18
).
Thus, for all
j\geq N_{x}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{2,3}^{j}(x)\leq\Big\lvert\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\widetilde{r}(\frac{x}{2},s)}^{\infty}\rho_{a_{j}}^{(x)}(u_{a_{j}}-u)\partial_{r}\phi\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}s\Big\rvert+\Big\lvert\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\widetilde{r}(\frac{x}{2},s)}^{\infty}\big(\rho_{a_{j}}^{(x)}-\rho^{(x)}\big)u\partial_{r}\phi\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}s\Big\rvert.

By the pointwise bound:
\rho_{a}^{(x)}(r,t)\leq C(x)
from Theorem
3.1
(iii)(iv)
,
and the uniform convergence from Lemma
6.3
, it follows that, as
j\to\infty
,

\displaystyle\Big\lvert\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\widetilde{r}(\frac{x}{2},s)}^{\infty}\rho_{a_{j}}^{(x)}(u_{a_{j}}-u)\partial_{r}\phi\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}s\Big\rvert\leq C(x)\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\widetilde{r}(\frac{x}{2},s)}^{\infty}\lvert u_{a_{j}}-u\rvert\partial_{r}\phi\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}s\to 0.

By Lemma
6.3
,
\chi_{\frac{x}{2}}u\partial_{r}\phi\in L^{1}(0,T;L^{2}([0,\infty),r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r))
.
Thus, by Lemma
6.4
(iii)(i)
,

\displaystyle\Big\lvert\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\widetilde{r}(\frac{x}{2},s)}^{\infty}\big(\rho_{a_{j}}^{(x)}-\rho^{(x)}\big)u\partial_{r}\phi\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}s\Big\rvert\to 0\qquad\ \text{as $j\to\infty$},

which implies that
\lim_{j\to\infty}\mathcal{I}_{2,3}^{j}(x)=0
for each
x>0
.
Now, for any given
\delta>0
, we fix
x\in(0,y_{\delta})
. Then
\mathcal{I}_{2}^{j}=\mathcal{I}_{2,1}^{j}(x)+\mathcal{I}_{2,2}^{j}(x)+\mathcal{I}_{2,3}^{j}(x)\leq\delta+\mathcal{I}_{2,3}^{j}(x)
.
Taking limit
j\to\infty
on both sides of this,
we see that
\lim_{j\to\infty}\mathcal{I}_{2}^{j}\leq\delta
.
Since
\delta>0
is arbitrarily small, we conclude that
\lim_{j\to\infty}\mathcal{I}_{2}^{j}=0
.
By the
same argument, we can also show that

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{3}^{j}\vcentcolon=\Big\lvert\int_{0}^{t}\int_{a_{j}}^{\infty}\rho_{a_{j}}\partial_{t}\phi\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}s-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\underline{r}(s)}^{\infty}\rho\partial_{t}\phi\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}s\Big\rvert\to 0\qquad\ \text{ as $j\to\infty$.}

Lemma
6.8
is proved by substituting these limits in (
6.30
)
and using Lemma
6.7
.
∎

Lemma 6.9
.

Let
\phi\in\mathcal{D}_{T}\cap C^{1}
be such that
{\mathrm{supp}}(\phi)\Subset\mathrm{F}
.
Then

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}(\rho u)(r,t)\varphi(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r-\int_{0}^{\infty}\rho_{0}(r)u_{0}(r)\varphi(r,0)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big\{\rho u\partial_{t}\varphi+\rho u^{2}\partial_{r}\varphi+\big(P-\beta(\partial_{r}u+m\dfrac{u}{r})\big)\big(\partial_{r}\varphi+m\dfrac{\varphi}{r}\big)\Big\}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}s,

(6.32)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}(\rho E)(r,t)\phi(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r-\int_{0}^{\infty}(\rho_{0}E_{0})(r)\phi(r,0)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{\infty}\rho E\partial_{t}\phi\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big\{(\rho E+P)u-2\mu u\partial_{r}u-\lambda u\big(\partial_{r}u+m\dfrac{u}{r}\big)-\kappa\partial_{r}e\Big\}\partial_{r}\phi\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}s.

(6.33)

Proof.

We give the proof only for (
6.33
), since (
6.32
) can be derived in the same way.

First, from the energy equation in Theorem
3.1
, we have the weak form:

\displaystyle\int_{a_{j}}^{\infty}(\rho_{a_{j}}E_{a_{j}})(r,t)\phi(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r-\int_{a_{j}}^{\infty}\Big(\dfrac{1}{2}\rho_{a_{j}}^{0}\lvert u_{a_{j}}^{0}\rvert^{2}+\rho_{a_{j}}^{0}e_{a_{j}}^{0}\Big)(r)\phi(r,0)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r

(6.34)

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{a_{j}}^{\infty}\Big\{2\mu u_{a_{j}}\partial_{r}u_{a_{j}}+\lambda u_{a_{j}}\big(\partial_{r}u_{a_{j}}+m\dfrac{u_{a_{j}}}{r}\big)+\kappa\partial_{r}e_{a_{j}}\Big\}\partial_{r}\phi\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{a_{j}}^{\infty}\big\{\rho_{a_{j}}E_{a_{j}}\partial_{t}\phi+(\rho_{a_{j}}E_{a_{j}}+P_{a_{j}})u_{a_{j}}\partial_{r}\phi\big\}\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r{\mathrm{d}}s,

where
P_{a_{j}}\vcentcolon=(\gamma-1)\rho_{a_{j}}e_{a_{j}}
and

E_{a_{j}}\vcentcolon=\frac{1}{2}\lvert u_{a_{j}}\rvert^{2}+e_{a_{j}}
.
Now, for each
x\in(0,1)
, we define

~

F

_x:= { (r,t) :  t∈[0,T],
~

r

(x,t) ¡ r ¡x^-1 }.

Then
\widetilde{\mathrm{F}}_{x}\cap\{0<t<T\}
is open for each
x\in(0,1)
,
since
(x,t)\mapsto\widetilde{r}(x,t)
is continuous.
Moreover, since
x\mapsto\widetilde{r}(x,t)
is strictly increasing for each
t\in[0,T]
,
it follows that
\widetilde{\mathrm{F}}_{x}\subsetneq\widetilde{\mathrm{F}}_{y}
if
y<x\in(0,1)
.
Using

\widetilde{r}(x,t)\searrow\underline{r}(t)
as
x\searrow 0
for all
t\in[0,T]
,
we see that
\mathrm{F}=\cup_{x\in(0,1)}\widetilde{\mathrm{F}}_{x}
, so that
\big\{\widetilde{\mathrm{F}}_{x}\cap\{0<t<T\}\big\}_{x\in(0,1)}
is an open
covering of
\mathrm{F}\cap\{0<t<T\}
.
Since
{\mathrm{supp}}(\phi)\Subset\mathrm{F}
is a compact subset,
there exists
\varepsilon>0
such that
{\mathrm{supp}}(\phi)\subset\widetilde{\mathrm{F}}_{2\varepsilon}
.
Defining
d:=\inf_{t\in[0,T]}\widetilde{r}(2\varepsilon,t)
, then
d>0

due to Lemma
6.2
(iii)(iv)
,
and there exists
N_{1}\in\mathbb{N}
such that
a_{j}<d
for
j\geq N_{1}
.
This implies that
a_{j}<\widetilde{r}(2\varepsilon,t)
if
t\in[0,T]
and
j\geq N_{1}
.
In addition, by the same argument for (
6.31
) as in the proof of Lemma
6.8
,
there exists
N_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\widetilde{r}_{a_{j}}(\varepsilon,t)<\widetilde{r}(2\varepsilon,t)
if
j\geq N_{2}

and
t\in[0,T]
.
Let
N\vcentcolon=\max\{N_{1},N_{2}\}
. Then, by Lemmas
6.3
–
6.4

and the dominated convergence theorem, we have

\displaystyle\Big\lvert\int_{a_{j}}^{\infty}(\rho_{a_{j}}E_{a_{j}})(r,t)\phi(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r-\int_{\underline{r}(t)}^{\infty}(\rho E)(r,t)\phi(r,t)\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\Big\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\widetilde{r}(2\varepsilon,t)}^{\infty}\rho_{a_{j}}^{(\varepsilon)}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\lvert u_{a_{j}}^{2}-u^{2}\rvert+\lvert e_{a_{j}}-e\rvert\Big)\phi\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r+\Big\lvert\int_{\widetilde{r}(2\varepsilon,t)}^{\infty}\big(\rho_{a_{j}}^{(\varepsilon)}-\rho^{(\varepsilon)}\big)\big(\dfrac{1}{2}u^{2}+e\big)\phi\,r^{m}{\mathrm{d}}r\Big\rvert
